# Concerning
## the Division of Verses in the Old Testament
### according to the two Testaments.

In order to deprive mockers of the opportunity to ridicule the explanation of the history of Abraham on account of this division of verses, and so that one may have some foundational understanding of it in this book, I wish to place this brief report on the matter after the preface and before the history of Abraham.

Christ says to Peter: **You are Peter, that is, you are a rock, and upon this rock I will build my congregation, so that the gates of hell shall not prevail against it.** <note>Matthew 16:18. The author emphasizes the linguistic connection between the name Peter and the Greek word for rock, 'petra'.</note> God’s Word itself is the rock upon which God has hitherto outwardly built His church; and the Old Testament, in its true doctrine, is primarily the rock upon which the now-coming universal church shall be built. Within this rock—in the Hebrew Old Testament—the entire church of all nations has therefore been engraved and marked out into three dispensations <note>The author uses 'Haushaltungen' to refer to distinct eras of divine administration.</note>, namely: Theology <note>original: "Götterlehre"</note>, the Old Testament, and the New Testament. Likewise, it is divided into four main religions: Heathens, Jews, Christians, and Turks <note>In this historical context, 'Turks' was a common term for Muslims.</note>. This is recorded specifically in the verses and in the parts of the verses into which a verse is further divided by the <term>Teamim</term> <note>Hebrew: 'Ta'amim', the cantillation marks or accents used in the Hebrew Bible to indicate musical rhythm and grammatical structure.</note> or accents. In this inscription, one can read in a figurative manner how our God—this heavenly Aaron—carried the whole Israel of all nations engraved upon the breastplate of His heart (that is, in His Word) and thought of them all. These figurative matters concerning the accents or <term>Teamim</term> should also serve as an indication and conviction to us that the entire Word of God is figurative, and should by no means be understood according to the literal letter, except in matters pertaining to virtue and godliness which are necessary for salvation. These latter things are contained quite clearly in the Word of God without any figure; but everything else is figurative and pertains to the mysteries.

<summary>The author argues that the Hebrew cantillation marks (Teamim) contain a hidden, figurative blueprint of world history and the four major world religions. He asserts that while moral laws are literal, the rest of the Bible must be interpreted as a series of divine mysteries.</summary>
<keywords>biblical interpretation, Hebrew accents, Teamim, Abraham, Peter, dispensations, four religions, figurative theology, mysticism</keywords>

--- Page 16 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's theological exploration of duality, likely following an introduction to the divine structure of history and scripture.</meta>

In the end, the entire Church of all nations shall be enclosed under the two Testaments: For just as God divided the nations through the two Testaments in the external doctrine of the letter; so also does He will, in the end, to gather them together into one through the true sense of the same in the Revelation of Jesus Christ. The division of the Word of God, therefore, rests primarily upon the <term>duality</term><note>original: "Zweyzahl"; refers to the concept of things existing in pairs or binaries</note>, and this division rests upon the foundation from which all things were created in duality and through duality, as through man and woman. Even so has God formed the Church through two Testaments, and in themselves there are only two Testaments, no more and no less. Nor can anyone deny that God has governed the Christian Church through the New Testament, and the Turks <note>In this 17th/18th-century context, "Turks" was often used as a general term for the Islamic world</note> through the Old Testament. These two peoples have attained the supreme sovereignty on earth through the two Testaments; for the Jews have no sovereignty at all, and the heathens have little, and so it is manifest that God has governed these two principal peoples through the two principal parts of His Word, namely through the two Testaments.

Nothing is without ground and cause, just as it is. It is not without cause that the world is divided into the upper and lower, into heaven and earth, into earth and sea, and time into summer and winter, into day and night, since a duality is everywhere. It is not without cause that the human race had to be created through a duality in man and woman: Likewise, that man, as the highest creature and God's image and likeness, also had to be so specially marked with duality in 2 eyes, 2 ears, 2 nostrils, 2 hands, and 2 feet. However, the causes of all these things lie in the essence of God Himself, and He Himself makes everything, even if He makes much on earth through men, for He makes everything, Isaiah 45. But one must divide God, man, Church, and doctrine into the internal and external, into an external temporal nature of the world, and into an eternal nature of the world; only then has He made it all, but otherwise not. Under the name "God," the external nature is also understood, for all external beings are subject to the external nature of the world as to a god, without which they can neither come to be nor subsist. But that is not the eternal God, just as the external man is not the eternal man. However, just as both, the external and internal man, are called by the single name "man,"

He emphasizes that God's essence is reflected in the binary nature of the physical body and the division between temporal and eternal realities.</summary>

<keywords>duality, Two Testaments, sovereignty, macrocosm and microcosm, internal vs external, Isaiah 45, divine image, Church history</keywords>

III
)( 2

[themselves] <note>original: "sich"; catchword from previous page</note> become, so too is the outer nature of the world and the eternal nature of the world called by the single name "God," and yet for that reason one is not the same as the other. This is only mentioned in passing; in the *Book of the World* one will find these things developed further.

The entire world, with all its celestial bodies, is like a single body with many members, and yet it is primarily divided into a duality—the Upper and the Lower—to which the great heavenly lights, the Sun and the Moon, also belong. In the same way, the Church of all nations is viewed as one body with many members, yet it is primarily enclosed within a duality under two Testaments. These are the two arms through which God has made everything in the Church, and continues to make even now. God looks upon this duality of the two Testaments in the Church; therefore, He has also figuratively placed this same duality into the division of the verses, which are alternately divided between the two Testaments. That the division of the verses signifies something is evident from the fact that the <term>Masora</term> <note>The Masora refers to the traditional system of notes on the Hebrew text of the Bible used to ensure its exact preservation.</note> at the end of every biblical book notes the number of verses of the entire book so precisely, so that nothing may be changed or displaced. Just as the Old Testament <note>original: "A. T." (Altes Testament)</note> precedes and the New Testament <note>original: "N. T." (Neues Testament)</note> follows, one should begin with the First Book of Moses <note>The Book of Genesis.</note> and assign the first verse to the Old Testament, and the second to the New Testament, and further the third again to the Old Testament, and so on alternately, from the beginning of the Old Testament to the end of the Old Testament, without looking further at the beginning of a new chapter or a new book; one simply assigns that beginning to whichever Testament falls there according to the aforementioned order. This method is also indicated by the fact that the Masora notes the sum of the verses of an entire book at the end of each book, without regarding the new chapters occurring within the sum of the verses. If one wishes to divide the entire Old Testament among the Testaments in such a manner, one need only mark the first verse of every chapter with the initial letter O. or N. of a Testament; thus all odd verses of the chapter share the same significance of one Testament as the first, and the even verses fall to the other Testament. However, one must take the sequence of the books exactly as they stand in the Hebrew, for the teachers of the Christian Church have dealt wickedly with the books of the Word of God, and have not left them in the order in which God wrote them. This order has in itself—

<summary>The author argues that the structure of the Bible, specifically the alternating verses, reflects a divine duality between the Old and New Testaments, mirroring the duality found in nature. He criticizes Christian scholars for altering the original Hebrew order of the biblical books.</summary>
<keywords>Masora, biblical structure, Old Testament, New Testament, Hebrew Bible, divine order, duality, celestial bodies</keywords>

contains within itself a special teaching. 3. He <note>likely referring to the divine author or the biblical compiler</note> shows that the five books of Moses, along with the Patriarchs, represent the first age of the world, when there were as yet no formal governments, but rather every father of a house was lord in his own home and depended on no one else. The time of the Judges indicates the period when governments began to form on a small scale, as various families and, in turn, various villages entered into a form of governance together. From these governments grew the great city-states, such as Athens, Sparta, and the like. These fought with one another for a long time until it eventually led to princely and royal government; and that only occurred in the times to which the Kings of Judah and Israel are assigned. All kings who are placed before this time are either images of wisdom, or, if they are recorded by secular writers <note>original: "Scribenten"</note>, they are lies with which they merely wished to exaggerate the great age and dominion of their nation. The figures of the Scripture indicate such things in their successive order, and yet the figures themselves still contain their other interpretations within them. The Hebrew order of the Old Testament <note>original: "A. T." (Altes Testament)</note>, however, is this: the five books of Moses, Joshua, Judges, Samuel, and Kings follow one another. The five books of Moses are called the Law <note>Torah</note>. Joshua, Judges, Samuel, and Kings are called the four Former Prophets. These are followed by the four Latter Prophets, namely Isaiah, Jeremiah, Ezekiel, and the twelve Minor Prophets, which are taken as one. Thus far extend the Law and the Prophets, and now follow the Writings <note>Ketuvim</note> in the following order: the Psalms, the Proverbs of Solomon, Job, the Song of Solomon, Ruth, the Lamentations of Jeremiah, Ecclesiastes <note>original: "der Prediger Salomonis"</note>, Esther, the prophet Daniel, Ezra, Nehemiah, and the Chronicles. This is the order of the books of the Old Testament, according to which one can make the aforementioned division of the verses.

---

Furthermore, a verse is again divided into two main parts by the <term>Taam</term> <note>a Hebrew cantillation mark or accent</note> or the accent <term>Atnach</term> <unclear>֑</unclear>. Reflect on this, you scholars: why is there not a single verse in the entire Old Testament that has more than one single Atnach, which thus divides the verse into two parts? Do you not see that by this, a duality is intended, which divides the verse into two parts—a duality which then points again to other things? You can also perceive this duality from...

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>The previous page concluded with a discussion on the structure of the biblical text; this page continues by explaining the mystical and structural significance of the Masoretic divisions.</meta>

that the sum of all verses in a book is divided into two parts by the Masora <note>The Masora refers to the tradition of notes and rules governing the precise transmission of the Hebrew Bible</note> by indicating the middle verse. Thus, if the first part of a verse refers to the Old Testament, the other refers to the New Testament, and vice versa. Through these divisions, it is often wonderfully signified where a matter actually belongs—under which people or Testament, or toward what object it is directed—because various things are indicated through the two Testaments. Furthermore, the *Taam* <term>Taam: a Hebrew term for the musical accents or cantillation marks used in scripture</term> or accent *Sakef Katon* <term>זָקֵף קָטָן: a "small upright" accent that serves as a minor divider in a verse</term> creates special small divisions both before and after the *Atnach* <term>Atnach: the principal divider within a Hebrew verse, splitting it into two logical halves</term>. It creates two, three, or up to four parts, but never more; for the numbers of the Church, toward which these parts are simultaneously aimed, exist only under two Testaments, or three dispensations, and in four main religions. Through these parts, specific things are again indicated, such as when a matter is distinct—internally and externally—or common among many. This is, therefore, a special characteristic script or way of reading which further explains and confirms the content of the things in the text. But whoever does not yet wish to pay attention to this may pass it by and keep only to the text, as I reminded in the preface. However, I shall prove to you scholars that the Masora, along with all such things, including the Talmud, Targum, and other such books, comes from God Himself and from no Rabbis. Here I give only a small indication of these things, so that you do not call these matters "fanatical whims," as is your habit.

Furthermore, the two Testaments are indicated through the persons of the Scripture. Paul testifies to this when he says that Hagar is Mount Sinai with the Law, namely the Old Testament, and Sarah is Jerusalem, as the New Testament (Galatians 4:21-24). Furthermore, the Old is Esau, the New is Jacob; the Old is Moab, the New is Ammon; the Old is Moses, the New is Joshua and also Christ; the Old is the Father, the New is the Son; the Old is Abraham, the New is Lot and Sodom; the Old is Joseph, the New is Benjamin; the Old is Saul, the New is David; the Old is Leah, the New is Rachel; the Old is Peter and Paul, the New is James; the Old is the husband, the New is the wife; the Old is the soul, the New is the body; the Old is the worldly government, the New is the spiritual government.

# Page VI

The Old Testament <note>Original: "A.", abbreviation for "Altes Testament"</note> is the right hand, the New Testament <note>Original: "N.", abbreviation for "Neues Testament"</note> is the left hand: the Old is the sea, the New is the earth: The Old is the heavens, the New is the earth. All these interpretations are grounded in nature itself, for just as the right hand possesses the most strength and is the primary hand in labor; so too is the greatest strength of the doctrine of wisdom found in the Old Testament, wherein the circumstances of the creation of the world and all creatures are contained. This is why the O. C. <note>Likely "Origo Creaturarum" or "Origin of Creatures"</note>, as the revelation of the beginning of God's creation, had to come out of the Old Testament. Furthermore, just as there is more seawater than dry land; so the Old Testament, even taken externally, has ruled the greatest part of the earth through the Turkish Church <note>A historical reference to the Ottoman Empire, which the author associates with the Old Testament's legalistic or external character</note>. It is the same with all other equations or comparisons, whereby one always looks to the greater or smaller, higher or lower, more or less, stronger or weaker, spiritual or physical, older or younger, preceding or following. Thus, with the right eye, hand, foot, thumb, toe, earlobe, and all such things called "Right," one looks toward the Old Testament and the things it signifies. For what else would such things serve in the Scripture, and how would God be served by the right thumb, toe, or earlobe, of which so much is written in the Books of Moses? Or does one think there is nothing at all contained beneath such things? The Old Testament is the blood, the New is the water; the Old is the morning and noon, the New is the evening and midnight. The Old is day and summer, the New is night and winter. The Old is the sun and gold, the New is the moon and silver. The Old is Mars and iron, the New is Venus and copper. The Old is stone, the New is wood. The Old is oxen and cattle, the New is sheep. The Old is wheat, the New is barley and linen. The Old is Egypt, the New is Canaan and Jerusalem. The Old is Israel, the New is Judah.

The Old Testament stands as the image of everything external, and the New as the image of the internal because of the name of Jesus. But externally, it is only the image of the internal, and not the thing itself. When the internal is taken essentially; then it is found in the doctrine of the Old Testament. In this sense, the true Jesus Christ is from the true doctrine of the Old Testament, and the New is itself included among all that is external. Also

# VII

the outer belongs to the time past, and the inner belongs to the time to come.

Everything now depends on the comparison of the one against the other, for the Scripture teaches throughout with parables <note>original: "Gleichnissen" – can also mean allegories or likenesses</note>. If one pays attention to this, one can soon see from the content of the matter whether it is intended for the inner or the outer, for this time, or for the time to come. I only wanted to cite these few points regarding these things so that sensible and honest men among the scholars can see that there is a basis for this division of the verses. Now, whoever reads the history of Abraham and notes these things therein will often find wonderful proofs; namely, in those places where such parts are specifically taught. This does not necessarily have to be the case every single time, just as with the letter ה or h <note>The Hebrew letter 'He', added by God to change Abram's name to Abraham</note>, which was added to the name of Abraham, it does not always teach that which it indicated during the transformation of the name Abram into Abraham.

In general, however, one must read these things slowly and with good reflection in order to reach the meaning; for one cannot run through them quickly as in the <term>Belles Lettres</term> <note>French: "Fine Letters" – referring to light or polite literature and humanities</note> of the scholars, where one can already guess from the beginning of a speech what a person intends to say, because one is already accustomed to such things. But of these matters, one knows nothing yet, and therefore one must read slowly and with great deliberation until the heart and mind become accustomed to the figurative representations. Especially in the history of Abraham, one should see to it that one finds and feels the meaning of things within oneself, for which enough guidance is given here and there in the explanation of the figures. But from this point on, do not read Abraham immediately, but first the second part of the book—namely the Blessing of Jacob with the 12 tribes until the end—then one will understand Abraham better and read with greater benefit.

Whoever reads through this book a few times with deliberation and good reflection will see what a key he will obtain for the understanding of the Word of God. May God let it be a blessing to everyone who reads it, for God knows that

# Abraham

### Section 3 <note>original: "Parascha"</note> Go forth. <note>original Hebrew: "Lech Lecha" (לך לך)</note> פפפ <note>The Hebrew letter 'Pe' repeated three times often indicates a paragraph break in the Torah.</note>

The departure of Abraham, or of Jesus Christ, both out of the beginning of the world into the current time of corruption—in order to multiply therein with the human race—as well as the departure out of this time back into the original state of blessing of the world. The <term>Books of Moses<note>The Pentateuch or Torah</note></term> are divided into <term>Parashot<note>Weekly portions or sections of the Torah read in the Jewish liturgy</note></term>, divisions, or expositions. In each, things are presented and considered in a unique and special manner. The first:

The first <term>Parashah<note>A weekly portion of the Torah read in Jewish liturgy.</note></term> goes from the beginning until Chapter 6, verse 9, and is called <term>Bereshit<note>Hebrew: "In the beginning."</note></term>, *In the Beginning*. It presents the beginning of all things, and because the final time of blessing with the Kingdom of Christ also has its beginning, this beginning is also described therein. The second Parashah or division, which begins from Chapter 6, verse 9 and continues to this point, is called: <term>Noah<note>Hebrew: "Rest" or "Comfort."</note></term>, that is, Beginning and End, just as Jesus Christ is called the O and A <note>The Alpha and Omega; the first and last letters of the Greek alphabet.</note>, the Beginning and the End. With this Parashah, it is presented how one must take all things in the Word of God, in the beginning and the end, just as Noah is presented as if he had crossed over from the end of one world and time into the beginning of the other. Here now begins the third Parashah with the departure of Abraham. Abraham is Jesus Christ, the blessing of all nations, and Jesus Christ is the first Adam at the beginning of the world. Adam is also the nature of the world itself. The entire human race is also Adam, and the Church and doctrine is Adam. Therefore, Abraham is taken both inwardly and outwardly, as is Christ. His departure is the departure of all that exists from one time into another, and from one Church and doctrine into another. In the beginning of the world, the departure was from the good time into the evil, and at the end, it is the departure from the evil time into the good. It says: *Go forth for yourself.* Jesus or Adam at the beginning of the world went, together with nature on earth, for his own sake, out of the state of blessing into the current state of corruption, in order to nourish himself therein and to have seed; and at the end, he also goes back with all of that into the beginning, in order to lead his seed into the promised land of blessing. Of all this, we now wish to see further from the Word of God itself.

# The 12th Chapter of the 1st Book of Moses <note>Genesis</note>.

## Abraham's Departure from his Fatherland.

1. Verse. And Jehovah spoke to Abraham: go forth for yourself out of your fatherland and from your kindred

> [Go out from your] kinship, and from your father's house; into a land that I will show you.
> 2. A. And I will make you into a great nation; and I will bless you; and I will make your name great; and you shall be a blessing.

Abraham means a "high father," and he is the heavenly Adam, Jesus Christ. <note>The author uses a mystical interpretation where biblical figures like Abraham and Adam are seen as prototypes or "types" of Christ.</note> This one is the blessing of all nations, and also the high and first forefather of all peoples. In the beginning of the world, he went out from his heavenly fatherland into this temporal time for the multiplication of the human race. This multiplication has now nearly reached its end, and he has become a great nation. The eternal essence of God entered into the essence of this time, and formed His eternal essence into an infinite multitude of human children, who had to be brought forth from one another in a continuous line of generation. However, the eternal essence of God was a stranger in the essence of outer nature; the inner man was a stranger in the outer, and Jesus Christ—who is both Adam and Abraham—was unknown to all people of the earth until this very day.

But now, as the multiplication of his people (the natural multiplication of humans) nears its end, he has revealed himself. Until now, the first Adam has borne a contemptible name. All his children, together with the whole of nature on earth—all of which is also Adam—were cast down into the contemptible state of corruption. <note>original: "Verderben," referring to the fallen state of humanity and nature.</note> But now Adam shall have a great name. He shall no longer be called Adam, but Jesus Christ, the high father Abraham. A corrupted nature on earth, together with a corrupted doctrine of the church, was previously his father's house, his fatherland, and his kinship; for the eternal essence was within the temporal essence and was surrounded by corruption, misery, and death. But now it is said: "Go out from your fatherland and from your kinship, and from your father's house, into a land that I will show you"—into the true doctrine and into the original paradise of the earth, which shall now be shown to you.

The beginning of all these things is, in the end, Elias <note>The author identifies Elias (Elijah) as a prophetic figure appearing at the end of time</note>, to whom God has given the revelation of Jesus Christ. This man is the personal Abraham on earth at the end of days, for Abraham stands as a figure <note>original: "Figur" - a symbolic representation or type</note> of Jesus Christ. Jesus Christ, as the personal God, is not personally on earth, but Abraham is personally here; he stands in His figure and must, as it were, represent His personal place. This is because He has placed His revealed name into his creaturely being—that high name which has been kept hidden from every human creature from the beginning of the world until today. 

This personal Abraham at the end also had to go out from his father’s house, out of the external Christian church and teaching. He had to enter into a land, or into a teaching, which God has shown him and still shows him: namely, into the true inner teaching of the Old Testament. In this land or teaching, God likewise made him a great name, because he must reveal the great name of Jesus Christ—the personal God-Adam—before all nations. God will bless him, and he shall be a blessing before all the peoples of the earth, for he is the Messiah at the end in person. Blessed shall be those who bless him with this teaching, and cursed shall be those who curse him with this teaching: for this teaching is from the Holy Spirit of the holy and eternal God. It is the land that God showed to Abraham. In this teaching, we also see the now-coming land of blessing and the paradise of the earth at the end of this time. Therefore, let no one curse Abraham, which is the teaching in the Revelation of Christ; for that is the sin against the Holy Spirit, which has no forgiveness.

Teaching, which God now shows to them. Faithful Christians must leave behind their outward Christ and his church, as if leaving their father’s house. The believers of past times always had to practice this departure of Abraham in their own specific way. They had to depart from evil inclinations and desires and crucify their flesh along with its lusts and cravings. They had to turn from the outward to the inward; only then were they blessed and became a blessing to others in turn. Whoever blessed them, and accepted and loved their good teachings, was thereby blessed again in his inner being. Whoever cursed them was also cursed by God in return: for that was also the sin against the Holy Spirit, which has no forgiveness. All teachings that focus on the interior, and flow from the inward divine life and aim back toward it, and point people in that direction, are from the Holy Spirit; and whoever hostilely opposes them inevitably draws a curse and damnation upon himself. For Abraham, in this sense, is the inner and eternal essence, both in God and in man. Therein lies the blessing of all nations, and whoever curses that is <note>original: "eo ipso"</note> by that very fact already cursed and damned: for the curse is indeed within him and not the blessing.

Abraham is therefore many things, and must be understood in various senses and meanings, as we shall see further in the following. This twofold tree has formed itself with its boughs, branches, blossoms, flowers, and fruits, in exactly the same way and manner as all trees and fruits are formed. The tree has grown upward from branch to branch, until reaching its full maturity of time. It has brought forth its blossoms, flowers, and fruits, both externally in the kingdom of nature, and internally in the kingdom of grace, or in the kingdom of Christ. External religion and worldly empires were the flowers and fruits in the kingdom of nature. The believers with the inner life from God, however, were the lineage of Christ, the spiritual tree, which grew along with and within the tree of nature, and at the end emerges with its beautiful blossoms, flowers, and fruits in such a way that it covers the natural tree so completely that one can no longer see it for the multitude of beautiful blossoms and flowers which are driven forth from the spirit of eternity; thus, time is entirely swallowed up in eternity, and the natural tree in the spiritual tree of Jesus Christ.

Only here is Christ revealed in the flesh, as is depicted with the external Christ as the external figure <note>The author distinguishes between the historical, physical appearance of Christ and the internal, spiritual realization of Christ within the believer</note>. Oh, who would not gladly endure! Is it not characteristic of all nature that it desires its continuation and preservation, and not its destruction and its end? Now, you high father Abraham, Jesus Christ, you eternal God! You have gone forth so that we, your children, might become what we are in our created essence. Let us, as your seed, inherit your kingdom, which is eternal, as many of us as have trusted in your word and also your promise!

Because through Abraham we look toward Jesus Christ, and Christ is also manifest outwardly, our gaze is primarily fixed upon the Christian church. things served. This land of the church under the name of Jesus, God intends to give to the seed of Abraham. The seed that was to come from him—namely, from the Christian church—was to inherit the true land of promise, but not this Abraham here himself. He possessed only the outward land in the outward doctrine, and the Canaanites—the literalist Christians <note>The author uses "literalist Christians" (Buchstaben-Christen) to describe those who follow the "dead letter" of the law rather than the spirit.</note>—were masters in the land, while he himself was a stranger within it, and his seed, which he had here, had to be in servitude.

Just as the Spirit passed through into the third dispensation <term>dispensation: a period of divine administration or "household" management of the church</term> of the church and only then entered into the true and great servitude with the believers; so also did it pass through three main ages and states with the condition of nature on earth, until the time of corruption. The first age was Paradise on earth. The second was the intermediate state, and the third is the current time of corruption, in which the seed of faith of Abraham was in a foreign land throughout all three dispensations. Of this state of being a stranger, the Jews are the outward figure in the world. They and their church are a true wonder of God in outward nature, in that they have neither land nor a worldly government. The Canaanite-like, abominable people among the Christians believe that this is a special curse because the Jews did not accept the outward Christ. However, just as the outward Christ with his church is only the outward figure of the blessing, and not the blessing itself; so also are the Jews with their church only the outward figure of the curse, and not the curse itself. The curse passes over all peoples, and most of all over the Christian church, and the blessing also passes over all peoples, and yet also most of all over the believers of the Christian church: for from it comes Jesus Christ in his revelation with the blessing over all peoples.

### 8. A. And he departed from there to the mountain toward the east of Bethel, and pitched his tent: Bethel toward the west, and Ai toward the east: and there he built an altar to Jehovah: and called upon the name of Jehovah.

### 9. N. And Abraham journeyed: going and journeying toward the south.

He argues that outward religious forms are merely figures of a deeper spiritual reality of blessing and curse.</summary>

The Christian church was followed by the Turkish <note>The author uses "Turkish church" to refer to Islam, common in 18th-century German theological writing</note> church, in which Abraham likewise walked with the two Testaments. The Old Testament, which holds dominion among the Turks, is also the Morning; therefore, he moved toward the mountain in the East <note>original: "gegen Morgen" - literally "toward morning," a traditional term for the East</note>. Bethel means a House of God, and is primarily assigned to the Old Testament. Ai means a "heap" <note>referring to the Hebrew meaning of Ai as a ruin or heap</note>; and with these two places, the two Testaments are indicated. In view of the chronological succession, the Christian church is the Morning in the dispensation of the New Testament, and the Turkish church, as the last, is the Evening. But in view of the succession of the two Testaments themselves, the Christian church with the New Testament is the Evening, and the Turkish church with the Old Testament is the Midday <note>The South</note>; and so Abraham moved here toward the East, and yet also toward the South: for the Old Testament is designated by Morning and Midday, just as the New is by Evening and Midnight. In these regions of the world, the two Testaments also dwell with their churches in the New Testament dispensation, as is evident to the eyes of all the world with Christians and Turks.

From these figures, it can be seen how Abraham entered into all nations and wandered through the whole earth, and how, for that reason, no other people is excluded from God's covenant with Abraham. How could it be otherwise? For the essence of God is everywhere in all of nature on earth. God himself forms all human beings, and he makes them out of his own essence, and plants his eternal essence within the outer man. He dwells within all things internally, and his written Word has gone out among all nations; how then should Abraham not be among all nations? Through the Word of God, all religions and world empires have been made. Externally, the Kingdom of Nature has produced the outward wonders with the governments and religions. No religion could have progress or preservation except through the Word of God, upon which everything had to be grounded. God brought the nations to outward faith through his Word, through the wonders which were depicted through the persons of the Scripture as having already actually happened; and so God showed himself externally through his Word in power and wonders, and also brought carnal men to faith in his Word. But beneath this outer Kingdom of Nature, the inner Kingdom of Grace—as the Kingdom of Christ—was also always being formed secretly within, and together with the believers, it constituted the inner church within the outer, just as the inner man lies within the outer, and

Here we have Abraham in the "Turkish Church" <note>The author likely uses "Turkish Church" as a contemporary 18th-century metaphor for a state of spiritual exteriority or non-Christian legalism</note> under the Old Testament. This is now placed back into the beginning, as into the administration <term>Haushaltung: a theological term for 'dispensation' or how God manages the world in different eras</term> of the Old Testament before the Christian Church, from which the transition into the administration of the New Testament is described with a new figure, as follows.

### 10. Old Testament: And there was a famine in the land:
<note>The author uses "A." and "N." to alternate between Old and New Testament perspectives or "Abba" and "Noachian" divisions</note> and Abraham went down into Egypt, to dwell there as a stranger: for the famine in the land was heavy.
### 11. New Testament: And it came to pass, when he came near to enter into Egypt:
he spoke to Sarai his wife: behold now, I know: that you are a beautiful woman to look upon.
### 12. Old Testament: And it shall come to pass, when the Egyptians see you: they shall say, this is his wife:
and they will kill me, and let you live.
### 13. New Testament: Therefore say, I pray you, that you are my sister:
so that it may go well with me for your sake: and my soul shall live because of you.

The famine in the land indicates the lack of the inner true doctrine in the administration of the Old Testament, which is why Abraham entered into the administration of the New Testament under the name of Jesus, in order to dwell therein as a stranger, for the Spirit did not wish to remain in the external doctrine of the letter <term>Buchstabenlehre: literalist or legalistic interpretation of scripture</term>. He went always toward the end; therefore God broke one external thing through another, and revealed Himself here in the New Testament with the name of Jesus, because through Jesus Christ the redemption from all externals was to happen. This Jesus was only the external figure at first, and the true Abraham dwelt as a stranger in the land.

<note>original: "מצרים"</note> Egypt stands in the dual number <note>In Hebrew, the name for Egypt, Mizraim, has a dual suffix (-aim), implying two Egypts</note>, and indicates a twofold Egypt. In the Church, these are the two Testaments in the doctrine of the letter.

<meta>This page continues the allegorical interpretation of Abraham’s journey, specifically focusing on the symbolic meaning of his descent into Egypt as it relates to the relationship between the Old and New Testaments.</meta>

It is primarily the Christian Church wherein both Testaments are found together. Egypt signifies a land of anguish, a land of sorrow. The "drawing down" or "descending" into Egypt indicates the transition into a lowly, earthly doctrine of the letter <note>The "letter" refers to a literal or superficial understanding of scripture versus a spiritual one</note>; and so this figure also testifies that the doctrine of the Christian Church is more earthly than the doctrine of the Old Testament.

In the realm of Nature, Abraham’s descent into Egypt represents the transition from the initial middle-state into the current course of corruption, wherein Abraham has dwelt as a stranger—both in nature upon the earth and in the teachings of all three dispensations <term>Haushaltungen: theological eras or "economies" of God's dealing with humanity</term> of the Church—and where the hunger or lack of the Eternal and Interior was heavy upon the exterior.

The Old entered into the Church of the New Testament, and both Testaments were meant to be and remain together, both the Christians and the Turks <note>In this 18th-century context, "Turks" often served as a general term for non-Christian/Islamic peoples or the "other" in the global religious landscape</note>. Abraham was hidden in the Christian Church under the name of Jesus, and the Old Testament is here represented by Sarai, from whom the true Jesus Christ is born and revealed. However, the Old Testament and indeed all the Word of God was not to be recognized in its inner, true sense; and this is why Sarai was not to say that she was Abraham’s wife. It was not to be known that the Old Testament is the "wife" of the Spirit of Revelation, for otherwise the New Testament could not have endured under the name of Jesus; they would have killed Abraham (or Jesus) along with the New, and let the Old live alone. Therefore, the two Testaments had to be viewed externally as sister and brother, as literal histories. The Old had to testify in the "letter" to Christ in the New Testament as if to Abraham, and the New likewise had to refer itself in the letter to the Old. This is why Sarai had to say she was Abraham’s sister; thus it went well for Abraham in the New Testament for the sake of the Old’s testimony regarding Jesus Christ, and the Old was likewise retained within the Christian Church.

Under the two Testaments <note>The Old and New Testaments of the Bible</note> the entire Church of all nations is included. What is depicted here regarding the Christian Church in relation to the Old Testament—which the Church has not accepted for what it is in its true sense—is also meant for every church’s Word of God, which it has not accepted for what it is in its true sense. It has been accepted in a carnal sense, as outward histories, and thus the Church has spread itself therein and fathered outward "children of the church," just as one takes a woman in a carnal manner and fathers children with her. The Word of God is beautiful to look upon, and the princes of Pharaoh—acting as the teachers who ruled in the Church—looked upon the beauty in the literal letter. They admired the histories in their figurative forms, and the Christian Church was especially pleased by the beautiful image of Christ. Every church was pleased with its Word of God and its doctrine, and they introduced them into an outward government <term>Regiment: refers here to both political rule and the organized administration of the church</term>, which is Pharaoh. The worldly government then also protected the spiritual government, and thus the spiritual and worldly kingdoms are understood together as one under Pharaoh.

Through this, it went well for Abraham as the Word of God; it had a more fortunate progress in the Church, and he received sheep and cattle—namely, enough external matters of faith—as well as enough servants and maids who served him.

But in the end, Pharaoh—representing the governance of the Church in the literal sense, primarily the Christian Church above all others—is struck by God with great plagues for the sake of the wife, Sarai. This is because they have accepted God's Word not in its true sense, but only literally. Sarai, as the true doctrine in the Revelation of Christ, has also entered the Christian Church; for this reason, the great plague involving <term>Mausim</term><note>The author likely refers to "Mauzzim" or "Maussim" from Daniel 11:38, often interpreted by mystical writers as a false god of forces or external power in the church.</note> comes upon the Church, and its doctrines and temples are torn down and destroyed. Then Abraham is called and accepted as the true Jesus Christ. One no longer keeps Abraham's wife, the Word of God, within the literal doctrine, but admits and confesses that she is the wife of the Spirit of Revelation. She is no longer kept as a wife to bear external "church-children," but is sent away in this new state. Thus, the external Abraham and the external Sarai, along with everything they possess externally, pass away. The question of why Abraham did this—passing his wife off as his sister—will be answered later in Chapter 20, where this matter occurs once more. This plague upon Pharaoh is therefore the end of the Christian Church, which is now led back to the beginning of the Christian Church, when Abraham went up from Egypt with Lot, until the time of the Turkish Church <note>The "Turkish Church" refers to Islam, which the author views through a prophetic-historical lens common in 18th-century mystical theology.</note>, when Abraham and Lot separated from one another. In this way, the two churches of the Christians and the Turks are described and distinguished from one another quite clearly and tangibly through various figures in the dispensation of the New Testament.

Abram went up out of Egypt toward the South <note>original: "Mittag," literally "midday," a traditional term for the South</note>, that is, out of the Christian Church into the Turkish Church <note>The author uses "Turkish Church" as a historical shorthand for Islam or the Eastern territories</note>. This "going up" is once again a proof that the doctrine of the Christian Church is more earthly than the doctrine of the Turks and the Church of the Old Testament in general. The two Testaments, Abraham and Lot, journeyed forth together. We find Lot here in the ascent out of Egypt, even though it was not previously stated that he had gone down into Egypt with Abraham. He therefore represents, along with Abraham, the two Testaments, which were represented by Abram and Sarai during the descent into Egypt. Abraham is taken here as the Old [Testament] and Lot as the New; both Testaments were very numerous in figures and images, from which the Church made its wealth. But because they were opposed to one another in the letter, and also in their ceremonies, they could not both exist or be practiced in their ceremonies within a single church. There was strife among the herdsmen of both Testaments, that is, between the teachers of the letter <note>those who interpret scripture literally rather than spiritually</note>, for we all had only animal eyes and animal understanding, and did not understand God's Word. We took Sarai, as the doctrine of the wisdom of God, in an animal and fleshly sense, for the inner meaning had withdrawn from us along with the true light and understanding, and had separated itself from the external; therefore, the separation of the two Testaments with their churches was inevitable. This is indicated first by Abraham’s journey toward the South, to the place where his tent was in the beginning, where he had made the altar at the start. This is the Old Testament, in which the Turkish Church went back again into circumcision; for the Old is the beginning and existed with its church, as the Jews, even before the New Testament.

Furthermore, it is indicated by the separation of Abraham and Lot, as we now see the matter even more clearly. Abraham asked Lot whether he wanted to go to the left, then he [Abraham] would go to the right. The left is the New Testament with the Christian Church, and this "left" the Targum <note>A-ramic translations or paraphrases of the Hebrew Bible</note> calls Midnight <note>original: "Mitternacht," a traditional term for the North</note>. In the northern part of the world, such as Europe, the Christian Church also dwells. But the right is the Old Testament, and that the Targum calls Midday [the South], where Abraham moved to here. Toward the South the Turkish Church also dwells, and so the whole earth witnesses to me that this explanation of mine is correct. Now, however, let us see where Lot separated himself. The Jordan is called a river of judgment. <note>The author derives this from the Hebrew 'Yarden' (descender) or 'Dan' (judge).</note> Judgment, however, belongs primarily to the Christian Church; therefore, the Church is also the Jordan. The Jordan flows into the dead Salt Sea, in which no creature can live, and that is the place called the destroyed Sodom. Just as the Jordan loses itself in this dead sea, so too the literal doctrine <note>original: "Buchstabenlehre" — the rigid, outward interpretation of the Bible</note> of the New Testament loses itself within the Christian Church in the Sodom of Mauzzim <note>original: "Mausim" — a reference to the "god of fortresses" in Daniel 11:38, often interpreted by mystics as a false or worldly religious power</note> at the end. The entire outward Christian Church with its doctrine is thus Sodom, but the true Sodom is at its end, up to which point Lot pitches his tent within the outward Christian Church, but no further.

The New Testament under the name of Jesus is the Garden of Jehovah and is also the land of Egypt. In itself, in its true sense, it is well-watered—rich with healing doctrines from the Spirit of Life. But the outward Jehovah, the outward Christ—namely, the outward literal doctrine of the Christians—has corrupted and overturned this beautiful garden from the beginning, and that was from the outward God of Nature. In the same way, the site of the Salt Sea at the beginning of the world was the Garden of Jehovah, the best place on earth, the special Paradise on earth where Adam was created into the lower nature and into the body of the nature of the earth. The corruption and overturning of this place into a dead Salt Sea is now the figure and image in the realm of outward nature for the final and total destruction of the Sodom of the Christian Church and doctrine. There, Lot’s wife—representing the outward doctrine of the Christians—remains standing as a pillar of salt, handed over to the uttermost curse that comes upon her with Mauzzim, just like the Salt Sea, and is useful for nothing more.

The Garden of Jehovah is situated as one comes toward Zoar, which means "small." This special garden is also small when considered against the remaining part of the earth. Lot chose the region of the Jordan as his primary inheritance, which represents the teaching of the New Testament. He departed from the East, representing the Old Testament, where Abraham walked alongside the Turks <note>The author uses "Turks" here as a broad historical term for non-Christian or Islamic peoples, often associated in this period's theology with the "outer" or "natural" world.</note>. In this manner, the two Testaments and their respective churches separated from one another like two brothers. Indeed, these two are also Jacob and Esau, who lay as twins within the single body of the New Testament's stewardship and struggled against one another.

Abraham dwelt in the land of Canaan, and Lot in the cities of that region. The Old Testament, along with the Turks, occupied the outer land of Canaan; yet the Old is also present within the Christian Church, as if within the Canaan of the doctrine. In this comparison against Lot, Abraham belongs to the Old Testament, and he dwelt where Lot also dwelt, and yet also among the Turks; thus, the separation of Christians and Turks is also signified here. This separation is a figure <term>figure: a symbolic representation or "type" where historical events foreshadow spiritual truths</term> in the kingdom of nature, showing how the Inner and the Outer have separated from each other, each into its own place, so that one is not the other even though they exist alongside one another. Sodom represents the Christian Church, and here it is explicitly stated that the people of Sodom were very wicked and great sinners against Jehovah. No people on earth are named so specifically as being particularly godless in the figures of Scripture as the Christians; therefore, Christ also says of his future coming that all unrighteousness would prevail, it would become an abomination of desolation, and he compares this desolation to the destruction of Sodom.

Four kings are named here who fight against five. The four represent the worldly government of the Christians, and the five represent the spiritual government, such as the Church and its doctrine: for the number five is the number of the Christian Church, and they are the five kings who have fallen from the ten horns; Revelation 17:10. **Amraphel** means one who speaks toward a fall and overthrow. He is called King of Shinar, which the Targum <note>A term for the Aramaic translations or paraphrases of the Hebrew Bible</note> identifies as King of Babylon, and that is the final and great Babylon of the Christians. **Arioch** derives his name from a lion; he is King of Ellasar, which means a deviating god, a god who takes something away and causes one to deviate. These are the regent-lions who fall away from the Word of God, do away with God’s law, and cause men to deviate and sin. **Chedorlaomer** means as much as a host of war over the sheaves, or like a funeral pyre before the sheaves. The sheaves are the matters of faith of the Church, wherein the Church had its harvest. He is King of Elam, which means a mystery, and that is Babylon, the great mystery, where they secretly prepare their affairs for the overthrow of the Church. **Tidal** means one who diligently ascends or enters, and he is called King of the Nations or of the Heathen. The aforementioned four arranged a war with **Bera**, which means one who is in evil; he is called King of Sodom, and that is the Christian Church, for Lot dwells in Sodom. The second is named **Birsha**, that is, one who is in godlessness. He is King of Gomorrah. The Christian Church is called Sodom in regard to the doctrine of the New Testament, and Gomorrah in regard to the doctrine of the Old Testament. The tyrants take away all the goods of Sodom and Gomorrah—all the teachings of the church and also the church properties, the spiritual nourishment as well as the physical—for robbing and plundering is their business. When they take away God’s Word, they also take his possessions, and poor Lot cannot help himself, for he does not hold the revelation of Christ in his hands. When they fundamentally demolish the Word of God by treating it merely as literal history—and thus as something entirely fabricated by men—the literal history cannot be sustained. With what then shall poor Lot help himself and move beyond doubt? Therefore, this is a true spiritual captivity and a great anguish for the minds of the poor children of God, who must necessarily become extremely anxious through the rapidly and visibly increasing desolations and judgments of God. With what shall the poor human being comfort himself when, in such distress, God’s Word is rendered doubtful to him and depicted as a fabricated story? But the merciful, eternal God will stand by poor Lot and rescue him from this distress; as now follows:

15. N. And he divided himself against them by night, he and his servants, and struck them <gloss>and pursued them as far as Hobah, which is to the left (or toward the north) of Damascus.</gloss>

16. A. And he brought back again all the goods; and also brought back his brother’s son Lot and his goods, as well as the women and the people.

The believers have been led captive from the very beginning of the Church into outward literal doctrines <note>original: "Buchstabenlehren" - a term used by mystics to describe a rigid, superficial understanding of scripture without spiritual life</note>, which captivity is included within this final captivity. The one who escaped and announced Lot’s captivity to Abram is the Spirit with the true doctrine in the Revelation of Christ. Abram is called a Hebrew here, which means "one from the other side" or "from another side." Abram is here in a different doctrine than the outward doctrine is; that is why he is called a Hebrew. This name appears here for the first time in the Old Testament, a name which the Jews have borne until now because they had to represent the outward figure of Abram’s seed in its servitude.

Abram, however, did not only rescue Lot—representing here the believers of the Christian Church—but also the believers of the entire Church of all three dispensations <term>Households/Dispensations: the three major eras of divine governance, usually identified as the Age of Nature (Gentiles), the Age of Law (Jews), and the Age of Grace (Christians)</term>. Therefore, he has the three allies: Mamre the Amorite, Eshcol, and Aner, who are the Word of God in the three dispensations. Mamre the Amorite is the New Testament, Eshcol the Old, and Aner is the Word of God for the Heathen <note>Gentile</note> Church. All of these are allies of Abram, for it is the Word of one single God. These were also the trained or prepared ones in his house, for he went among all nations with God’s Word. The number 18 specifically signifies the 18 centuries <note>original: "Säcula"</note> of the Christian Church. The 300, however, signify the three dispensations, or also the three religions—of the Jews, Christians, and Turks <note>Muslims</note>—where one has had to strike the other in the letter <note>the literal sense of scripture</note>, for which the youths were also trained in the letter. Therefore, Abram divided himself with his servants over them by night and struck them. The night, however, is the time of the outward Church, and is also simultaneously the thick, dark night at the end, when they are struck.

The Spirit has always fought against captivity in external things and has pursued the enemies as far as Dan—that is, into the Judgment, which is the Revelation of Christ. Here, all these things are brought to light alongside the three allies <note>referring to Aner, Eshcol, and Mamre from Genesis 14</note>; and with them, the personal Abraham also organizes his servants. He demonstrates what the Word of God is, and how religions and households all stand exactly as they are described as figures in God’s Word. He explains what has already passed and happened, what is occurring now with the final captivity of Lot, and what will yet happen and be in the future. All of this he demonstrates with God’s own Word, and thereby he upholds God’s Word as a true Word; he brings back these captured goods of Lot and rescues Lot from his captivity. He has also divided himself with his servant—as the doctrine of the Revelation of Christ—during the night that has prevailed until now, both in two representations of the same and in other ways. He has continually struck them with their laws and regimes, and he does not cease, but pursues them as far as Hobah—which means "Guilt" <note>original: "Verschuldung"</note>. That is, until they reach the utmost guilt through the revelation of their abominations. This Hobah is to the left of Damascus. Damascus is the capital of Syria; but in the figures <note>symbolic types</note>, Syria is an image of the Christian Church. Damascus means "a sack of blood" <note>etymological interpretation of Damascus as 'Dam' (blood) and 'Saq' (sack)</note>; and is that not also the Christian Church? Has not the blood of the saints and witnesses of Jesus been found in this Babylon? This sack of blood, however, is primarily at the end, and to the left—or, as the Targum <note>Aramaic translation/commentary of the Hebrew Bible</note> says, toward midnight <note>meaning the North</note> before Damascus. In this bloodbath is the true guilt of the tyrant: Mauzzim <note>a deity or force of fortresses mentioned in Daniel 11:38</note>, and until then Abraham pursues him constantly with judging and striking. There, Lot is also brought back with all his goods, and all the people are redeemed from the captivity of the tyrants.

This war, which according to the literal letter is placed so far back in the beginning of the world, and yet is primarily at the end, has been set as a mirror for the human race. In it, they could see the devilish nature of rulers and of everything that loves to be high and wants to govern, as well as God’s help and salvation for the pious in their distress. One can see from this who the children of God and the children of this world are, and how greatly the children of this world are possessed and blinded by the devil. They murder one another

They strive for worldly power and majesty, and put their lives in danger to swing themselves high aloft, though they do not know if they will maintain it. It is through the delusion of Satan that one exalts himself over another and takes what is his by force; and all kings who start untimely wars and devastate lands and people are the bondservants of Satan. Satan is the god and prince of this world, and has the upper hand over what is good; therefore, these godless ones only strive for how they may force themselves upward and ride over bowed knees. But the holy God only gives and does good to all, taking nothing and plaguing no one; even so are His children natured, whom He enlivens and drives. But that war is not from the holy God but from the Devil is proven by the fact that God, in the end, will break all war, sword, and bow. It is in vain if one wishes to justify war with the example of Abraham <note>original: "Abram"</note>. The war of Abraham is no war with sword and projectile, but with the spirit of his mouth, with which he kills the godless and rescues the pious. God certainly wants to root out evil when it is time, but He does not strangle it Himself; rather, He has His servant, the Devil, who may strangle with his rulers, for he must do according to His will: namely, break his own kingdom and root out the godless, so that the true King of Glory may enter into His reign. Oh, how clearly this war of Sodom reveals from what sort of root the rulers and their wars originate. Abraham brings them to an end, and for that, King Melchizedek blesses him in the name of the holy God; therefore, they must certainly not be from the holy God.

->**The Valley of Shaveh**<-

The Valley of Shaveh means a valley in which everything is made equal and level, and that is the teaching of Jesus Christ: for through Jesus Christ everything is made equal and right, and all religions are made equal to one another; therefore it is also called the King’s Valley. Into this King’s Valley comes the king of Sodom to meet Abram, as he returned from the battle with Chedorlaomer, and this signifies the following:

Through the teaching of Jesus Christ, the outward government is defeated. Now, however, the teaching of the outward Christian church has also defeated the government of the Church of the Old Testament <note>original: "A. Testam."</note>, and the question arises, as it were, with this figure <note>A "figure" in this context is a symbolic type or prophetic foreshadowing.</note>, whether the outward Christ, who has thus defeated the government of the Old Testament Church, is the

the true Jesus Christ, or the rightful Abram, in whom all nations shall be blessed. The King of Sodom—representing the external, literal doctrine of Christ—went out to meet Abram as he returned from this battle; that is to say, this "Sodom" doctrine wanted to claim to be the true Abram. The institutional Christian Church and its formal teachings wanted to claim credit for this victory; their "Christ of the Letter" <note>The author distinguishes between the "Christ of the Letter" (historical/literal) and the "Living Spirit."</note> was supposed to have established everything. Once they had "defeated" the Old Testament <note>original: "A. Testam."</note>, they claimed to have defeated Chedorlaomer. In short, they turned to their external Christ and attributed everything to him; and this is the King of Sodom going out to meet Abram.

This Melchizedek is the Spirit of God itself with the true internal doctrine. At the end, he meets the personal Abram within the mind <note>original: "Gemüthe," referring to the soul or inner disposition</note> and presents him with bread and wine; that is, he presents him with the true internal meaning of the Two Testaments. This is the "tenth" taken from all things—the interior within the exterior—and Abram gives this back to Melchizedek. He attributes everything solely to the true Jesus Christ as the tithe from all external things, and he desires nothing at all from the possessions of the King of Sodom, nor from any of the literal doctrines of the Christian Church. He has lifted up his hands—his teaching of the Two Testaments—to Jehovah, the most high God, who created Heaven and Earth. Toward Him he directs all teachings of Jesus Christ, and this is the true Jesus Christ himself. It is not, however, the external Christ who is said to have been born of a woman 1,700 years ago; for that figure did not create Heaven and Earth, and how could he have created Heaven and Earth when he himself had not yet been born?

Melchizedek means a King of Righteousness; he is called a King of Salem, which is to say, a King of Peace, because with him peace comes to the whole earth, and all war ends with this war of Sodom. From this, the King of Sodom may conclude that his external Christ is not the true Jesus Christ: for with that Christ, peace did not come to the earth, and war did not end. Rather, his church itself was the worst den of murderers, full of war and bloodshed on earth, as its end now testifies. Therefore, this church was not the Kingdom of Christ, and his kingdom was not of this world. # Jerusalem

...rusalem, which is one and the same as the King of Salem, for with him the New Jerusalem comes down from God out of heaven. <note>A reference to Revelation (Apocalypse) 21:2.</note>

This Melchizedek is within Abram himself and is one with him. He gives him the tenth <note>The tithe</note>, the inner essence from all outward teachings, and then Abram gives this tenth back to him again: for man can give God nothing except what God Himself has previously given him. In the outward appearance, Abraham is a man like other men, but in the inner being, he is Melchizedek, the King of Righteousness. The revelation of Jesus Christ is the tenth which Melchizedek, the Spirit of the Most High God, has given to him. In this teaching, he is a priest of the Most High God, for it comes from the Most High God and is also the highest teaching on earth. Therefore, it is also called the Son of God, and Melchizedek is compared to the Son of God, as the Epistle to the Hebrews says. <note>Hebrews 7:3</note> The personal Abram at the end is therefore also Christ, or the Messiah, who is to come at the end, and in his teaching, he is the King of Righteousness. He brings righteousness to light in the teaching and judges rightly; he is a priest of the Most High God and is King and Priest at the same time; for he is the true King of Jerusalem, even though he must now still go up the Mount of Olives with David and weep. Because God has made David king over His people Israel, he, as Abram, strikes down all other kings. He strikes them with the spirit of his mouth, and in this teaching, the Most High God gives them into his hands. They are his enemies, and he regards all governments <term>Regimente: refers here to worldly political powers or regimes</term> of the whole earth as his enemies and not as his friends; as enemies of the Most High God and not as His friends. Therefore Melchizedek says to Abram: "Blessed be you by the Most High God, who made heaven and earth, and praised be the Most High God, who has delivered your enemies into your hand." <note>Genesis 14:19-20</note> All governments of the earth are enemies of the Most High God. It was all vanity, and His kingdom was not of this world, but their kingdom was. Now, however, He will no longer leave His honor to another, nor His glory to idols. Therefore, Abram does not stop striking until all kingdoms of the earth, together with all religions, are crushed and shattered, and all people have been redeemed and set free.

Abram is called a Hebrew here, and the Epistle to the Hebrews—that is, to those who are meant to transition into a different doctrine—says much about this Priesthood of Melchizedek. It says, in Chapter 5, verse 4: No one takes this honor upon himself, but he must be called by God, just as Aaron was <note>Aaron represents the established, hereditary priesthood of the Old Testament</note>, like the outward teaching office of Christians, which was outwardly called in its own time and order. Thus also Christ—who is here Abraham himself <note>The author uses an allegorical interpretation where Abraham represents the developing Christ-spirit within the believer</note>—did not put himself into the honor of becoming a High Priest; for he could not take or give the Revelation of Christ to himself. Rather, it was He who said to him: *You are my son, today I have begotten you* <note>original Latin: "Filius meus es tu, ego hodie genui te"</note>; today, in the doctrine of the Revelation of Christ, which has never before existed on earth, have I first begotten you. *You are a priest forever, after the order of Melchizedek.* What he had to suffer, how he had to learn obedience and thereby become a cause of eternal salvation for all—that is, for this eternal Kingdom of Christ—can be read further. The Spirit says: He is named by God a High Priest after the order of Melchizedek, of whom we would have much to say, but it is difficult because you are so slow of understanding.

In the 7th Chapter, Abram is taken as the outward Word of God and held up against Melchizedek; and the Spirit says there of Melchizedek that he is without father and mother, and without genealogy. He has neither beginning of days nor end of life; he is made like unto the Son of God and remains a priest forever. His lineage is not named. Here, the outward priesthood with its outward doctrine is set in opposition to this inward priesthood with the inward, true doctrine. This Melchizedek at the end has no father or mother from the outward priesthood or outward doctrine: for he is not from it and has no lineage within it. This doctrine is not from any human teachings <term>human teachings: "Menschenlehren," referring to dogmas or traditions created by men rather than divine inspiration</term>, but directly from the Spirit of God; and the Spirit is actually the true Melchizedek, who has neither beginning nor end of life. The doctrine itself, however, does not have its beginning exactly at the moment it was revealed, but already lay hidden previously in the Word of God. It will also never take an end so long as a doctrine exists on earth, but remains a priest forever: for this Kingdom of Christ in the universal Church is also called an eternal kingdom. This, then, is the actual sense of the words: without beginning...

beginning of days, nor end of life. No one could name his lineage, for who could name this teaching before it existed, and who could imagine that such a teaching would yet come forth from the Word of God? Furthermore, the Levites are brought into the argument, who are descended from Abraham and took tithes from the people, even though Abraham himself gave the tithe to Melchizedek, for which Melchizedek blessed him; and the lesser is always blessed by the better. By this it is suggested that the Levites, or outward teachers, have indeed held their teaching to be the right and true priesthood—which the tithe <note>The "tithe" here symbolizes the spiritual essence or the "best part" of the teaching.</note> signifies, which they allowed to be given to them—and yet this is not yet the true teaching and the true priesthood. This is proven thus: Abraham, from whom they descend, gave the tithe to Melchizedek. In this comparison, one should take Abraham as the Word of God itself, from which the Levites with their outward teaching originate. Now, if Abraham, as the Word of God, gives the tithe, or the inner essence, from himself, then the Levites with their outward teaching were also included in Abraham, and they must now in the end also concede the tithe from themselves to Melchizedek; and they must allow the teaching of the Revelation of Christ the preference over their outward teaching: for the teaching of the Word of God in the letter <note>The "letter" refers to the literal, surface-level reading of scripture as opposed to its spiritual meaning.</note> is justified and blessed through the true inner sense of the Spirit, whereby the literal teaching is set in its right place, and only then is it true, but otherwise not. Since the lesser is blessed by the better, the teaching in the Revelation of Christ is better than the previous outward teaching.

Now it continues: If perfection had come through the Levitical priesthood—that is, through the outward teaching—what further need would there be to say that another priesthood should arise, according to the order of Melchizedek, and not according to the order of Aaron? That is, according to the outward order of the Christians: for Aaron stands as a figure of Christ, and the outward Christ is the image of the true Jesus Christ; therefore he is also Aaron, who made the golden idol-calf. <note>A provocative comparison suggesting that focusing only on the historical or "outward" Christ without the inner spirit is akin to Aaron's idolatry.</note> Further:

For where the priesthood is changed, there must also be a change of the law. For he of whom these things are spoken belongs to another tribe, from which no one has ever served at the altar.

And moreover, which is significant, this happened not without an oath; for those others, the external teachers, became priests without an oath, but this one with an oath by Him who says to him: *The Lord has sworn and will not repent: You are a priest for eternity, according to the order of Melchizedek.* Abraham will therefore be and remain King and Priest, and no devil shall be able to prevent it. He takes all of this according to the literal sense <note>original: "dem Buchstaben nach"</note> and attributes it to Abram or Jesus Christ. But Abram wants nothing to do with it. He says to the King of Sodom: I have lifted up my hands to Jehovah, the most high God, who made heaven and earth, that I will not take anything of all that is yours, from a thread even to a shoelace, so that you may not say, "I, even I, have made Abram rich." Just as solemnly as Jehovah swore to Abram or Melchizedek <note>The author identifies Melchizedek as a type or manifestation of the eternal priesthood</note> that he shall be a priest and king forever, and that he shall be a blessing before all nations; so also does Abram swear highly and dearly to the King of Sodom—the Christian Church—that he will not accept even the very least of all its doctrines which it teaches and believes concerning Christ, concerning his person, his birth, life, suffering, death, resurrection, ascension, sitting at the right hand of God, and return for judgment. There is not a true word in any of it, according to the sense and opinion of the King of Sodom. Abram rejects it all and crushes it all, for not one stone shall remain upon another in this entire religious temple of the Christians, so that this King of Sodom may not say he has made me rich with his teaching, that he possessed the right doctrine of Christ and I had received and borrowed it from him. One need only look to see if this doctrine of mine can be found with a single teacher of the entire Christian Church, indeed, of the entire earth. Truly not; therefore it is without genealogy, father, or mother <note>A reference to the description of Melchizedek in Hebrews 7:3</note>, and has come upon me from no other human being. I will therefore have nothing of the property of the King of Sodom, and he shall not have the souls of men either; rather, they shall enter into this doctrine of mine. But the three boys—the three external households <note>likely referring to the three historical dispensations or external church structures</note>—and the people who were therein, they may have their portion of external things. The external doctrine was their portion, and one doctrine had to strike the other, for thus was God's word in the letter set against itself, and so they were certainly with Abram. But here at the end, Abram wants no such external portion; yet all three shall nevertheless have a share in Abram's internal goods and dwell with him in Hebron, as many of them as are written in the Book of Life.

But, when the captivity of Lot was announced to Abram, Mamre, Eshcol, and Aner stand in the background, because in the Christian Church, which is Mamre, the revelation of Christ has dawned, wherein one sees the captivity

## Concerning God’s Covenant with Abram, and how his Faith was reckoned to him as Righteousness.

Wonderful is God, and just as wonderful is His Word. Just as God has the ways of all men in His hand and guides us, even so will every person find their own path in God’s Word—whatever touches them regarding their journey—if only the Spirit of Life enters into them, so that they can be moved by God’s Word. I will begin here with myself and touch upon what primarily concerns me and all those who are united with me in the Revelation of Jesus Christ, or of Adam. The application of these words to the general public will then be easily made, because we humans are all of one kind, nature, and essence, and children of a single Father.

In the preceding figure, it was depicted how Abram slew the kings; and one should note that this did not happen with sword and projectile, but through the spirit of his mouth <note>The author interprets the biblical battle of Abraham as a spiritual or rhetorical victory rather than a physical one</note>. This occurred primarily in the two presentations of the Revelation of Christ. God made great promises to Abram: namely, that He would set him as a blessing to all nations, and as a light to all nations, to open the eyes of the blind; that he should be a blessing, and that all nations of the earth should be blessed in him and through him. God, through the Spirit of Life, pressed these promises into his heart, so that even his outer man could know for certain that these words of God were intended for his person, and that it was God’s will that he should slay the kings in this manner. Meanwhile, their end is not yet here, and Abraham has no seed—that is, his teaching has no progress. A thousand doubts and anxieties have assailed him since the presentation in the year 1761. He has had to be unsettled and fleeting, from one place to another, as the history of Abram generally shows, that he had to wander back and forth. He did not know that the Sodomites had been given a further extension of nine years, during which he was to walk about in tribulation. Thus he had to be quite lonely and

God had thus anointed David as king, but he nevertheless had to be cast out for a time; and Job had to let himself be mocked by people and bow before them, whom he would not have wished to place as sheepdogs with his sheep. When this voice, "fear not," becomes alive in the essence of man, then he does not fear, for the essence of God itself, which is in his soul, speaks or works this; and then one believes and trusts firmly and has certainty within oneself and no doubt. But that does not remain so at all times, and the powerful sensation of God's working in the soul often withdraws; then fear, anxiety, doubt, and uncertainty return, for in the outer man there is no certainty, not even in Abram himself, unless it be [established] through the stepping forth of the inner power of God into the outer man.
