Thus, when God speaks to Abram, saying, "fear not," it already presupposes that he had been afraid; for had he not feared, God would not have found it necessary to encourage him not to be afraid. Everyone can therefore see that Abram, in the outer man <note>The "outer man" refers to the physical, mortal self as opposed to the spiritual "inner man."</note>, is nothing other than all other men: weak, frail, deficient, and therefore not free from fear when God withdraws His strength.

Therefore, the Epistle to the Hebrews says of Christ, as of Melchizedek, in Chapter 5:7: *In the days of his flesh, he offered up prayers and supplications with loud cries and tears to him who was able to save him from death, and he was heard because of his reverence. Although he was a son, he learned obedience through what he suffered; and being made perfect, he became the source of eternal salvation to all who obey him, being designated by God a high priest after the order of Melchizedek.* <note>original: "nach der Ordnung Melchisedeck"</note>

The Son of God is this Abram at the end, and nothing else at all. The external Christ is a nothing and is rooted out; therefore, one must no longer direct one's thoughts toward him. Nor is it the true Jesus Christ: for He is the personal God Himself, and in the latter times He is no priest on earth as Melchizedek or Abram is. The Son of God is thus this Abram, and is also David, Elijah, Moses, Enoch, Noah, Jeremiah, Jacob, Joshua—which are all one and the same. 

Although God has now anointed him as King, he has nevertheless had to live in much misery and wretchedness since this anointing, and has had to be a servant and minister to others, seeking his bread with much sorrow. Thus he has had to learn obedience, even though he knows that he is the Son of God, of whom He says: *You are my son, today I have begotten you.* <note>A reference to Psalm 2:7, frequently cited in the New Testament to establish Christ's divine sonship.</note> Of this Son of God it is also said: *He is not ashamed to call us his brothers.* 

We are all brothers and children of a single heavenly Father, or sons of God, and in this time we are all subject to the same weaknesses, frailties, fear, and doubt. It goes strangely with us. We often do not see or notice the seed of the Spirit within us; instead, we find ourselves as if dry and unfruitful. It appears as if there were nothing else in us or about us but corrupted nature. And a naturally honest man, who does not yet stand in the inner life, knows of nothing else within himself but nature,

For the spirit is unknown to him. He goes along lonely and barren, seeing no spiritual seed. If we only enter into such a state of fear, unrest, and doubt alongside Abraham—who also saw no seed, and yet God received him into His covenant—then we should wrap ourselves here in the promise of God when He speaks: **"Fear not, I am your shield and your very great reward."** <note>Genesis 15:1</note> Then we shall say: "Ah Lord, you give me nothing; I go along barren in the life of the spirit, and my natural being in its corruption must inherit what is due to it." But if we earnestly desire to have seed, God speaks: "Your corrupted nature shall not inherit, but that which shall yet come from your inner being—even though it is not yet there—that shall inherit; that shall multiply within your being, in purity and clarity, like the stars of heaven." Only look toward heaven—that is, direct your heart and mind toward heaven and withdraw it from earthly things as much as you can—and your seed will surely come. Only believe it, hope for it, and wait for it. So also, my dear brothers and sisters, you who have accepted the revelation of Christ, or will yet accept it: believe in the promise of God, hope for it, and wait for it, even if everything God has promised is not yet present.

It is said of Abraham: **"He believed Jehovah, and that was counted to him for righteousness."** <note>original: "Er glaubte dem Jehova"</note> I have believed the promises of God and still believe them; therefore I have written the revelation of Christ and continue to write. This is my righteousness, for in the outer man I have none. Who can find a pure one among those where there is no pure one? We all fail in manifold ways, and there is no one on earth who does not sin. Lord, if you should mark iniquities, who could stand before you! <note>A reference to Psalm 130:3</note>

Abraham still had no seed. Should he then be damned because of that? By no means. God says to him: **"Fear not, I am your shield and your very great reward, and you shall have seed, even if you have none right now."** He believed God quite firmly because he dearly wished it to be so, and that was his righteousness; otherwise, he had none.

No one lacks anything, for God is in each, and God is enough for all; no one will envy another, but rather each will rejoice in the beauty and wonders of the other. This diversity was the very purpose of creation. To this end, God spread out the heavens with the constellations and guided His eternal powers into these outward forms in division and multiplicity, in order to thereby create the host of humanity, and to lead Himself with the human race into a glory that He did not have before the creation of the world. This, His host of humans, He leads out according to their number, and calls them all by name, and not a single one shall be missing.

When Abraham asks how he shall recognize that he will possess the land? This indicates how the external man <note>the "outer man" refers to the physical or worldly self as opposed to the spiritual "inner man"</note> so easily falls into fear and doubt regarding the promises of God, as also happened here with Abram. Things do not always go as the external man thinks. Therefore God says, Isaiah 55:9: My thoughts are not as your thoughts, and your ways are not as my ways. Just as the rain and snow fall from heaven and do not return there again, but moisten the earth so that it brings forth and greens, to sow seed and give bread for food; so shall my word be, which has gone out of my mouth. It shall not return to me empty until it has accomplished what pleases me, and is successful in that for which I sent it. When the rain falls, the herb, seed, bread, and everything are not there instantly, but one comes out of the other and after the other; so it also had to go with the revelation of Christ, which had gone out as God's word from His mouth. What it is meant to accomplish, it will accomplish, and God's ways with it were different than Abram initially thought. The sign that God gave to Abram with the animals, by which he should recognize that he would certainly possess the land, will become clear in the following; here I only wish to note further that Abraham's departure from Ur at the beginning of the world is nothing other than the passage through the great movement of nature, which occurred through the strong movement of the subterranean fire, as the satanic nature. For man fell asleep to this internal, divine life-force, and from that point on, the actual external sleeping of humanity first began; previously, however, there was no sleep. The images of Scripture, which indicate spiritual things, are thus taken from things that actually exist in nature. In nature, they are for the most part the thing itself, but they are an "image" in respect to more internal and spiritual things, for everything external is an image and parable of the internal. The spiritual tree is hidden within the tree of nature; therein the two kingdoms—the Kingdom of Nature and the Kingdom of Christ—are brought together as one in these figures.

Because the revelation of life perished in external nature, a great terror and darkness fell upon the internal Abram. The high understanding of humanity vanished and a great darkness covered it; therefore, one has been unable to understand the Word of God in its deep mysteries. Humans were reduced to the status of animals and had to mate for procreation just like the animals. God demanded them Himself from Abram, and Abram presented them: for when God speaks, it happens, and when He commands, it stands firm.

Abram performed the dividing of the animals. Thus, humans divided themselves, but the external God-of-Nature was within man with His speaking, and humans acted under a threefold written Word of God. In all of this, Abram was present internally, but externally he had his great faults and weaknesses. For we were all enclosed under sin and unbelief, and blindness had to befall the entire "Israel of all nations." This was the great darkness that fell upon Abram. The Land of Blessing was promised to him and his seed, but with this condition: namely, that all of this had to pass over him. This is entirely natural, for they have placed themselves in God’s seat of judgment and judicial essence. Therefore, when the essence of God itself sits for judgment, they will be seized in the severity in which they have unmercifully placed themselves. But whoever has been merciful toward his subordinates shall also obtain mercy. The government of external nature has indeed raised itself above others, and not the holy God. If, however, they have the Tree of Life <term>Tree of Life: A mystical concept representing divine grace and eternal vitality</term> along with them in the Tree of Nature, then their high station not only does them no harm, but gives them all the more means and opportunity to glorify the holy God in the quality of His mercy and kindness toward the lowly, the humble, and the poor.

On this day, which represents the entire span of corruption from beginning to end, God made a covenant with Abram—specifically a twofold covenant. There is the inner covenant at the end, which is even now being made and written in this time, and the outer covenant in the beginning. Within the outer, however, the inner remains hidden, for the kingdom of nature and the kingdom of Christ exist within one another. According to this covenant, God intended to give the land to Abram’s children. He gave it to them outwardly under the curse, and at the end, He gives it to them in the blessing; for we had to walk through both His curse and His blessing, as Moses says. By the river of Egypt and the great river Euphrates <note>original: "Phrat"</note>, the Church is first indicated in the number 2, as in two Testaments. Egypt is that which precedes in view of what follows, as one departs from Egypt, as the precursor, into the following state. In this reflection, the Old Testament is Egypt. Euphrates means "fruitful," and that is the stewardship <term>Haushaltung: a theological term for a specific era of God's plan or 'dispensation'</term> of the New Testament. The ten nations <note>Genesis 15:19-21</note> signify the whole of the outward Church of all peoples. They are divided into three verses, which signify the three stewardships, and thus it is a pure doctrine of numbers.

This sacrifice of Abram with the animals and their pieces was therefore a miraculous sacrifice, and is the sacrifice of Christ; but both internally and externally. All of nature on earth, the entire Church, and the whole man in all his parts belong within it; it is thus no trivial matter to be viewed superficially. The purely outward man consists of three parts alone, for he carries within him the two natures above and below the firmament <note>original: "Wolkenveste," referring to the atmospheric or spiritual divide in Genesis</note>, which together with the body make three parts. In these three parts, the outward man was corrupted, and the fierce qualities of the satanic nature fell like birds of prey upon these parts, awakening evil thoughts and desires. But Abraham—representing the Spirit—continually chased them away whenever he was active within a person. The inner being was crucified within the outward man, which caused the Spirit unspeakable groans; hence a sighing often arises within us without the outward man knowing why, and he cannot put it into words. The fire that passed between the pieces is also twofold. The fire of the satanic nature works evil. But the inner holy fire of the Spirit consumes the evil again when it passes between the parts. When the iniquity of the Amorites is full, the holy fire of the Spirit comes with judgment and consumes all evil.

She is called an Egyptian, and in Egypt there is both servitude and also the state of being a stranger; but Egypt exists in Nature, the current course of Nature in its fallen state. Without this corrupted time, the procreation of mankind was not possible, and before this, Abram could not beget children with Sarai. Sarai and Hagar represent the two kingdoms; the Inner and Eternal is Sarai, and the Outer and Temporal is Hagar. They are the twofold tree: the Tree of Nature, with the outer kingdom, and the Tree of Life, with the inner kingdom of Christ. The Inner and Eternal could not multiply in any other way than within the Outer and Temporal, and thus the Inner had to be built up through the Outer for the purpose of birthing. Abram is Adam, and he is also Christ Jesus. Everything comes from him; he is the root of the entire human race. Because we now see that the procreation of the human race happens in the outer, corrupted nature, he also had to be clothed with an outer, corrupted nature, so that the outer man could also originate from his outer man. The root of the tree had to be planted in the field of outer nature in its fallen state, so that it could then grow out into a trunk with its boughs, branches, blossoms, flowers, and fruits, which are the entire human race. Therefore, see, you dear children of men! We all stand together in this tree. Let us rejoice that we stand in the will of God, even though we are in imperfection. He willed it so, and nothing—nothing—will the imperfection harm us, if we only stand in mere natural honesty and fairness. The eternal tree has grown hidden beneath the cover of the temporal, until its full expansion. Now it reveals itself. The power of its root has finally driven out the flower of Paradise in Abraham. The root has revealed itself. Jesus Christ is manifest and shines to all the peoples of the earth. The tree rejuvenates itself. The root drives its power and its sap into all its boughs and branches, and casts the withered branches away from itself. In the process of procreation, the external <note>the outward, physical nature</note> gains the upper hand; the internal <note>the spiritual essence</note> is contemptible in the eyes of the external. For this reason, the children of God were contemptible in the eyes of the children of the world. Canaan is, with regard to the two Testaments, the departure from the dispensation <note>German: Haushaltung; refers to the theological concept of "economies" or eras of divine administration</note> of the Old Testament into the dispensation of the New Testament. The ten years in the land of Canaan, during which Sarah was barren and until Abraham went in to Hagar, represent the time of the Christian Church from its beginning until the rise of the Turkish Church <note>The author uses "Turkish Church" to refer to Islam, following a common early modern European convention</note>; for the latter, as Hagar, became pregnant under the Old <insert>Testament</insert>, and despised the external Sarah, representing the Church under the name of Jesus. In the same way, the Christian Church despised the internal Sarah along with the Old: for in this view, she is the handmaid herself—the Jerusalem which is in bondage with her children, and not the free Sarah. When Abraham begets children with Hagar, he must be understood externally, and Sarah internally, with whom he does not beget. Sarah says to Abraham: "May the wrong done to me be upon you," or, as the Targum <note>An ancient Aramaic translation and interpretation of the Hebrew Bible</note> says: "I have a judgment against you; I gave my handmaid into your bosom, and when she saw that she was pregnant, I was despised in her eyes; may Jehovah judge between me and you." This is an indication of how a judgment shall be held over the external Church and doctrine, and over the realm of external nature. Hitherto, the judgment, the violation, and the oppression fell upon the internal; but in the end, it falls upon the external. Therefore Sarah says, "May the wrong done to me be upon you,"—upon the external—"may Jehovah judge between me and you," that is, may Jesus Christ judge in His revelation between me and you. Of this judgment, which comes with the revelation of Christ at the end of the Church, the two Testaments had to represent the figure in the external world, such that one chastised and broke the other. The external Sarah of the Christian Church chastised the Church of the Old Testament as Hagar. This Hagar, however, fled from the external Sarah—as from the Church under the name of Jesus—and separated herself with the Old into the Turkish Church. The Christian Church wanted to be the free woman, but she was herself the bondwoman with her children, who is now being cast out. Each only despised the other, and they were altogether mockers. Furthermore, it is said of Hagar:

God wanted to hear and grant a hearing in the lowly state or in misery: for otherwise, no procreation would have been possible.

How could it be otherwise? The entire tree of the nature of the world and of the human race is the divine being itself, in its expanded powers, acting as limbs and branches. Within this natural tree is the inner spiritual tree, which seeks to emerge into manifestation out of and through the natural one. Therefore, He is always mindful of the preservation of the natural tree. The same is within His covenant, and He wishes to permeate and enliven it. # On the Nature of Ishmael and the External Church

himself and through the same he reveals; therefore he is called Ishmael, whom God has heard, and who is by no means cast out from His presence. However, the merely natural man is indeed rough and wild when compared to the inner man in the spirit, and such is also the external church.

In the church, Ishmael primarily represents the Turkish Church <note>The author uses "Turkish Church" as a contemporary 18th-century term for Islam or the Ottoman religious sphere.</note>. This is also the largest external church on earth, upon which the innumerable multitude of the seed of Hagar walks. Ishmael is a wild man. Such is the external nature of man and also the external church. His hand, or his wild nature, is in all—in all people and in all religions—and every man's hand is in him. Translators generally overlook that his hand should be *against* everyone <note>original: "gegen alle sey"</note>. One can certainly render it that way, for every religion is also against the others, but strictly speaking, it means *in* all. The Targum <note>A term for ancient Aramaic translations/paraphrases of the Hebrew Bible</note> says: he will be necessary to all, and men will also be necessary to him. The external man is necessary for the inner man, for without him the inner man could not come into being. The inner man is also necessary to the external, for without him the external man could not exist in blessing and rest. It is exactly the same with the two Testaments and their churches. 

The Christian church, with the name of Jesus and with the revelation of Christ, is necessary for all nations, for without it they could not be united together into one: because the line of the Covenant passes to the Christian church, and the spirit of revelation proceeds with the revelation of Christ in the Christian church. Conversely, the Christian church cannot exist without the Turkish church, nor without the Old <insert>Testament</insert>: because the revelation of Christ comes out of the true doctrine of the Old Testament, and because the Turkish church, which stands under the Old, possesses the largest part of the earth and must be the instrument in the hand of God to swiftly bring the revelation of Christ to the whole earth and to bring about the unification of all peoples. To this end, this church was externally led again as a mediator under the Old <insert>Covenant</insert>, as the Word of God of the middle dispensation <term>Haushaltung: a theological term for 'dispensation' or 'economy,' referring to how God manages different eras of human history</term>, yet it had to stand in the New, because everything shall come into one in the name of Jesus. Therefore, it carries the external figure of the Old within and alongside the New and belongs to the dispensation of the New; and is the true Ishmael, who dwells before the face of all his brothers. > 15. A. And Hagar bore Abram a son:

The 80 signifies the external, especially the beginning of the dispensation <note>German: Haushaltung; refers to the divine administration or "economy" of a specific era of salvation history</note> of the New Testament, since the external things of the preceding church were cut off on the 8th day, after the preceding church with its number 7 had come to an end. If one adds the 10 to 75, they make 85 years. The end of the 10 years, in the beginning of the world, is the time when the external propagation of mankind began, and thus the entire time until now is the 1 year—the 86th of Abraham—in which Hagar bore him Ishmael; and all people, in their external man, are the son of Hagar, and all religions are likewise. In the dispensation of the New Testament, however, the 10 years are the time until the beginning of the Turkish church <note>The Islamic faith</note>, and the entire duration of the Turkish church is the 1 year, or the 86th year, in which Hagar bore this Ishmael. Everything external was therefore Ishmael, and it was necessary. One was necessary to the other, and yet one was hostile to the other, for such is the kingdom of nature in its division. But Ishmael was not for that reason rejected by God, although he was included in the current time of corruption and excluded from the future time of life, as from the inheritance of Israel. Of this, the Turks and Christians were the external figure, as the Turks also had to be excluded from the name of the Christian Church. But they were not thereby excluded from the true Jesus Christ and from the inner covenant of God, any more than the Christian Church <insert>actually</insert> possessed the true Jesus Christ.

Every church had its Word of God as its water-well, and they were all in the desert with Hagar. The eternal well of all salvation lies within the essence of all people, but it remains locked until it is moved within us in Christ. The external nature is indeed as if rejected by God in this time, but God does not wish to have it pushed away from Him eternally. When He calls it back to Himself again, the poor, strayed, natural man will marvel at the great love of God and say: "Have I also looked here after Him who has looked upon me so graciously and watched over me, so that I too should come into His glorious kingdom on earth?" God must call us all and awaken us in the inner life, for this does not lie within nature's own ability; God must first gaze upon it, as He did with Hagar. Therefore, the merely natural man should not despair, even though he is still in the desert with Hagar, for God did not reject Hagar because of her wandering. Let him only commend himself to the merciful God in His compassion and recognize that he is wandering and not in his true fatherland; then God will surely bring him to the well when the little hour <note>German: "Stündlein," referring to the appointed time of grace or the moment of spiritual awakening</note> comes for him.

# The Realm of Nature and Universal Grace

When we look upon the realm of nature alongside external religions, the universal love of God for all people living in nature shines forth to us from these figures <note>The author uses "figures" here in the sense of "typology," where biblical characters represent larger spiritual truths or groups of people.</note>; and we see how He does not wish to reject nature nor cast away any nation. Sarah and Isaac, taken externally, represent the New Testament. Now Ishmael—representing the Turks <note>In early modern German texts, "Turks" was often a general term for Muslims.</note>—turned to the Father, as to the Old [Testament], when he sought the blessing, and not to Isaac, as to the Son or the New Testament. That is to say, they sought the inheritance in the Old and not in the New.

But now the Father is revealed in the Son; therefore they come to Jesus Christ through the Father, as through the Old Testament. And this is what it means when Christ says: *No one comes to me unless the Father draws him* <note>original: "Niemand kommt zu mir, der Vater ziehe ihn denn" (John 6:44)</note>. For even Christians do not come to the true Jesus Christ except through the Old Testament in its true sense. There is also no other God but Jesus Christ. Whoever worships a God, worships the same one, no matter what nation they may be. The Turks and Jews worship Him according to the teaching of the Old Testament, as in the Father; and the Christians worship Him according to the teaching of the New Testament; the Heathens <note>term: Heathens (Heyden). Used here to refer to non-Abrahamic peoples or those following natural religion.</note> do so according to their Word of God. 

It is all one: for Jesus Christ has written every Word of God, and they have all together not known the one whom they have worshiped and honored. Yet they have honored Him nonetheless, and how should their wandering with Hagar be able to harm them? The Father and Son—that is, the Old and New Testaments—are one, according to the inner true sense. The Christian Church is the external house of Abraham <note>original: "Abrams"</note> from which the Turks were cast out; but what does that old, leaky hut matter, which must fall down anyway, if only they enter into the inner house of Abraham? 

They are the figure of the Prodigal Son who wandered away from his father (Luke 15:11-32). But when the Angel calls them again with Hagar, they return to the Father. There will be great joy then: the true golden Jubilee Year of the Wedding of the Lamb. The elder brother who remained in the house—representing the Christian Church—will grumble and be dissatisfied on that account, but that will not disturb the joy. The external Christian Church is the son to whom the father said, "Do this," and he said "Yes," but went away and did it not. The Turks, however, are the other son who said "No," but did it anyway (Matthew 21:28-31). These figures indicate how only the evil self-will <note>term: self-will (Eigensinn). A central concept in German mysticism referring to the ego's prideful separation from God.</note>, which wants to be righteous in its own particularity and considers itself better than others, is rejected; but how God [the...]

## Chapter 17.
### Concerning the Covenant of Circumcision.

> 1. **A.** And Abram was 90 years and 9 years old <note>The author emphasizes the specific division of 99 into 90 and 9 to highlight numerical symbolism.</note>
> **n.** And Jehovah appeared to Abram and said to him: I am El Shaddai <note>original: "Elschadai"; a Hebrew title for God often translated as "God Almighty."</note>, walk before my face and be upright, or perfect.
> 2. **N.** And I will establish my covenant between me and you
> **a.** and will multiply you very, very much.

The number 9 is the concluding number and points to a transition from one state into another. In the beginning, it was the time when Abram was to pass into the previous era in order to multiply therein; but now, at the end, it is the time when multiplication shall have an end, and when procreation, together with the fleshly desire for it, shall be cut off. This is the mystery which is here included in this figure: namely, that at the end, when the blessing of Abram comes and his seed inherits the land, the procreation of the human race shall cease. Therefore, the covenant of circumcision is placed upon the male member, with which procreation occurs, to indicate that this matter shall be cut off when we enter into the great union with God. This happens in the old age of Abram, in the old age and conclusion of the previous time—which the 99 years of Abram indicate—whereupon he enters into a new time at 100. This is the rebirth and renewal

# The Transformation of Nature and the Covenant

...ration of the entire lower nature on earth, where the spiritual seed of Abram is to appear and truly begin. However, because the natural birth must first precede it, and the spiritual cannot occur without the natural, the natural birth is also included. The Covenant of Circumcision <note>Genesis 17</note> points, in this regard, to the beginning of the propagation of the human race. For this reason, this ceremony was placed at the beginning of a young boy's life, to signify the start of reproduction. A spiritual, eternal host of humanity was the ultimate goal of fleshly multiplication in this time; this purpose is marked with the sign of circumcision on the flesh at the very start of physical increase. This indicates that God, from the beginning, already looked toward the end—toward the eternal covenant—where the imperfection of this time, along with all imperfect and sinful things necessary for the work of reproduction, shall be cut away again. This is what the circumcision of the flesh represents as a figure and a sign.

The holy, eternal God looks upon eternal things and promises an innumerable, eternal seed to Abraham. But because this could not be obtained without the natural children of Adam, the external, natural propagation of the human race is also intended under this covenant of God with Abram. Thus, the covenant is twofold: external and internal. Even the external, natural man, according to his kind, is included in God’s covenant of grace.

Now, just as in the beginning of the world—when natural propagation was to start—a necessary "desolation" <note>The author uses "Verwüstung" (desolation/destruction) to describe a transformative clearing of the old state to make way for the new.</note> had to occur in nature on earth, whereby the inner land of blessing had to be laid waste; so too now at the end, when the promised land of blessing is to reappear with the seed of Abram's faith, a similar desolation must occur, wherein the external curse is laid waste in turn. Therefore, Jehovah appears here to Abram and says: **"I am El Shaddai"** <note>original: "Elschadai". The author interprets this Hebrew name as "The Strong God of Desolations" or "The Destroyer," likely linking it to the root *shadad* (to destroy/devastate) to emphasize God's power to clear away the old nature.</note>, which means, **"I am the strong God of desolations; walk before my face and be perfect,"** or, **"be upright."** **"And I will establish my covenant between me and you and will multiply you very, very much."** According to the external covenant, man was to live entirely, perfectly, and uprightly within the external law of nature so that the propagation and multiplication God desired could take place. In this external law, he was to live sincerely and without falsehood, as this word expresses. This could...

Abram means "an exalted father," and that is Adam within nature on earth, still in the initial state of perfection. In the Church, the symbolic figure of this matter is the transition from the dispensation of the Old Testament into the dispensation of the New Testament. There, Abram fell upon his face with the Old. The Old was brought low, and the exalted name of Jesus was set forth with the New in the dust of the literal teaching <note>original: "Buchstabenlehre"; refers to the outward, written word as opposed to the spirit</note>. The Old is high according to its inner meaning and reveals the true Jesus Christ. The New, however, is low and earthly in the letter—the Jerusalem that was in bondage with her children. To the name Abram, an ה or H is added, which letter in Hebrew counts as 5 in numerical value, just as it is the 5th letter in the Hebrew alphabet. The number 5, however, is the character of the Christian Church because of the name of Jesus <note>The author likely refers to the "Pentagrammaton," the five-letter version of the divine name (YHShVH) used in Christian Kabbalah</note>. Therefore, with the addition of this letter, the Church of the New Testament is indicated, as the name of Jesus also had to be spread and taught outwardly at first, just as Abraham first multiplied outwardly with the human race. Abraham is here outward in the multiplication of his seed, and Christ, who is Abram, is here the outward Christ.

From this outward Abraham and his seed, kings have indeed also come—both worldly and spiritual governments. The Targum <note>An Aramaic translation or interpretation of the Hebrew Bible</note> says: You shall become assemblies—meaning religions or church assemblies—and kings who rule over the peoples shall come from you.

All of this has come to pass just as God spoken, and it is the outward law of outward nature: for in the future, there shall be no kings in the New Covenant who rule over the nations. In this figure <note>The author uses "figure" here in the sense of a "typology"—a person or event in the Old Testament that foreshadows something in the New Testament.</note>, one should take careful note of how the Christian Church, through the course of time up to now in its state of corruption, always stands under the same image. One can see this very clearly here from the change of Abram’s name and the propagation of humanity connected with it: for Abraham is Christ, and the seed of Abraham is both outward and inward, natural and spiritual; therefore, Christ must also be so. # On Circumcision and the Four Generations

Circumcision is a sign of the covenant; but a sign points toward another matter. Circumcision points toward the cutting away of all external, corrupt, and vain things, both in doctrine and in the impulses and effects of nature. We shall first consider the matter in terms of doctrine, and the rest will follow naturally. Why should specifically the male sex be circumcised, and not the female? For this reason: because the man rules in the house and not the woman, and because this rule is an image that points to something else. In the Church, doctrine is that which rules, and that is the "masculine" element. When the Church or dispensation <note>The author uses "Haushaltung" (dispensation/economy) to describe specific eras of God's dealing with humanity.</note> of the Old Testament arrived, it had to cut away the external doctrines of the Church that existed under the doctrine of the gods <note>Referring to pagan or polytheistic beliefs.</note>, because they were in themselves corrupt and vain in the literal sense. This was the "foreskin" of their flesh. The "flesh" of the Church is its doctrine, and the "foreskin" is the preceding doctrine. The word "foreskin" also has the meaning of "clogging" or "stopping up." The preceding doctrine was also stopped up in the following one, and that is the circumcision or the cutting away.

Just as the Old Testament Church had to cut away the doctrine of the pagan church, so too the New Testament Church had to cut away the doctrine of the Old Testament Church; and the Turkish <note>Historically used here to refer to Islam or the Ottoman religious sphere.</note> Church had to do the same again with the Old against the Christian Church. This is the external figure in the entire Church of all nations, which indicates how it shall go with all external doctrines in the end. These are three external circumcisions or cuttings-away, and the current cutting-away of all external doctrines is the fourth, and that is the "fourth generation" which shall return to the land of blessing, of which we spoke above. 

The command for circumcision therefore stands here four times, namely in verses 10, 11, 12, and 13. In this 13th verse it says literally: *circumcising he shall be circumcised* <note>original: "beschneidende soll beschnitten werden" — a rendering of the Hebrew emphatic infinitive absolute construction.</note>, he who is born in your house and he who is bought with money. The doubled word "circumcise" also indicates a twofold matter, namely circumcising the one born in the house and the one bought with money. Those "born in the house" are those who are raised and born within any religion. At the end, these must all now be circumcised, and that is the true circumcision at the end. Those "bought with money," however, belong to the circumcisions of past times, when one dispensation or church was by the other...

<summary>This page provides a mystical interpretation of circumcision as a recurring historical process where new religious dispensations "cut away" the doctrines of their predecessors. It identifies four such stages, linking the final stage to the biblical "fourth generation" and a universal spiritual stripping of external forms.</summary>

...has been cut. For example, when the doctrine of the New Testament <note>original: "Neuen Test"</note> existed in the literal sense, it was the currency of the Christian church. With this doctrine, the church had to draw other nations into itself, and if they accepted the Christian religion, they had to allow their previous doctrines to be cut away. This was the spiritual commerce; for this reason, Christ calls those who merely teach the external letter "buyers and sellers" who had seated themselves in the temple of God. This, then, is the twofold law of circumcision, internal and external. Because the law is to circumcise only the male and not the female, it is obvious that there can be nothing essential in this act that would be useful or helpful for salvation; for how else should the female sex have been saved? It is, therefore, nothing other than a mere sign that points to something else, just as it is expressly called only a sign of the covenant. However, the Church of the Old Testament <note>original: "Alt. Test."</note> was primarily intended to have this sign, for the Old is the man, and the New is the woman. Therefore, because the Turks <note>A common 18th-century term for Muslims</note> primarily stand upon the Old, they also had to adopt circumcision. In this matter, the Old with its church is the figure in the entire church, which indicates that in the end, all external doctrines of all nations shall be cut away by the true internal doctrine of the Old Testament. The 8th day of circumcision had this meaning: every dispensation <term>Haushaltung: a theological period or "economy" of God's dealing with humanity</term> is indicated in its entirety by the number 7, because all things are included within the number 7. When one dispensation has followed another, that is an 8th day, on which the previous doctrine had to be cut away. In the same way, all three dispensations taken together are regarded as a number 7, and the Revelation of Christ, in which they all come to an end, is the 8th number, in which they all must be cut away. It is the same with the number 8 as it was above with the number 4, as the 4th generation which is to return. Here, we are not looking at the internal interpretation of the numbers according to their properties—for in that view, the numbers 4 and 8 refer to the external in conflict and division—but rather we are looking at the order of succession, where the 8 follows the number 7 of the entire external church, and the 4 follows the number 3 in the three dispensations. Whoever, in times past, did not cut away the doctrines of the preceding church when entering a subsequent one, was not tolerated in the following one,

From this state come all sinful and corrupt lusts, inclinations, and desires; and the circumcision of the flesh points to the cutting off of these same things. God’s Word testifies to this enough when it demands the circumcision of the heart, which is the spiritual rebirth or new birth.

This is a different birth than the fleshly birth, for in it the inner spiritual tree rises up within the tree of outward nature. This circumcision or rebirth is a work of the Spirit of God, which is everywhere in the nature of the world and can work within every human being, if they only allow Him space. In that time, the Spirit of God makes this circumcision of the heart perfect, and because of it, fleshly birthing, or the reproduction of mankind, comes to an end. The inner spiritual covenant was thus present in its own degree in the past time, but the one at the end is nonetheless distinct from it. The words of God also point to this: that He makes a covenant with Abraham and his seed after him, where something more is yet to follow. Just as essential things are depicted through figures in the Word of God, so too the Church presented itself with various religions as if in particular figures; the Old Testament depicted the Kingdom of Nature, and the New Testament depicted the Kingdom of Christ. Yet even this Kingdom of Christ was only the external figure. 

The Spirit of God in all of nature always looked toward the end and wished to move outward into the external realm. But the Spirit always presented figures through religions and ceremonies of that which is to happen at the end. With this handiwork, the Spirit proceeds with figuring until He breaks all external forms of religions and presents the Essence itself. The figures could not give life; only the Spirit within the figures could. When people turned to their inward selves during these figurative things, they obtained life from the Spirit within, and not from the figures, which existed only to awaken inward spiritual ideas and thoughts. 

But whoever honors only the external figures and ceremonies, participating in them without the inward life of the Spirit, honors dead idols—gods of stone and wood. So it goes now: everyone runs into the churches and temples, but no one thinks of the improvement of their heart; instead, this ceremony is supposed to be their divine service. The baptism of Christ is, in its true sense, entirely one with circumcision, for it is the washing away of sins. Therefore, Christ allowed himself to be circumcised as a Jew, and baptized as a Christian; however, the believers among all people were themselves this Christ. Paul was all things to all men <note>original: "allen allerley," a reference to 1 Corinthians 9:22</note>, that is, the Spirit of Christ was among all with teaching and admonishing toward the good, within every nation according to its own ceremonies. But if the baptism of Christ is set in opposition to circumcision as something different, then the Old Testament with circumcision is viewed as the figure <note>term: figure | a symbolic representation or "type" that foreshadows a spiritual reality</note> of everything external, and the New Testament with baptism as the figure of the internal new covenant which is now coming. Both, however, were only figures, and the external baptism of Christians is just as little use for salvation as circumcision. How should the external dead figures be able to help! Is it not also obvious enough that plenty of godless people are circumcised and baptized, and are they therefore children of God? Nor is it the case, as some children of God have taught, that believing Christians have an advantage over the believers of the Old Testament—namely, that they have "put on Christ in the flesh." The Christian church has no advantage, except only this: that the Spirit of revelation has entered into it, and goes out from it with the revelation of Christ. Christ had indeed appeared until now in the Spirit, within the believers, but not in the flesh. All passages in the New Testament which speak of this refer to the future time, when all sin is taken away from the flesh, and when a tabernacle of God will be among men on earth; for the beast in flesh and blood, with the animalistic mind, sense, and will, must first be removed.

Abraham and the entire external Church were circumcised. Thirteen years from that point reach until 1770, a year in which so many other symbolic images also reach their complete end. These thirteen years are viewed specifically as the "Day of Circumcision," which is why the words follow immediately after: *on that very day Abraham and his son Ishmael were circumcised.* This marks the end of the beautiful <term>Parashah Lech Lecha</term><note>The weekly Torah portion titled "Go Forth" (Genesis 12:1–17:27).</note>, "Go forth for yourself," namely concerning the departure of Abraham, which must be understood in both its beginning and its end: in the beginning, moving from the internal to the external in order to multiply naturally; and in the end, moving back from the external to the internal. Therefore, the seed of Abraham is twofold—natural and spiritual—and thus the Covenant and the Law are likewise twofold.

This departure of Abraham has been presented in this <term>Parashah</term> in various ways and through various figures, which in essence are always one and the same; however, in one figure this mystery is included, and in another figure that mystery is specifically emphasized, as can be seen from the explanation provided thus far. One should read through them with reflection and compare one figure against the other; then one will find something unique in each, a special impression and emphasis on this or that matter. Every <term>Parashah</term> is divided by the Hebrews into seven parts. In this portion, the first part is from Chapter 12, verses 1 to 14, in which Abraham’s entry into the land of Canaan is described, as well as his journey down into Egypt because of the famine. The second part, from there until Chapter 13, verse 5, contains the taking away of Abraham’s wife. The third part, from there until Chapter 14, verse 1, contains the separation of Lot and Abraham. The fourth part, from there until verse 21, contains the war at Sodom. The fifth part, from there until Chapter 15, verse 6, contains Abraham’s refusal to accept anything from the King of Sodom; his lament that he is without offspring; the promise of his seed; and his faith in the promise, which was his righteousness. The sixth part, from there until Chapter 17, verse 7, contains the signs of the four-hundred-year servitude of Abraham’s seed and the inheritance following it. The seventh part contains the Covenant of Circumcision, along with the changing of the names of Abram and Sarai. Circumcision, however, in its true sense, signifies the end of all these things. This was the content of the third <term>Parashah</term>.

Toward this end, God also moves in a threefold manner: In the eternal essence of His love; in the essence of His wrath, as in the satanic nature; and in the external nature upon earth—for the three men turn toward Sodom, and all of nature and creation must serve in the judgment over Sodom. Isaac is the revelation of Christ, and his birth is announced while the angels are on their way to destroy Sodom. The revelation of the entire divine being, and the secrets of His Word, thus proceed alongside the judgment; and at Mamre, in the third dispensation of the Church—the Christian Church—Abraham beholds this wonderful vision.

God has revealed Himself in the entire creation of the world in the number three, with the three primary natures of the world, which are: the nature of the fixed stars; the nature of the planets; and the lower nature, or the under-world. This under-world alone exists in three primary natures: namely, the nature above the firmament of clouds; the nature beneath this firmament; and the physical nature of the earth. The earth has three epochs with three primary changes: namely, Paradise in the beginning; the middle state; and the current time of the propagation of the human race in corruption. During this time, God has maintained His external Church in three dispensations <note>The author refers to the "Three Dispensations" or "Households" of sacred history: the Age of Nature (Adam to Moses), the Age of Law (Moses to Christ), and the Age of Grace (the Christian era).</note> with a threefold Word of God, wherein He came to Abraham and spoke with him. This number three of the divine essence in the nature of the macrocosm <note>original: "großen Welt"</note> is also within man, as in the microcosm <note>original: "kleinen Welt"</note>. Within the three external primary natures of the world is the eternal nature, the eternal essence of God. Through the willing of this one eternal God, the number three of the nature of the external world came to be, and thus three are named in a single essence, or a threefold essence of God is understood under the name God, as a Lord of all things. This number three of the divine essence has become humanized <note>original: "vermenschet"</note> in the propagation of the human race, or has made itself into man, and has become a man in every human being. Therefore, Abraham says to these three men as if to one: *Adonai* <note>Hebrew: "Lord"</note>, if I have found grace in your eyes, do not pass by your servant—that is, become a man in me as well. God is served and honored through His Word. In this way, the three men leaned under the tree! And in this way, Abraham served them! Whoever has served the poor children of God has served these three men, or God Himself; as Christ says, "Whatever you have done for one of the least of these my brothers, you have done for me" <note>Matthew 25:40</note>. Herein lies the blessing of Abraham, the blessing of all nations.

Abraham says: I will fetch a morsel of bread, that you may strengthen your heart, for this is why you have come to your servant. One should consider this entire conversation, both on the part of God and His Spirit, and on the part of His faithful children, who are the members of His own body. God and His Spirit are Abraham, and the believers are also Abraham <note>The author uses "Abraham" here as a mystical archetype for both the Provider (God) and the recipient of faith</note>. God gives to the believers in all three dispensations <note>original: "Haushaltungen"; referring to the three eras of salvation history: the Father/Law, the Son/Gospel, and the Spirit</note>, as to the three men, both physical and spiritual bread, so that they can strengthen their hearts; for He serves all, and has become, as it were, the servant of mankind in nature upon the earth. His Word is the food of our souls. When His Spirit lives in us, His Word also becomes alive in us, and that strengthens and refreshes our heart and mind. Abraham says the men should strengthen their heart with the morsel of bread. This already presupposes fatigue, lack of strength, and weakness. Such is the human heart and mind! It is often fearful, despondent, and faint-hearted, full of doubt and uncertainty. But when a happy and gracious hour comes, when a lovely wind of His Spirit blows upon our heart and mind, and in such a state one reads this or that in His Word, or also in other good books that point toward godliness; then that strengthens our heart and mind, and nothing therein is more powerful than His own Word. In this way, God Himself strengthens and refreshes the three men and serves them. But when the believers now serve God and refresh the men, this happens when they refresh and strengthen the poor members of Christ. Whoever does this and is able to do it, serves God in truth and in essence. This is what it means when Christ, who is God Himself, says: "You have done this unto me." What else could we do for God or how else could we serve Him? He has no need of us, but we have need of Him, that He may help us when we are in need and misery on this earth and in this corrupted time; and He does this in man and through man.

Many here ask where Sarah might be, and yet afterwards only one says that he wants to return. This indicates that Sarah—from whom the true Isaac is to be born—is not found within the "Multiplicity" <note>The author likely refers to the three visitors as a "multiplicity" or plurality</note>, and likewise the true Abraham was not therein either. In the words, *they spoke to him*, the Hebrew word <term>אליו (elav)</term> <note>original: "אליו"</note> has the letter <term>י (Yod)</term> specially punctuated, outside of the usual order; and if one removes the <term>ל (Lamed)</term> so that the <term>י (Yod)</term> follows immediately after the <term>א (Aleph)</term>, then it reads <term>איו (ayyo)</term> <note>original: "איו"</note> meaning "Where is he?" They spoke: "Where is he?" The true Abraham was just as little present as the true Sarah; that is, the true meaning of both Testaments <note>The Old and New Testaments</note> was lacking, even though every religion claimed that Sarah was within their own "religious-tent." The One was not present, and the many asked after the One. Therefore the One answers and says: I will certain—

I will certainly return to you at the time of life, and Sarah shall have a son. It says expressly, *after the time of life*, or *at the time of life*, but not, "if I shall live," as it is commonly and falsely translated. <note>The author is correcting a common contemporary translation of Genesis 18:10, arguing that "time of life" refers to a specific prophetic era rather than the speaker's own lifespan.</note> The future time is the time of life, when the holy God will return to us with His blessing; for until now, He was with Abraham and His blessing was not among us. But when He returns to us, then Isaac lives, and Sarah has her son. The external tabernacle of religion <note>original: "Religionshütte"</note> stands behind this time of life, for this present evil age is then cast behind. The external doctrine is the door of the tabernacle, through which one enters into the true, inner tabernacle. In this door, Sarah heard the speech that she should have a son. But she laughed in her heart—that is, she rejoices in her external things, which are her heart—and does not believe that another doctrine could yet be born from her, after she had become so old and had endured for so long.

In the very same way, it will seem ridiculous and impossible to the external man if one tells him now what shall shortly become of him, and that an old man shall become young again. That is incomprehensible, yet it shall nonetheless come to pass, for before God this is not too wonderful. It is also unbelievable and incomprehensible how this could be foretold, but before God nothing is covered or hidden, and He can reveal it when it pleases Him, and when the appointed time for it is here. Instead of the words *is anything too wonderful for Jehovah?*, the Targum <note>The Targum is an ancient Aramaic translation/paraphrase of the Hebrew Bible.</note> has: *is anything hidden from Him?* <note>original: "ist etwas vor ihm verdeckt?"</note> He thus presents a matter in His revelation which is wonderful, hidden, and separated from the eyes of the external church. 

Would anyone have believed that in the Revelation of Christ <note>likely referring to the Book of Revelation or the unfolding of Christian history</note> such a doctrine should yet come out of Sarah, as the external Christian church? Nevertheless, it has happened; therefore, the renewal of all nature on earth and of the whole man will also follow. This is proclaimed as the true birth of Isaac even before the destruction of Sodom, for from here the men go toward Sodom to destroy it. When the age of Abraham and Sarah is mentioned, and that it no longer went with her after the manner of women, it is thereby indicated that this birth will happen in the old age of time and at the end of the Christian church, and not according to the common way, or according to the external doctrine, wherein until now—

This is also what is meant when Habakkuk says in Chapter III, verse 2: "When you are angry, Jehovah, remember your mercy," <note>original: "Wenn du zürnest, Jehova, so gedenke an deine Barmherzigkeit"</note> that is, remember Abraham. Jehovah said: "How can I hide from Abraham what I am doing, since Abraham shall surely become a great and mighty nation, and in him all the nations of the earth shall be blessed?" For the outward multiplication of the seed of Abraham, the overturning of nature at the beginning of the world was necessary; and for the spiritual multiplication in the blessing at the end, the overturning of evil Sodom is necessary. 

Now, because the inhabitants of Sodom are also Abraham's natural seed, yet according to the justice of God <term>Gerechtigkeit Gottes: the divine standard of righteousness and legal standing before God</term> they cannot inherit the blessing of Abraham because they do not keep the commandments of God, nor do they practice law and justice; nevertheless, Abraham must command his children and his household after him—namely, after the outward Church—that they should comply with and do all such things, so that Jehovah may bring upon Abraham what He has spoken to him. 

Thus, in the end, Abraham must know these things. He must know that a separation shall be made between the wicked and the good, as well as between the outward letter <note>The "outward letter" refers to a literal or superficial understanding of scripture</note> and the inner true doctrine. He must know that the believers shall be preserved when the godless perish with Sodom, and that it is these believers upon whom shall come what God promised to Abraham. He must know what he should command them to do, and must present to them the true sense of the Word of God, so that the Word of God itself does not perish when the outward doctrine of the letter perishes with Sodom. For all these reasons God says: "How can I hide from Abraham what I am doing?" Abraham, with his seed, shall go out from Sodom and shall not remain therein. He shall remain in no outward doctrine, and he must know all of this when Sodom is to be destroyed, for otherwise he could not go out from it. Therefore, God does nothing unless He reveals it to His servants the prophets. Amos III. <note>Amos 3:7: "Surely the Sovereign Lord does nothing without revealing his plan to his servants the prophets."</note>

It is only one of these three men <note>A reference to the three visitors who appeared to Abraham at Mamre in Genesis 18</note> who says that he can hide nothing from Abraham. This one is the inner and eternal essence of the Godhead, for from that all revelation proceeds; and therefore the Revelation of Christ is also from the eternal God. In the sequence that follows, Abraham goes down as far as 10. Sodom is presented as 5 cities, and 5 times 10 is 50, which are the righteous in Sodom. Who then is righteous? No person on earth can call themselves righteous in their outer man; therefore, righteousness must be sought in the inner, sincere, and honest mind and will of the human being. Where the inner life dwells within a person, that person possesses the righteous essence within themselves. Furthermore, where a person is naturally honest and upright, they also possess the spark of righteousness and equity within them. Whoever has served the poor and the needy has done so unto Christ, and has served the three men alongside Abraham <note>A reference to Genesis 18, where Abraham hosts three divine visitors</note>; and this they have done out of the spark of righteousness that is within them. Regarding these righteous sparks—whether they be spiritual or merely natural—the spirit of Abraham prays that God might not destroy them along with the rest when He destroys Sodom, but rather that He would preserve all such people, and that He would bring forth righteousness perfectly in their entire being, in both soul and body. Accordingly, I also pray to the holy God with my whole heart, and say: do not break the bruised reed, and do not quench the smoldering wick <note>A biblical metaphor from Isaiah 42:3 and Matthew 12:20, signifying God's gentleness toward the weak</note>. Have mercy upon all such souls and preserve them! Preserve for me my poor brothers and sisters. Preserve all those who are related to me in the revelation You have given me! Preserve those who have supported me until now and have offered me assistance in Your own cause. Help all pious and honest people, and strengthen all who are weak! Far be it from You that You should kill all these along with the godless, or that they should be treated as the godless; should the Judge of all the earth not do what is right? <note>original: "sollte der Richter der ganzen Erde nicht recht thun?" - a direct quote of Abraham's plea in Genesis 18:25</note> Oh God, no, that cannot be! I cannot equate these poor souls with the godless, nor condemn them; therefore, You cannot either. For what does the tiny spark of mercy within me signify compared to Your great Being, which is Mercy itself, and from which my own spark of mercy has flowed? A person's mercy extends over their neighbor, but God's mercy extends over the whole world. Mercy stands against strict justice throughout all of nature and pleads for the poor, weak essence of humanity. This is Christ, or the Spirit of Christ in all nature and every creature, who holds back the wrath from the pious during judgment and snatches them away. Thus, a person prays for their own preservation by the power of this Spirit, and simultaneously for the preservation of their neighbor; for this Spirit demands that one should love their neighbor as oneself. In all believers there is only one single Spirit, within the many members of a single Body, where one member lends a helping hand to another, and one prays for the other, wishing them all salvation and goodness from God. This is the intercession of Christ for us, for it is the universal, single Spirit of God who works such things in everyone; and so this Spirit rescues all its members from the destruction of Sodom, and one member helps to save the other. Therefore, the place will not be forgiven where the 50 righteous are not found.

### 27. Answer. And Abraham answered and said:
> a. Behold now, I have taken it upon myself to speak with the Lord <note>original: "Adonai"</note>: I who am but dust and ashes.

### 28. Answer. Perhaps there are five lacking from the 50 righteous: would you destroy the whole city for the sake of those five?
> n. And he said, I will not destroy it: if I find there: 40 and 5.

This refers to the entire era of the external church, in which Abraham was dust and ashes. The 5 righteous ones are the inner church of believers within the external church, which is signified by the number 40. The number 4, however, points to the four main religions <note>Likely referring to the four major world religions or confessions known to the author's era, often categorized as Jewish, Christian, Mahometan (Muslim), and Heathen.</note>, and because the 5—as the inner church of believers—were found alongside the number 40—as the external church—God did not want to destroy them, as long as the sin of Sodom had not yet come to its full measure. The 5 were lacking from the number 50; that is, the inner church had not yet reached the Jubilee Year <term>Jubilee Year: In the Bible, the 50th year of liberation and restoration; here used mystically to represent spiritual perfection or the final age of the Holy Spirit</term>, represented by the number 50. But for that reason, it should not be destroyed, and the external judgment should not yet come, because the time for it was not yet there. Abraham, acting within the believers, had taken it upon himself to speak with God amidst external matters of faith; this was well-pleasing to God, and He was satisfied with it and bore the imperfection with divine patience.

as the believers in the church under the two Testaments, for whose sake it [the church] has been sustained. In the entire outward church, the believers taken together make the number ten, for they are all one, and for their sake God has endured all outward things with patience. They have formed the will to speak with God amidst outward things, and that was also God’s will in outward nature; therefore the name *Adonai* <note>original: "Adonai"; a Hebrew title for God meaning "Lord," often used in the context of His sovereignty over the physical world</note> stands throughout this entire discourse. *Adonai*, however, indicates the inner divine essence within an outward natural essence. So long as God spoke in such an outward manner from an outward teaching of His word with the "Abraham" of the believers, the "Sodom" of the outward church was not destroyed. But now *Jehovah* <note>original: "Jehova"; the personal name of God, here signifying a transition to a more direct divine action</note> departs from the outward teaching and has finished speaking with Abraham in this way; and Abraham, as Jesus Christ, departs from the literal teaching of the New Testament to his own place, into the true teaching of the Old Testament. Therefore, the destruction of Sodom is now inevitable and must occur, as the description of it follows in the next chapter.

This intercession of Abraham, from fifty down to ten, is thus a pure "doctrine of numbers" <term>Zahlenlehre: the study of the spiritual or mystical significance of numbers in scripture</term>, which is nevertheless full of edification and soul-saving lessons. The entire church in all its parts is contained within it, as anyone can easily see: for why else would Abraham bargain with God using such numbers? The destruction of Sodom is primarily found within the four religions of the Christians. The destruction of the church has its own appointed time, as does the destruction in nature, which follows at the end and, according to other figures, lasts forty days. Before this destruction, God has sealed His own, for Lot is taken by the hand by the angels before the wrath is kindled. From the number forty onwards, Abraham also goes back by tens, just as the believers go back through these forty days into the original blessing of the world. When the end comes, we shall see more clearly into all these things.

1. A. And the two angels came to Sodom in the evening, and Lot sat in the gate of Sodom.

n. And Lot saw them, and rose up to meet them; and he bowed his face toward the ground.
2. N. And he said, "Behold now, my lords, please turn aside into your servant's house, and stay the night, and wash your feet; and then rise up early and go your way."
a. And they said, "No; for we will stay in the street overnight."
3. A. And he pressed them greatly; and they turned aside to him, and entered into his house.
n. And he made them a feast, and baked unleavened cakes, and they ate.

Two angels are named here who come to Sodom. In the <term>Doctrine</term><note>The author uses "Doctrine" to refer to the inner, spiritual meaning of the Bible</note> they are the two Testaments <note>The Old and New Testaments</note>, and they must be understood both internally and externally, at the end and in the beginning. The evening is the end of the Church, when they enter the Church in their inner, true sense. However, they do not wish to enter Lot's house here—into the literal doctrine of the Christian Church—but rather they wish to remain in the street, just as the two witnesses of the Revelation of Christ <note>A reference to the "two witnesses" in Revelation 11:8</note> remained lying as if dead and ineffective in the street of the great city. In the beginning, however, the evening is the start of the Church of the New Testament, which is likewise called the evening. There, Lot compelled them to enter his house, as if into the external doctrine, for he sat in the gate of Sodom, in the literal doctrine of the Christian Church. One saw the two Testaments, one stood up to meet them, and bowed before them as before God's Word and Will.

The name, "my lords," is <note>original: "אדני"</note> Adonai, as seen above in the vision of the three men; but here at the end there is only the <term>Patach</term><note>A Hebrew vowel mark representing a short 'a'</note> instead of the <term>Qamets</term><note>A Hebrew vowel mark representing a long 'a'</note>. The Patach in this name indicates something common, as in this form it is also applied to men; whereas the same name with the Qamets is always the name of God. This indicates, therefore, that Lot, representing the Church, viewed the two Testaments as something external, as external histories. Therefore he also says they should "turn aside" to him—namely, out of the inner doctrine into the external.

### The Doctrine of Letters
The washing of the feet <note>referring to the ritual performed by Jesus, here interpreted as a spiritual cleansing</note> is, in this context, the washing away of all preceding doctrines and ceremonies. This is also the meal of Lot and the baking of unleavened cakes; for that was a Passover <note>original: "Pascha"</note>, a transition, where the leaven of all previous external teachings had to be swept out. Now, at the end, it is the washing away of all external teachings through the Revelation of Christ, and the inner, true doctrine is the unleavened cake. Eating is the taking of a thing into oneself. The divine essence takes the creaturely essence and the will of man into itself, and the human creaturely essence takes the divine essence into itself. This happens to a believer when he directs his will, heart, mind, and soul toward God through the Word of God; and that is the "food of angels," where the divine essence and the human essence communicate with one another. Through this, Lot is preserved and led out during the judgment of Sodom. The two angels also signify God's Truth and God's Justice. Through the essence of the holy God, as through Truth, the sick and circumcised Abraham is healed, and Lot is rescued. Through Justice, however, Sodom is destroyed and the godless are judged. Even the satanic nature itself belongs to this angel <note>referring to the instrument of judgment</note>, for all of nature and every creature must serve in this judgment.

The fact that the two angels had not yet lain down indicates that the teaching of the Word of God was not yet at rest, for in the external church everything was in turmoil regarding doctrine. The men of Sodom—the external, quarrelsome teachers—surrounded the house (representing the Word of God) and wanted to possess the inner true sense of the two Testaments <note>The Old and New Testaments</note> within the literal letter. The "doctrine of the letter" <term>Buchstabenlehre: a literalist interpretation of scripture that ignores spiritual depth</term> was intended to be the true sense, and they quarreled over it; however, the two Testaments were closed off according to their inner meaning. Lot, acting as the Spirit, spoke to them from the doorway in the literal sense, but they would not and could not understand what he said, for he closed the door to the true meaning behind him. The believers, who possessed their measure of the Spirit according to the inner teaching, were then also represented by Lot. Whenever such a person spoke out against the "Sodomite nature" of the external church during a dispute, the immediate response was: "Are you and your teaching not a stranger among us?"

and yet you dare to judge us? Thus has the external teaching office of the Christian Church acted against the pious children of God among them: those to whom God had given some light and knowledge, they have scolded as <term>heretics</term> and <term>errant spirits</term>, and persecuted most cruelly, as the entire history of the church from the beginning until now testifies.

When Lot wished to have the two angels protected, and yet gave out his two daughters to them to do with as they pleased; this signifies how the Spirit has kept the true inner meaning of the <term>Two Testaments</term><note>The Old and New Testaments of the Bible</note> hidden from such "beast-men," and has given into their power and understanding only the dead letter. With this letter, throughout the entire night of the external church-age, they have committed all manner of shameful deeds and abominations with spiritual whoredoms, and have instigated murder and bloodshed over matters of faith. They were struck with blindness, so that they could not find the door to the true meaning of the Word of God; therefore they raged so before the door in the "letter" <note>The literal, non-spiritual interpretation of scripture</note>. They finally grew weary of trying to find the door, which is why so many <term>freethinkers</term><note>Those who reject traditional religious authority or dogma</note> have arisen in the Christian Church, who no longer hold to God’s Word at all and cease from seeking the door. All of this has continued until now, at the end, the <term>men of Mauzzim</term><note>A reference to Daniel 11:38; often interpreted as followers of a false god of forces or worldly power</note> come and break down the door, representing the Two Testaments. During this breaking, however, Lot—representing the inner church of the faithful—is drawn into the true inner meaning of the Two Testaments: for tribulation teaches one to pay heed to the Word.

The Church received the Two Testaments, as the two daughters of Lot, in her beginning as virgins, when they had not yet known any <term>priest-man</term><note>original: "Pfaffen-Mann"; a derogatory term for worldly or corrupt clergy</note>; but the priests have defiled them and dragged them into all kinds of quarrelsome opinions, in which they have fathered their "church-children," who then also became blind. Thus the whole city of Sodom was struck with blindness, from the small to the great. They were unruly beasts in their quarreling; therefore only the dead letter was given into their power, and the true inner meaning was kept locked away from them until the judgment upon Sodom.

The Dawn is ultimately the Revelation of Christ, the light of the Messiah, the Sun of Righteousness, which rises for the believers, according to Malachi 4 <note>original: "Mal. IV"; refers to Malachi 4:2, "But unto you that fear my name shall the Sun of righteousness arise with healing in his wings."</note>. Everything rises at once for the Judgment. The godless pay no heed to the preaching of Lot, who has preached to them for a long time, both during the era of the external Church and during the Revelation of Christ. Because the Judgment did not arrive in its full essence immediately, it seems to them as if the sun has now risen in Sodom specifically for their benefit. Thus, when the Sun of Mauzzim has risen, Lot must truly hurry, because then the punishment and destruction are at hand. The angels press upon Lot, urging him to hurry out quickly. The hand of the Lord, as the power of God, will seize the faithful Lot in the essence of his soul. He will take his children into His love, protect them, and lead them out of destruction. The doctrine of the Revelation of Christ, however, is the external means, or the hand, with which He seizes them and leads them out.

In the beginning of the Christian Church, however, Lot was led out of the household of the Old Testament through the doctrine of the New Testament. At that time, Jehovah still spared Lot’s wife—representing the external Church and the doctrine of the Christians—and placed her with the two daughters, or the two Testaments, outside the city of the Old Testament Church; here, the angels are also not called angels, but merely men. This leading out is therefore not the same as the current leading out at the end of time, for then God does not spare Lot’s wife, but she becomes a pillar of salt. This was the leading out from Egypt, when God took His people by the hand and led them like little children in the external doctrine of the letter—as if by the hand—from a preceding Church into the following one. This is what is meant here by the men seizing them by the hand.

18. Narrative. **And Lot said to them:**

20. Narrative. **"Behold now, this city which is near, to flee there, and it is small:"**

Only one speaks here, the one who led them out and who said they should save themselves and not look behind them in the entire region, and that they should escape to the mountain. This "one" is the Spirit of God in the Revelation of Christ <note>original: "Offenbar. Chr." (Offenbarung Christi)</note>, who commands that the faithful in all three dispensations <term>dispensations: historical periods of God's dealings with mankind, often divided into the eras of Nature, Law, and Grace</term> should rescue themselves out of the outward letter of the doctrine, and should not look back at any place into the externals of the Church. They are to escape to the mountain; the mountain, however, is primarily the Old Testament <note>original: "Alt. Test."</note>. The Revelation of Christ, which comes out of the true doctrine of the Old Testament, is the mountain upon which the Lord’s house shall stand in the last days. To this Mount Zion they should escape so that they do not perish.

However, because the outward transition from one dispensation into another is also included in this; so Lot—representing the believers of the New Testament <note>original: "Neuen Test."</note> Church—now accords with God and says he cannot save himself upon the mountain (meaning the doctrine of the Old Testament), lest an accident befall him, because he sought his salvation in the name of Jesus. Therefore he said: "Behold this city, this doctrine of the New Testament in which the name of Jesus is found!" This is near to me, that I may escape there, and it is small; it is the outward Zoar <note>Zoar was the small city Lot fled to; the author interprets it here as the accessible, outward form of New Testament teaching.</note>. Here he also speaks to "them" as many, and not just to the "one."

He Himself works this hurrying within them. He seizes them hastily in His power to tear them out, and so Love steps forward in them against Wrath, and Mercy triumphs over Judgment. This does not mean that the faithful could delay God's judgment—no, for the judgment has its goal, which can be moved neither forward nor backward; it only indicates how God, on His part, hurries to save His own.

Therefore, Lot must first be in Zoar—in the doctrine of the Revelation of Christ—before the true breaking-apart comes. That is, people must already be in this doctrine, even if they are few in number, which does not change the matter and does not exclude other believers to whom the Revelation of Christ does not come before this destruction. The Zoar to which he went was the place where Jerusalem is. Zoar and Jerusalem therefore stand in the figure of the Christian Church. When the sun rose upon the earth, Lot entered into Zoar. When the sun of the outward doctrine of Jesus Christ rose upon the earth, the "Lot" of the faithful entered into the outward Christian Church, and it rained sulfur and fire over the doctrines and ceremonies of the Jews and Heathens, all of which were overturned. It says: *Jehovah caused sulfur and fire to rain, from Jehovah out of heaven.* <note>Genesis 19:24</note> These words, where one Jehovah causes it to rain from another, are meant to say something significant. The outward Christ in the "letter" <note>The literal or external word of scripture.</note> thundered and hailed in the name of the true Jesus Christ over everything that would not profess Christ and believe in Him; but that was only the outward figure of what shall happen at the end.
