<meta>This page continues the discussion of the spiritual and physical transformation of nature during the final judgment, focusing on the alchemical properties of the elements as instruments of divine justice.</meta>

The elements are the first three properties or stages of the birth of nature, and these are ignited in wrath, whereby the whole of nature and every creature arms itself for vengeance against the godless. This fire is also the sword of vengeance in this judgment, through which God will avenge Himself upon His enemies and redeem Zion; it belongs primarily to the forty days at the end. This is the **Great Turba** <note>original: "Turba magna"; a term used by Jacob Böhme to describe the spirit of destruction or the "commotion" that breaks forms apart</note>, the great shattering in the soul of the macrocosm, in the spirit of nature, from which external nature catches fire. It takes on a physical form according to the property of poisonous Mercury <note>In alchemical thought, Mercury represents the volatile, bitter, and communicative principle</note> from the harsh counter-sting of the element of Air, and according to the property of Sulfur from the element of Fire, which is awakened by the first two.

This will create an anxious state of being in all life upon earth. The harsh Salt-quality will seize the greedy and the unmerciful, the cruel and their like. The stinging Mercurial property of the Air will seize the envious and the hostile, the quarrelsome and the brawlers, the hateful and their like; and the Sulfurous property of Fire seizes the angry, the proud, and the haughty, who so gladly exalt themselves over others and ride over the poor and the lowly—for all these people are made of such properties in their own natural essence.

Therefore, when all of nature moves toward judgment, it is easy to understand how the godless can be seized—like <term>Lot's Wife</term> <note>Referring to the biblical figure who turned into a pillar of salt; here used to explain a literal alchemical transformation of the body</note>—in the cursed Salt, Mercurial, and Sulfurous essence, and can remain standing with their bodies as an abomination to all flesh. This, then, is the matter of this fire of Sodom; but the actual fire of judgment, which is the satanic nature itself, consumes earth and stones. This movement occurs only at the end of the universal Church on earth, when all the coarseness of the earth and of human bodies is consumed by this fire; then the earth, together with the bodies of men (who will pass through this fire without feeling it), will appear in crystalline clarity.

Thus, everything must pass through the fire and be tested, yet one thing is not the same as the other. The faithful <term>Lot</term> <note>The biblical patriarch who fled Sodom; represents the righteous soul</note> must also pass through the fire of Sodom, and there is no <term>Zoar</term> <note>The small town where Lot found refuge; here representing a place of safety or a specific state of grace</note> where he is entirely free from it, but God leads him out of this overturning and redeems Zion. The Zoar into which he enters before the overturning is only the doctrine of the Revelation of Christ in regard to the few who enter it beforehand, and the inner life in regard to all believers.

That this is no literal history <note>original: "buchstäbliche Historie"</note> is obvious. Furthermore, it is entirely impossible in nature for a man to be able to sleep with and impregnate a woman if he were so drunk that he was not aware when a woman lay down with him and stood up again, and consequently did not know at all that a woman had been with him. This matter occurs through the highest sensation of the external human nature, and body and soul must act therein to the utmost, without which no reproduction <term>Fortpflanzung: the biological and spiritual propagation of life</term> of body and soul could happen; therefore it is not possible that a completely drunken and senseless person could perform such a work.

# The Mystery of Lot and the Two Testaments

This is a formation within nature, and the free-thinker <note>German: *Freygeist*; a term used in the 18th century for skeptics or deists who rejected revelation</note> should not look upon this history of Lot with "calf-eyes" <note>an idiom for staring blankly or foolishly</note> as if it were something silly. Great and hidden wisdom lies within it; but because the free-thinker is blind and drunk, he does not see it. Thus, he proves by his own actions that this history of Lot is truth, because he himself does not know what he does and does not understand what he says. Furthermore, the entire Church testifies that she has been blind and drunk, in that she has been able to accept and believe these things according to the letter as a literal history, even though they run against all natural possibility. This also proves that God Himself has arranged it so. All of this stands before the eyes of the whole world, and no one can deny it; therefore, this matter of Lot is a most true matter, even if it is not true according to the literal sense.

Because this matter has been covered up—and for the reasons already stated, had to remain covered—but is now being uncovered, this is also a certain sign that the end of this age and the propagation of the human race is at hand. The night of Lot’s drunkenness is coming to an end, and it is becoming day, and Lot is waking up.

The eldest daughter of Lot bore a son, whom she named Moab, which means "from the father" <note>original: *Moab*, from the Hebrew *Me-ab*</note>. In the Church, this represents the Old Testament with its church, for the Old Testament is also called "the Father." The son of the second daughter is named Ben-Ammi, which means "the son of my people." This is the New Testament with its church, which is also called "the Son." Because of the name of Jesus, this church is called the People of God, and this name "People" in Hebrew is *Am* <insert>עם</insert>. 

Otherwise, the name for "people" is also *Goi* <insert>גוי</insert>, or *Goyim*, the nations, which is commonly translated as "Heathens" <note>or Gentiles</note>, and these are the nations outside of the Christian Church. The Christians, however, are only the *Am* in an allegorical sense—in the significance or the outward figure—and not in the true essence. But the inner Church of believers, which is scattered among all nations, is the true *Am* or People of God. 

Now, because the Jews, with their dispersion, are the image of this in outward nature, they are also the outward, natural *Am* or People of God; and all other nations, taken merely outwardly against them, are to be regarded as the *Goyim* or Heathens. This is therefore in respect to the Old Testament, to which the Jews cling quite alone and very firmly before all other nations: for the Old Testament is the main foundation—

If these two sons of Lot are taken in the sense of Nature, then Moab is the soulful being and Ammon is the bodily being. From this, the entire human race was generated, and that was the drunkenness of Lot throughout all of Nature on Earth. In the Church, however, these two sons or two peoples belong under the two Testaments. Entire nations are thus indicated under such names, and not single persons. See, therefore, how God’s Word unlocks itself in its true sense, as one place explains the other, just as God reveals Himself and makes the diversity of His manifold being manifest through various effects; for one perceives from Wrath that He is not Love, and that Evil is not Good. The Good is God, and the Evil is the Devil. Each will now reveal and present itself in its highest power, and so the world in judgment will have to recognize through feeling what is God or Devil, what is Truth and Lies, Right or Injustice. Here, the scholars *de belles lettres* <note>original: "de belles lettres," meaning "of fine letters" or polite literature; the author uses this to critique those who study only the surface of texts</note>, that is, of the beautiful letters, will lose their breath and languish, for they do not know where or what He is, where Heaven or Hell may be, nor what they themselves are, and they likely even doubt in part whether they are eternal beings or not. In their "beautiful sciences"—that is, in their beautiful letters—they have nothing; therefore they perish with *Kirath Sepher* <note>original: "Kirath Sepher," a Hebrew place name meaning "City of Books"</note>, that is, with the City of Books. For *Othniel* <note>original: "Athniel"</note>, which means "my time is the strong God," wins and overcomes this city, and receives in return the daughter of Caleb as the true teaching for a wife: for it is Caleb alone who, with Joshua, enters the land, and the other book-merchants or "letter-peddlers" must all remain behind with their teachings.

<summary>The author provides an allegorical interpretation of Lot's descendants as symbols of the soul and body, arguing that true spiritual understanding comes from divine revelation rather than the superficial "beautiful letters" of worldly scholars.</summary>

## Chapter 20.

### Abraham travels toward the South to King Abimelech at Gerar, and once again presents his wife Sarah as his sister.

-> <note>The Hebrew letter Pe (פ) marks a "Petuchah" or open paragraph break in the Torah.</note> <-

1. **A.** And Abraham moved away from there into the land toward the South: and dwelt between Kadesh and Shur, and was a stranger in Gerar.
2. **N.** And Abraham said of his wife Sarah, she is my sister—
**a.** then Abimelech the king of Gerar sent for her: and had Sarah taken away.
3. **A.** And Elohim <note>original: "Elohim" - a Hebrew name for God emphasizing His role as Judge or Creator</note> came to Abimelech in a dream by night—
**n.** and said to him, behold, you are a dead man because of the woman whom you have taken, for she is a man's wife.

Abraham moved away from the place of Sodom into the land toward the south, where he dwelt as a stranger with Abimelech at Gerar. Sodom is primarily the Christian Church, and the destruction of every preceding church and doctrine by a succeeding one is also a destruction of Sodom. Because Abraham had to dwell here as a stranger after the destruction of Sodom, this is not the current destruction of Sodom at the end of time, for with such an event all pilgrimage on earth also comes to an end. Therefore, one must seek this matter in a change within the external church. With the beginning of the Church of the New Testament, the doctrine and Church of the Old Testament were destroyed, as God led Lot out of that very Sodom. In the Christian Church of the New Testament, Lot was then made quite drunk by the wine of the two daughters—representing the literal doctrines <note>original: "Buchstabenlehren"; literally "letter-teachings" or a strictly literal interpretation of scripture</note> of the two Testaments. With the beginning of the Turkish Church <note>The author uses "Turkish Church" to refer to Islam or the Ottoman religious sphere</note>, this Sodom of the Christians in the Orient <note>original: "Morgenland"; the East</note> was destroyed again, and there Abraham moved with the Old Testament into the Turkish Church. This is here "toward the south." He dwelt between Kadesh and Shur, which means between holiness and enmity. The external religions were hostile toward one another, and yet were regarded as holy things. Among all these, Abraham dwelt as the Spirit of God with His Word, and as the church of the believers, as if in a foreign land where the seed of Abraham had to be subservient. Gerar means "to cut off." The Turkish Church cut

The Old Testament is called the Father, and this was the King here in this Church, and it governed; and Abraham, as the Spirit of Faith, also walked within it. Now, because the Old Testament also stands as a symbol of everything external, and because the doctrine of every household and Church is also its King; so too, under Abimelech, all the external forms of all religions are simultaneously indicated. Abraham is the Inner Being, and the Spirit of God within humanity and within the Word of God. He wandered about the whole earth, from one religion to another, and from one people to another, and thus Abraham's travels indicate and depict the pilgrimage of the children of God on earth. Christ had to wander from one place to another, and nowhere was the true divine essence publicly tolerated in its making and doing, working and willing. The Lord of all things had to be a servant, and had to, as it were, hide and conceal Himself from the government of external nature, just as Abraham had to hide and conceal his wife. His children had to live in fear, and as if unstable and fleeting. Nowhere were they allowed to lift their heads high, but rather had to fear like Cain, that whoever found them would strike them dead, just as Abraham was in fear that they would strike him dead for the sake of Sarah, who represents the Inner Being. The Spirit of God thus had to bow beneath external things within the believers, and had to place itself on a level with the external; for this reason, the Word of God was written and described as external histories, and divine service as external actions with external matters of faith. Hereby the external spirit in man, with its external concepts, gained the upper hand, and the inner spirit with the true understanding and true sense of the Word of God had to lie hidden under this cover. He spoke from God's Word under the images of persons, but these persons were taken for external men, and consequently their teaching for a sister of such a person—that is, for teachings which one must understand according to the letter <term>the letter: the literal or surface-level meaning of scripture as opposed to its spiritual essence</term>. This is why Abraham says of his wife that she is his sister, and why she says he is her brother. The two Testaments are also like sister and brother in the letter, and just as the Old Testament was accepted as external, so too the New Testament <note>original: "Neue Test."</note>, as the sister, was intended to be an external history.

Above, Abraham’s wife was taken from him by Pharaoh in Egypt, and here it happened once more with Abimelech. In a general sense, both are one and the same thing. However, if both histories are held as distinct from one another, and the king did not touch the woman; then Pharaoh represents the Christian Church and its teaching office. This church took Abraham’s wife—as the Old Testament—to itself, but did not touch it <note>i.e., did not physically "defile" or fully adopt the literal practices</note> in its ceremonies.

Abimelech, however, is here the Turkish Church <note>The author uses "Turkish Church" to refer to Islam/the Ottoman religious structure</note> with its teaching office. This also took the New Testament away as Abraham’s wife, but also did not touch it in its external ceremonies after the manner of the Christians. All of this happened at night in a dream, and at night in a dream Elohim <note>original: "Elohim" — a Hebrew name for God often associated with His role as Creator or Judge in nature</note> commanded Abimelech that he should give Abraham’s wife back again. That is to say: in the external doctrine of the letter—which was only a dream in the night of the external church—the Turks received the command to give the New Testament with its ceremonies (as Sarah) back again, and to live according to the teaching of the Old Testament. The Christian Church, however, received in just such dreams or literal doctrines the command to give the Old Testament with its ceremonies back again, and to live according to the teaching of the New Testament. This was the external law in external nature; therefore, Elohim appeared to Abimelech in a dream, and not Jehovah <note>original: "Jehova" — the author distinguishes between the universal God of nature (Elohim) and the personal, revealed God of the covenant (Jehovah)</note>. This was the external exchange, and the journey of Abraham from one church into the other, and Sarah is here taken as Abraham’s sister and in an external sense. That this is the true meaning is also clear from the fact that God says to Abimelech that He prevented him so that he did not touch Sarah and sin against Him. One does not sin against the *inner* Sarah if one touches her, but one sins against the *external* <insert>Sarah</insert>, because everything external lies under sin. Now, because the external elements of a preceding church were to be abolished by the succeeding church and doctrine, the succeeding one had to act and do according to its Word of God which it had accepted. Had it not abandoned the preceding one, it would have been "of death" as a succeeding church, or could not have been a succeeding church at all. Yet it was God’s ordinance in nature that both the Christians and Turks should accept both Testaments, although each people was primarily to live according to only one Testament in external ceremonies, and was not to approach the other.

Men would not have been able to accept God’s Word if it had not been written in such a form of external histories. The Scripture says, God made all things through His Word. Through His essential Word He made all essential things, and through His written Word, He made the Church. Now God’s Word consists of images, which depict what was to become of the Church. Through these images, written already at the beginning of the world, He formed the Church, and in the forming of the Church itself, both externally and internally, the images are first fulfilled. Now the images contain histories within themselves, which describe and show the wonders of God, His government over all things, His protection over the pious, and His punishment over the ungodly, the redemption of the godly, and the downfall of the ungodly, and indeed as things already happened and passed away. This had to incite men to reverence toward God and His Word. How could such a thing have been possible if God’s Word, in many things, had not been written as things already passed away? From the beginning, mankind had to accept God’s Word; but what could have been written therein concerning God’s help in distress, His redemption, His punishment of the ungodly, and a thousand other things—which they could have accepted as an example and for the strengthening of their faith—since nothing of the sort had yet happened? They had not yet been rescued from distress, but were only just entering into it; and in the midst of distress, it is difficult to believe when one sees no examples before them. Therefore, God’s Word was written in many respects as things that had already happened, even though they were yet to occur. Much was written concerning the very first Adam and his wife and children, and likewise with the subsequent Fathers <note>The "Fathers" refers to the Old Testament Patriarchs like Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob</note>, so that from the beginning, people could always see before them examples of piety and ungodliness, of God’s grace or help, and of His punishment—which things, however, were mostly intended for the subsequent generations themselves.

Through this and in this manner, mankind could accept God’s Word with trust and confidence, which otherwise could not have happened so easily. If one considers all this well, one will see that Abraham had cause enough not to tell the entire truth and to pass his wife off as his sister; that is, it was necessary that Abraham, as the Word of God, spoke thus in the letter <note>The "letter" refers to the literal or outward meaning of the text as opposed to the spirit</note> as if such things were meant regarding external matters, persons, and the like. Without this, God’s Word would not have been accepted as divine, for in the external government of nature there is no regard for the inner eternal essence, and in that case, Abraham would have been strangled <note>i.e., the spiritual meaning would have been suppressed or destroyed by the worldly mind</note>. But in this way, everything was made appropriate according to the outward appearance, and the Inner could pass through secretly within the Outward.

Abraham says of Sarah: **"In truth she is also my sister, the daughter of my father, but not the daughter of my mother."** Who could understand this if God did not open the understanding? Abraham’s father was Terah <note>original: "Thara"</note>, but who his mother was is not stated. Terah begat three sons: Abraham, Nahor, and Haran. These three are the <term>triple Word of God</term> <note>A mystical interpretation where the family members represent different stages of divine revelation</note> in the three <term>dispensations</term> <note>original: "Haushaltungen"; refers to the different eras of God's dealings with humanity, such as the Age of Law or the Age of Grace</note>. Nahor and Haran are the Old and New Testaments. Haran begat Lot; but Haran is primarily the New Testament, therefore Lot also belongs to the New Testament, in the Christian Church.

The New Testament teaches of Jesus Christ, yet in the literal letter <note>The "letter" refers to the surface-level reading of the text as opposed to its spiritual essence.</note> that is not yet the true Jesus Christ, who is revealed and recognized through the Old Testament. For this reason, the Turks <note>In this 18th-century context, "Turks" often served as a general term for non-Christian or Islamic peoples who maintained a connection to Old Testament traditions.</note> had to return to the Old Testament and leave the Christian Church.

Through this, both Testaments were preserved as brother and sister, and that was good for the time of Abraham's wandering, for thereby he was secretly preserved, and not one word of God perished. The *Targum Onkelos* <note>An ancient Aramaic translation and commentary on the Hebrew Torah.</note> renders the aforementioned words thus: "when the nations wandered after the works of their hands"—namely in their external religious matters—"then God let me draw near from my father's house to His service," namely to external forms of worship. Then I spoke to her, to Sarah: "this is your kindness which you shall do for me; in all places where we come, say of me, 'he is my brother.'" In the books of Moses, Abraham says, "when *Elohim* <note>original: "Elohim" — a Hebrew name for God.</note> caused me to wander," but in the *Targum* it says, "when the nations wandered after the works of their hands." This is already a closer explanation that leads us to the wandering of entire nations, and not just to a single man; for among all nations, Abraham was wandering with his seed.

### A covering of the eyes: to all those who are with you, indeed to everyone, and she was rebuked.

Abraham prayed on behalf of Abimelech, and then Abimelech gave him the sheep, oxen, male servants, and female servants; that is to say, through the Word of God, the Spirit of God sought to draw humanity to Himself. Those matters of faith, which are commanded according to the literal letter in the Word of God, were offered by the Church to the honor of God. These are the gifts that Abimelech gave to Abraham. With these outward things, the Christians served Jesus Christ and gave back Sarah, representing the Old Testament with its ceremonies. The Turks <note>The author uses "Turks" as a general historical term for Muslims or the Ottoman Empire, contrasting them with Western Christendom.</note>, however, served the one God under the Old Testament and gave back Sarah as the New Testament, with its ceremonies. In every party, Abimelech gave Abraham—as their Word of God—the entire land to dwell in freely. God has made and done all of this; He has governed the northern part of the world, Europe, through the New Testament and instilled it in the Christians; but the midday land <note>The South or the Orient.</note> He governed through the Old and gave it to the Turks as a possession.

To Sarah, Abimelech says: "Behold, I have given your brother 1,000 pieces of silver." Sarah is the Christian Church, and her brother is the outward Christ of the Christians, for they indeed made him into the blessing of all nations, which is Abraham. Hereby the Christian Church is designated with the "Crown-Number" of 1,000, and these are the 1,000 allegorical years of the outward kingdom of Christ, at the end of which the dragon with Gog and Magog becomes loose and causes the destruction of Babylon, Revelation 20 <note>original: "Apoc. XX"</note>. Furthermore, Abimelech says to Sarah: "Behold, he is to you a covering of the eyes, to all those who are with you, indeed to everyone," and she was rebuked.

The Targum Onkelos <note>An ancient Aramaic translation/paraphrase of the Torah.</note> says: "Behold, I have given your brother 1,000 silver shekels; behold, he is to you a covering of the excellence of the eyes, because I had you taken away, and because I have seen you and all that is with you; and for everything you have spoken, you are rebuked."

The external literal doctrine <note>original: "Buchstabenlehre"</note> of all nations was a veil before the mysteries. In the same way, the external doctrine of every church was its everything, and it was thereby punished with blindness because its church-king or church-government had taken Sarah away as the Word of God in a literal sense, and because he had looked upon her with literal eyes, along with everything that is in the Word of God. Above all, however, Sarah is punished for what she spoke as the church—for she is also the church. Abraham, as the external Christ and brother of Sarah, was also a veil of excellence and honor, a shining veil before the eyes of men; for the image of Christ was glorious, and so was the external doctrine of every church before the eyes of the children of the church. This is the wonderful story of Abimelech and Abraham. Abimelech represents everything external to the church and its doctrine, and yet primarily signifies the Turkish Church <note>The author likely refers to Islam or the Ottoman Empire, often called "Turkish" in 18th-century German religious texts</note> under the Old Testament. The manner of the Spirit of God in God’s Word is this: that under each specific church, He simultaneously hints at all others, and yet designates each one particularly through certain characteristics. Here, the Turkish Church is identified with the South-land and with Abimelech, whose name signifies "Father" and "King." <note>The Hebrew name Abimelech (אֲבִימֶלֶךְ) translates to "my father is king."</note> Through this, all peoples and religions are placed on equal footing, as they are all outwardly the corrupted Adam, and inwardly Christ. Is that not right and fair? Is God not the Creator and Father of all people? Should the salvation of one nation not be just as important to Him as the salvation of another? Should He not have given His Word with doctrine and instruction to all nations? Yes, certainly, even if they do not all possess the same Word of God in the same forms.

Abraham wandered among all nations with the blessing; God’s Word is with everyone, and God’s Spirit can penetrate the essence of all people and can work within them, provided they do not oppose Him with willful malice. The successive religions are the external figure of how the inner Spirit of God has moved from one place to another. In the Christian Church alone, we see this quite particularly. Often, there have been special awakenings <term>Erweckungen: periods of renewed religious fervor or "revivals"</term> in various places, which afterwards—when people turned their spiritual exercises into mere chatter and habit—were lost again; meanwhile, in other places, different awakenings arose. This was the pilgrimage of Abraham, acting as the Spirit of God on earth. Everywhere, however, he had to keep himself hidden, and he passed secretly through the entire external church until the end. God preserved him and kept him unharmed with his wife—representing the Word of God, which had been taken away into external opinions. Such a friend of God was Abraham with his seed in the midst of his sojourn! Should God not be able to preserve the Revelation of Christ in the same way through His power? That He will do so, we already have enough signs and proofs in the uniformity it shares with the course—

Abraham’s journey through the external church. For twelve years now, she <note>The "inner church" or spiritual community</note> has existed in secret upon the earth. God has guarded and preserved her amidst great and wondrous dangers. Nothing has yet arisen to destroy her, even though many people from the ranks of teachers and rulers have knowledge of her; usually, people are quick to attack any writing that goes against external established constitutions. However, when the Beast <note>A reference to the adversarial power in Revelation</note> comes out into the open against her, she will certainly crush the head of this serpent. God’s Word testifies to this, just as it testifies to the time of her revelation, the time of her public presentation, and her period of secrecy. These things are already fulfilled; therefore, the fulfillment of her victory against the Beast will not fail to occur.

Abimelech signifies, in a natural sense, the external man living by the light of reason <term>Vernunftlicht: the intellectual capacity of humans to understand the world without divine revelation</term>, yet still outside of the inner life, just as he is outside of Christ. This is similar to how the Turkish Church <note>The author uses "Turkish Church" to refer to Islam</note> is outside the Christian Church, yet lives in a better and more rational religion than the dead "name-Christians" <note>Those who are Christian in name only, lacking inner spiritual life</note>. God has led these peoples back into the light of nature more so than the external Christian Church. Abimelech means "a father who is king," and likewise, natural reason is the ruler and king that orders and governs human actions. Just as the Turkish Church is near to the Christian Church and is a neighbor to the Church of the New Testament, so too is a healthy and well-ordered reason—and a naturally honest and sensible person—near to the spiritual light or Christ within us. Such a person will not easily become hostile toward the truth and the inner life, provided he remains in humility.

The great world <note>The macrocosm</note> with its peoples and religions is like the small world <note>The microcosm</note>, which is man. When a person enters into rebirth <term>Wiedergeburt: the spiritual process of being born again into a divine life</term>, as into the inner life, his light of reason also becomes sharper, so that he can recognize many things better. However, because reason—as the light of nature—is subject to temptations, trials, and adversities, a person awakened in the inner life is also in danger of being led astray by the light of reason and being diverted from the inner life. This is just as the Turkish Church diverged from the Church under the name of Jesus, and as Abraham was in danger of losing his life while with Abimelech.

Alongside a good light of reason, humility is necessary and useful, so that the outer natural element of reason always bows beneath the inner light; for adversity and temptation fall upon the outer nature and can easily bring man to a fall in many different ways.

a. and she departed, and wandered: in the wilderness of Beersheba <note>original: "Bersaba"; the name means "Well of the Oath" or "Well of the Seven."</note>.
15. **Author.** And when the water in the bottle was spent—she cast the boy down under one of the shrubs.

a. and she sat over against him: and lift up her voice, and wept.

This expulsion of Hagar with her son serves as an excellent image; showing how we all, in this time of corruption, have been cast out from the clear countenance of God and from the Paradisiacal land of blessing into the curse of nature upon earth. All of nature is a wilderness, and lies in the vanity of death, surrounded by sins. Early in the morning, at the beginning of the world, God sent us into this wilderness through the transformation of nature on earth. The Heavenly Bread and the bottle with the Water of Eternal Life—namely the eternal essence which is from the eternal God—He has implanted within our creaturely being. But because we wander about in external corruption, and go astray in this wilderness, the water diminishes, and we draw near to spiritual death. Meanwhile, we are in the wilderness of Beersheba, which means, a Well of the Oath, or of the Covenant. The hidden Covenant of God lies deeply imprinted in our being, and the inner law of the Spirit, acting as the conscience <term>conscience: here used as the "Gewissen," the internal moral compass that connects the soul to divine law</term>, demands of man that he should bring forth heavenly fruits, heavenly thoughts, mind, will, and desire; but the external nature cannot do so, finding itself earthly, powerless, and always deficient. Because of this, the soul becomes despondent, sinking down as if dying. Everything becomes a burden to it, and that which previously gave it joy, now rejoices

She hopes and yet doubts at the same time, for certainty is not within her own self; rather, the divine Being from within must constitute the certainty in her, and must enter into her doubt with essential Truth and drive such doubts away like dark clouds. When a person reaches contrition <term>Zerknirschung: a state of deep remorse and "crushing" of the ego</term> of the heart, this occurs through the powerful movement of the Spirit in the inner man, which, like a light, makes his imperfection manifest. Then the Spirit rejects the outer man and judges him as unclean, sinful, and unrighteous. From this arises mourning, trepidation, anxiety, and doubt in the outer man, so that he regards himself as a stranger, like Hagar <note>Referring to Genesis 21, where Hagar and Ishmael are cast out into the wilderness</note>. He sits as if from afar and weeps that he is thus cast out. When a person then recognizes himself as imperfect and unworthy, and wishes to die to his own will—just like Hagar’s boy—then God hears the anxious voice of the soul's nature and opens the gentle fountain of the water of life within her, whereby the anxiety ceases and the mind is lightened again. 

But this does not remain at all times, for her fountain often dries up again, and the person enters back into vanity. Then God’s wrath, the Accuser, enters into her again and distresses her; and when that happens, and she is truly placed in anxiety, God shows her the fountain of water within herself again, whereby the poor, thirsty soul sitting in distress is again refreshed, quickened, and eased. Such a wandering about is our lot as poor children of men in this time, wherein we are cast out from the clear countenance of God—that is, where the divine Being does not entirely dwell within and animate our outer nature. 

But although God has had to reject the outer nature (as Hagar) out of necessity, she shall not remain rejected forever. Though she is not in Christ in this present time, she shall be in Christ in the time to come. Of this, the outer figure stands before our eyes with the Church of all the world. # The Blessing of All Nations

May the blessing of all nations be upon us. Just as God here called Hagar and her son to Himself, so He calls the Jews and the Turks <note>In this 17th/18th-century context, "Turks" was a common term for Muslims.</note> to the confession of Jesus Christ. He shows them the wellspring of water in this teaching of the Revelation of Christ given to me. And just as all nations on this earth shall yet stand in the true teaching of Jesus Christ, so shall the entire outward man of all believers on this earth stand in Jesus Christ. They shall be filled and permeated with divine power, life, blessing, and heavenly rest and peace, just as fire permeates iron.

With every new dispensation of the Church, Abraham rose early in the morning. Their "Word of God" was their bread and their water-skin, which he placed upon their shoulders; and the children of the Church were themselves the boy <note>Referring to Ishmael as a symbol for the followers of a specific religious era.</note>. With this, the Church went forth and went astray in the wilderness of Beersheba. The Word of God, in the literal letter itself, was the wilderness of Beersheba—the "Covenant of the Oath"—through which the Church entered into a covenant with God. 

The word <term>midbar <note>original Hebrew: מדבר</note></term> (wilderness) is also derived from <term>dabar <note>original Hebrew: דבר</note></term> (the word). Thus, God's Word is itself this wilderness in which Hagar went astray. Through this Word, God created the outward Church. External nature on earth is also a "created Word of God," through which He in turn created all things on earth. All of this was a wilderness in which we went astray, and it was necessary for the sake of human procreation. 

That the Spirit of God looks primarily upon this is easily perceived and recognized by the fact that in all such figures <note>Symbolic types or allegories.</note>, He introduces the female sex along with the procreation of children. Why else would the Spirit of God always have so much to do in His Word with women and with cohabitation?

When one dispensation of the Church came to an end and another began, the water-skin of the previous Church ran dry, and the boy was cast away. Thus, the water-skin of the Old Testament teaching ran dry at the beginning of the Christian Church. There, Hagar stood opposite the Christian Church, a bowshot away; that is, she placed herself in a different teaching, for the teaching was the "bow" of the Church with which she shot. The boy of the Old Testament was cast away under his shrubbery, but Hagar, representing Nature, did not want to see him die. Therefore, God revealed the well with the teaching of the Turkish Church <note>The Islamic faith</note>, in which the discarded boy—representing the Old Testament—was lifted up once again.

For just as the true inner meaning of the Spirit shall emerge from every word of God, so also shall the divine life emerge again in all of nature, so that a true tabernacle of God may be among men on earth. The departure from one into the other was also an exodus from Egypt, but all of that was not yet the true exodus, just as the outward Christian Church was not yet the true Sarah. Through this breaking of one by the other, the children of God were humbled: for the believers necessarily had to suffer much during the rejections of one religion by another, and had to enter into manifold doubts, uncertainties, and anxieties. This was the figure of the crucifixion of the self-will: for everything outward was natural self-will. When the self-will is broken and rejected, poor nature falls into mourning and trepidation. The Church will experience this now, when everything outward is taken from her, for she is wandering in the wilderness with Hagar and has nothing certain. All naturally honest people who have died until now have entered into their rest according to their outward life, even if they have not yet immediately entered into the true blessedness of the saints; and this same lot will also be shared by the honest natural people who are dying now. When the inner reveals itself in the outer, it will be a testimony to Abraham—as Jesus Christ—that he dug this well, and that he has always taken the outer church into his covenant through his Word. Therefore, all of this is called Beersheba <note>original: "Bersaba" — meaning "Well of the Oath" or "Well of the Seven."</note>, because God and man swore to one another there and made a covenant with one another.

If this is indeed the one and true God, then this trial is the sign that he fears the true God and nothing else—no other god, whoever he may be.

This, then, is the sacrifice of Isaac in all its parts. That it is not a literal history is obvious enough, for he would be a wicked man who would accept a voice as being from God which told him he should kill his child. If a god wanted to command this of me, I would promptly spit in his face; for a being who would bid me perform evil and mercilessness or cruelty, I will not honor nor fear, even if it were God Himself. What is good, just, and beneficent, compassionate and merciful—that I will honor, that shall be my God, and of no other God do I wish to know or hear. But just as it would be irrational to worship such a being as God who would bid me commit a murder and to act according to his command; so irrational has it been that we have believed this matter, and so many other things in God’s word, according to the letter <note>The "letter" refers to the literal, non-spiritual reading of the Bible</note>, and have attributed many things to the true God that are entirely against His essence, nature, and character. Have we not thereby bound the essence of Truth, as Isaac, upon the wood, and wanted to slaughter him merely based on a literal voice in the word of God, which we took for the right and true voice of God? Therefore, that was only a temptation, and now we shall [open] our eyes—

One must pay primary attention to the comparisons of one against the other, for therein lies everything; and that is why it is said that Christ spoke in parables and comparisons. The external Abraham thus had children in the Christian Church, and his brother Nahor also had children in the Church of the Old Testament and among the Turks.

## Chapter 23.

### Fifth Parashah, the Life of Sarah. <note>Hebrew: Chayei [Sarah]</note>

1. **A.** And the life of Sarah was: 100 years and 20 years and 7 years.
**n.** The years of the life of Sarah.
2. **N.** And Sarah died in Kiriath-arba, that is Hebron in the land of Canaan.
**a.** And Abraham came: to mourn for Sarah and to weep for her.

The death of Sarah represents the sinking away of the true doctrine of wisdom in the Church, and of the true understanding of the Word of God. The word *Chayyim* <note>original: חיים</note>—"Life"—always stands in the plural in Hebrew to indicate that man possesses a life composed of different natures. The life of Sarah is also presented here in three parts: 100 years, 20 years, and 7 years. These parts, taken as three, signify the three dispensations of the Church, wherein the true doctrine and the true understanding of man have lain in death in all three.

She died at Kiriath-arba, which means "the City of Four," <note>original: "Kiriatharba"</note> that is, in all four religions. The Christian Church, however, is primarily the outward Sarah, and her four religions are Kiriath-arba, in which the true, inner Sarah has died. This is also Hebron, which takes its name from "uniting together"; <note>The author connects the Hebrew root of Hebron (HBR) to the concept of association or joining.</note> but in Jesus Christ, all things shall be united into one. It is also the land of Canaan where Sarah died; but where she died, there she now rises again as the true doctrine. Under the Christian Church in its four religions, all four main religions are also meant simultaneously. Our first mother, Eve, is the rightful Sarah, and she lived in the actual land of Canaan and died there as well; for she, together with Adam, necessarily had to pass through death, because at that time no transformation of nature could yet occur. Consequently, that which will now happen to the believers—who shall never taste death—did not happen to our first parents. They did, however, possess the Paradisiacal life beforehand, which we shall possess after the corruption.

When the years of Sarah are divided into three parts, and yet it says once more, "the years of the life of Sarah," it is thereby indicated that Sarah is twofold: internal and external, since the external Sarah was sinful. Because Sarah is twofold, her dying is also twofold. In the beginning, the inner Sarah died; the divine life passed into the death of the external nature, and the true understanding of the Word of God departed. At the end, however, the external Sarah dies; the external doctrine comes to an end. But in nature, this is not an actual dying, but rather a sinking of the external into the internal, where the external spirit of the world and of man is baptized <note>original: "verteufet," likely a variant of "getauft" (baptized) or "vertieft" (deepened/immersed)</note> and submerged into the inner Spirit of God, so that the inner man shall rule and guide everything through the outer. In all four religions, the inner Sarah was buried at Hebron in Kiriath-arba, and Abraham too; therefore it is said of four men and women that they lay buried at Hebron: namely Adam and Eve, Abraham and Sarah, Isaac and Rebecca, Jacob and Leah.

When Abraham mourns and laments for Sarah, this indicates how the Spirit in the believers has lamented that the inner power of God in nature and the true light in the understanding of men had so far departed, that the Spirit had to lie as if in darkness and as if fettered with heavy bonds.

This lamentation of the spirit exists within human nature without the external man knowing or understanding it; from this often arises that deep sighing within a person, of which one cannot articulate the cause. Oh, what lies within this lamenting and sighing of the spirit! No one can form a conception of it unless he himself truly enters into it and feels it within himself; only then will he obtain some light from this description. When in the future the spirit within us is released from these heavy burdens and bonds into freedom, only then will we see the deep misery in which we have been.

## The Burial of Sarah. Chapter 23.

Abraham was a stranger and possessed nothing of his own, and yet he desired to have a permanent burial place in the land of his pilgrimage. Humanity, along with true light and understanding, has been buried, so much so that in our outward appearance we have become like the beasts. From these graves, people shall now rise again, of which the Scripture says: *Awake, you who sleep, arise from the dead, and Christ will enlighten you*; likewise, *Arise, shine, for your light has come, and the glory of Jehovah rises over you.* <note>The author quotes Ephesians 5:14 and Isaiah 60:1, interpreting the "resurrection" as a spiritual awakening of the intellect.</note>

Abraham desires the burial place for Sarah, for he is one with her. Christ humbled himself even unto death, and bowed before the children of the land. The Spirit spoke from God’s Word in an external voice according to the letter. Through this, the inner being died and was buried; and this is why Abraham wanted to have a burial place of his own. For through external speaking about external things, the inner true meaning of the Word of God escaped the understanding of men, and the Spirit with true understanding withdrew, as if into a grave or under a cover. Abraham wanted to bury his dead away from his sight. That is, in the outward there should be no death, but rather the outward should live. The external spirit of the world <term>Weltgeist: the mundane or secular mindset that focuses only on the physical</term> and external teachings took the upper hand, and thereby the inner was buried. The external Sarah began to live and the inner Sarah went into death; or rather, the external man lived and the internal man was buried with Christ into death. Thus it was in the teaching, and thus it was in nature. Abraham stood up from his dead and spoke to the children of Heth; that is, he spoke externally, and as it were ceased to speak and work internally. The name Heth signifies "breaking" <note>original: "Zerbrechen"; a common mystical etymology linking the Hittites/Heth to the Hebrew root 'hathat', meaning to be shattered or terrified</note>; through the external, the internal was broken, and thus the Spirit spoke literally from the Word of God to the children of breaking. Everything lay in brokenness—the people together with the teaching. The Spirit spoke from God’s Word, but the spirit of the world twisted the meaning through the sound of the letter, moving from the internal to the external. Thus the rushing of the wind or Spirit was indeed heard, but one did not know where it came from, nor where it went, or what it pointed toward. The children of Heth spoke: *Hear us, my lord, you are a prince of Elohim <note>original: "Fürst Elohim"; a mighty prince or prince of God</note> among us; bury your dead in the best of our graves.* They accepted the speeches of the Word of God according to how it fell into their understanding, according to how the spirit of the world shaped that understanding. Yet the Word of God was to them a Prince of God;

7. **A.** And Abraham stood up and bowed himself before the people of the land, before the children of Heth.

The people bowed before Abraham, and Abraham bowed before the people. This represents the **humiliation of Christ**, <term>humiliation of Christ: the theological concept of "kenosis" or Christ emptying himself to take on human form</term> as the divine essence formed itself into human essence and became a man within every human being. There, the Spirit spoke in an external manner from God’s Word, and humans bowed before it, accepting it as God’s Word in an external, lowly sense. The divine essence became human within people, and within those people were humble concepts and a meager light, which the humbled divine essence itself produced within them. And so Abraham, as the Spirit, spoke in a lowly, literal manner from God’s Word, and within humanity, he grasped it in humble concepts. He spoke with the children of Heth, and they spoke with him; thus one must take Abraham and the children of Heth—or God and man—as one, for in this way God has made all things.

Abraham says that if it pleased them for him to bury his dead, they should speak for him with Ephron the son of Zohar. Ephron means **dust**, <note>original: "Ephron" - derived from the Hebrew 'aphar' meaning dust or earth.</note> and thus Abraham said above that he was "dust and ashes."

The **Zohar** <note>original: "Zohar"; refers to the foundational work of Jewish mysticism, meaning "Splendor" or "Radiance"</note> however, derives its name from "shining" or "being white." **Ephron** <note>The Hittite from whom Abraham purchased the cave of Machpelah; the author etymologically links the name to the Hebrew word 'aphar' meaning dust</note> represents the external doctrine of letters concerning Christ in the New Testament, which is but dust, yet it also possessed a great radiance through the name of Jesus. All external teaching is therefore simultaneously included therein. Through the external Christ, the true Jesus Christ has been buried, and this is the burial of **Sarah**, representing the inner, true doctrine. To this end, they had to speak with Ephron before Abraham; that is, they had to speak and teach of Christ in a doctrine that is merely dust. For Abraham—or Christ—also spoke thus in the "letter" <note>The literal, surface-level meaning of scripture as opposed to its spirit</note>, and so Abraham was dust and ashes, and was buried.

Abraham desired to have the **cave of Machpelah**, which means a double or twofold cave. In the Church, the two Testaments are this twofold cave, which are both held together in the Christian Church. Ephron held this cave as his property; that is, the external teachings of both Testaments were dust and a sepulcher for Abraham. Abraham wanted the burial site for the "full price" <note>referring to the 400 shekels of silver paid in Genesis 23</note>, for it had to occur through a spiritual commerce in the Temple, wherein the buyers and sellers sat. Abraham gave the money to Ephron; that is, God's Word was given to the Church as literal histories. The Spirit spoke from it literally, and thus humanity had to receive it from His hand. They also had to attribute it back to Him literally, and offer Him literal services of worship from it, and thereby they gave Abraham a hereditary burial site among them.

Within man, there is a twofold cave: the external nature and the soulful nature, which is bound to the external nature. In both, the Spirit lies buried with the inner man, and produces a very slight and dim light in the external man, because the dark clouds therein are too thick, and the light can only shine through in a broken manner here and there; for this reason, one has also been unable to understand God's Word. The Spirit thus desired no other burial than His own Word and His own human essence within the children of men. The cave of Machpelah lies at the end of the field of Ephron. In the Church, this is the **dispensation** <term>Haushaltung: a theological period or "economy" of God's dealings with humanity</term> of the New Testament, which is the last; and the external man was also the last or the end in the initial Creation. Likewise, the course of time until now, in its corruption, was the last. In all of these, Abraham desired to have his burial; and in this time there was also death and dying, and men had to be buried.

Through Jesus Christ, they have been purchased as his own property; therefore, they should now all together confess Jesus Christ and believe in him. Jesus Christ has written his Word of God for every single church. He has given it to her, and from him she received it. By recognizing and confessing his Word as divine, and by worshiping a God from whom this Word originated, she has thereby confessed and worshiped Jesus Christ without knowing that it was he. This is like when Joseph spoke with his brothers and they did not recognize him <note>Genesis 42:8; a common mystical trope where Christ is hidden in the Old Testament like Joseph was hidden from his kin</note>; and when he reveals himself, they all stand frozen in astonishment that God himself is also our brother. The field of Ephron is the entire Church, as well as the entire nature of the world and of man. The field lies at Machpelah, as is said here; that is, it is twofold <note>original: "zweyfach"; the author connects the Hebrew name Machpelah to the root meaning "double" or "folded"</note>, a temporal and eternal being, like the natural soul and the outer nature within man. In the Church, these are the two Testaments, which are also twofold, being both internal and external. It lies facing Mamre; that is, it is primarily within the Christian Church, for therein Christ becomes manifest, because it was therein that he primarily entered into death.

Machpelah is taken here as a place or region upon which the field of Ephron is said to be, and the cave is said to be upon this field. However, the cave itself is called Machpelah above, and thus one clearly sees from this that these are not literal histories <note>The author argues that the shifting definitions of the location in the biblical text point toward an allegorical rather than a purely historical reading</note>. Machpelah, the field, and the cave are all one, and all three dispensations <term>Haushaltungen: stages of divine administration or eras of spiritual history</term> and the four main religions belong to it. This entire region, and all the trees—or doctrines—that are upon it, belong to Abraham or Jesus Christ as his own property. All of this was his burial place, wherein he lay in death among all people. But now, as he shall rise again, they shall all live with him. The Christian Church, however, which has borne his name outwardly and stands in place of all others, is also primarily this field of Ephron with the twofold cave. Death and burial are accompanied by mourning and lamentation, and such was our life until now, which was to be called a death rather than a life. What joy will therefore follow when we now truly wake up from this death and rise again to a life glorious and blessed beyond all measure, in the final glory of the lower world on earth! May the Holy God help us to that end!

1. **A.** And Abraham was old: and well stricken in years—
**n.** and Jehovah had blessed Abraham in all things.

# On the Mystery of the Covenant

Everything is made by him, and everything must obey him. The hips of Abraham <note>original: "Hüfte Abrahams"; refers to Genesis 24:2-9 where the servant places his hand under Abraham's thigh to swear an oath</note> signify the previous condition of external nature and doctrine, from which the reproduction and multiplication of humanity and the Church has occurred; for within the hips lies the generative power for reproduction. This servant of Abraham is sworn, within this condition of nature and doctrine, by the God of heaven and earth, that he will not take a wife for Isaac from the corrupt Canaanites. Nature is turned from the external to the internal and immersed, and is united into one with the internal Abraham of eternal blessing; this is the swearing of the servant, with the laying of his hand under the hip of Abraham. 

Through this, the hip is dislocated, just as Jacob’s hip was dislocated in his struggle with God <note>A reference to Jacob wrestling the angel in Genesis 32</note>. By this means, the reproduction of [natural] men comes to an end; therefore, in the coming time, Isaac also receives no wife from the corrupt Canaanites. The servant of Abraham is sworn by the God of heaven and earth. In nature, this indicates that a change will occur in our atmosphere <note>original: "Lufthimmel," literally the air-heaven</note> up to the lower firmament, and upon the earth, whereby the channels of heaven are opened again with blessing, and heaven and earth are, as it were, bound and united into one. Here the servant, as external nature, must sink his hand—his acting, making, and doing—into the internal Abraham, so that the external and temporal may be permeated and swallowed up by the power of the internal. This is the swearing of the servant into the hip of his master. 

In the Church and the teaching of the Word of God, the external is likewise turned inward; then the teaching of the Word of God, according to the internal and true sense of the Spirit, is the wife of Isaac, from whom the universal Church shall be begotten. Isaac shall have such a wife, and not a wife or a doctrine of the external Christian Church, which is the Canaanite people of abomination. These have hitherto occupied and inhabited the Land of Promise with the external Christ, but shall now be cast out by Joshua as the true Jesus Christ through the Revelation of Christ. The doctrine of the Revelation of Christ shall not be a doctrine of any external religions, for all external teachings are the external land of Canaan with the peoples of abomination; but the true internal doctrine is the internal land of Canaan. Canaan takes its name from "being low" <note>The author correctly identifies the Hebrew etymology of Canaan (Kena'an), often interpreted as "lowland" or "humbled"</note>. The external is so named in view of the low, meager—

But toward the external Canaanite, impure, and sinful essence, it shall not turn.

The number 10 is an image of the Whole, and also an image of the Interior. The ten camels in the Church are the entire Word of God taken together, and specifically the Old Testament, which stands as an image of everything external, and by which the bride is led to Isaac. These camels carry with them all kinds of goods of divine and natural wisdom. In man, the camels are his natural being in all its powers and qualities. The servant traveled to Aram Naharaim <note>Aram of the Two Rivers, or Mesopotamia</note> to the city of Nahor. Aram is the Christian Church, which has a high name because of the name of Jesus: for Aram means "high" <note>a traditional etymological interpretation of the Hebrew root</note>. Naharaim means "two rivers," and these are the two Testaments <note>The Old and New Testaments</note>. The city of Nahor is primarily the Old Testament. The Old Testament in the Christian Church is viewed in this way because the revelation of Christ comes out of it, the teaching of which is the bride of Isaac. The camels kneel down outside the city at a water well. The water well is the teaching of the Church itself. The city is taken here to represent the future universal Church, which exists in the true teaching. Outside of this city is the external teaching. The evening time indicates the end of a dispensation <note>original: "Haushaltung"</note> and the beginning of a new teaching, from which water is drawn; and the teachings are also the "daughters." That teaching which thus waters Abraham's servant and also his camels, she

shall be the bride of Isaac; that is, when the true meaning of the Spirit appears within the images and riddles of the Scripture, then these camels are, as it were, watered with the water of true doctrine. They provide instruction and teaching, and thus also water Abraham's servant, representing the teacher. All religions taken together are also Abraham's servants and camels, for they have collectively carried Abraham's goods. A doctrine which is now suitable for all religions, and can water them all—this is the one which God has chosen for Isaac. No single outward doctrine is for all religions, for the doctrine of the New Testament is not for the Jews nor the Heathens <note>original: "Heyden"; referring to non-believers or pagans</note>; the outward doctrine of the Old Testament is not for the Christians and Heathens; and the doctrine of the Heathens is not for others. But the doctrine of the Revelation of Christ is for all, and it displaces all other outward doctrines; therefore, this is the bride of Isaac.

In man, the bride of Isaac is recognized thus: When an essence is felt which refreshes and quickens the soul and all properties of nature; which softens the anxious, dry, and parched mind <note>original: "Gemüth"; the inner seat of emotions and soul-life</note> and makes it soft, benevolent, humble, compassionate, kind, and gentle—that is the spiritual Virgin who is destined for Isaac. This is also the inner spiritual well of water, around which the servant and the camels—representing the natural, outward man—are encamped outside the inner city, waiting until the maiden comes. Man can do nothing for himself, and cannot make this maiden, this inner gentle essence, come when he wishes. He must, with Abraham's servant, pray to God that He might meet him and show mercy to him. He also lays himself down at the well of the Word of God to learn God's will; he commends himself to God and leaves it to God to reveal Himself within him. Nature must wait until it pleases God to lead forth the Virgin as the inner man, so that the outward man can feel and perceive her. That which waters and refreshes our outward anxious nature from within is the Bride of the Lamb, who is wedded to the outward nature and the soul's nature, as Isaac. The natural soul is the bridegroom, and the gentle inner essence is the bride. This marriage is also the birth of Christ in us, a Son of God, a formed God and visible image of the Deity according to the creature, wherein the invisible and immeasurable God—as the divine essence spread throughout the whole world—dwells in the creaturely image of man.

...dwells within, or inhabits the same, just as the air inhabits a house and yet is also present everywhere outside of the house. At evening time, the servant came to the water-well where the Virgin came to him; that is, in the final age of the world, the Virgin <note>The "Virgin" here refers to Sophia or Divine Wisdom, a central figure in Behmenist and Pietist mysticism representing the pure, spiritual nature of humanity.</note> will be found both in doctrine and in nature. The believers of past times surely also received their measure and portion in their "evening"—that is, in the waning of their wild, natural inclinations—but that is to be regarded as only a very weak image compared to what is now to come. The Virgin was not named to the servant beforehand, and he did not know her previously. Just so, the merely natural man does not know the inner, spiritual man beforehand; and even we, who know him now, do not yet sufficiently know the exuberant power of God which will reveal itself in the future throughout all of nature, and we do not know in this time what glory is prepared for us in the days of Isaac. For this reason, we still live now in faith and not in sight, just as the servant trusted God and believed He would grant him what He promised to Abraham, and that the mercy of God would be revealed through Abraham. God is faithful, and His promises are "Yes and Amen" <note>original: "Ja und Amen"; a reference to 2 Corinthians 1:20, signifying certainty.</note>; therefore, the Isaac of the final time must become a possessor of all the goods and treasures of Jesus Christ. We must, however, wait for this with Abraham's servant, for it does not depend on any man's "running," but on God's mercy <note>A reference to Romans 9:16: "It is not of him that willeth, nor of him that runneth, but of God that sheweth mercy."</note>, and on the time in which God has set the goal, when the promised Seed of the Woman shall appear and bruise the head of the serpent. This Seed has lain hidden in nature from the beginning, as if in God's servant, who carries Abraham's goods within himself; and God, according to His good pleasure, has placed this goal in a man at the evening of the world, when God's voice sounds in the Seed of the Woman within nature, and when nature, as God's servant, brings forth the hidden jewel. Just as this first happens in doctrine with the revelation of Christ in one individual, so it will happen in divine power within the nature of all people whom God has created for His final glory on earth. Just as the final revelation of Jesus Christ did not depend on any man's will or effort, but on God's mercy and pleasure—and this Virgin was found by a man who had never been a teacher at all, even though all righteous teachers sought her and did not find her—so it lies...

Likewise, rebirth and the beginning of the inner life do not depend on a person's own striving, but on the inward movement of God within human nature. He moves within a person's nature whenever the condition of that nature allows it. Now, we do not all possess the same nature, nor did anyone create their own nature; rather, God makes everything. Therefore, no person's nature is rejected by God before the opening of this internal fountain of water <note>original: "Wasserbrunnens"; a metaphor for spiritual awakening</note>, provided that the person remains in natural honesty and fairness and does not enter into malice and wickedness. One must only wait with Abraham's servant and ask God for mercy; He will surely lead the maiden forth when the time and propriety <note>original: "Schicklichkeit"; implies a divinely ordained fitness or timing</note> are right.

Abraham’s servant represents the Word of God and also the office of teaching. This servant has been speaking until now, and while he is still speaking and has not yet finished, Rebecca appears—representing the true teaching of the Old Testament. She is very beautiful to behold; she is a virgin who has never yet conceived "church-children," and no man—no external teacher—has ever known her. This is the Virgin Mary, from whom Jesus Christ is born or revealed without the assistance of a priest-man <note>original: "Pfaffen-Mannes"; a derogatory or sharp term for a cleric, emphasizing that Christ's birth is purely spiritual and not through human religious institutions</note>. This is the birth of Christ from a virgin, and there has never been any other in the world, except that Christ is also born in us from the internal spiritual ground as if from a virgin. From this ground, no natural creation or human procreation proceeds, for natural creation only occurs through the division of the properties of nature in its fallen state.

This virgin, or teaching, descends into the depths to the well of the Word of God, draws wisdom and knowledge from it, fills her pitcher, and ascends with it into the Church. Abraham’s servant runs toward this virgin, and she gives him a drink, and also to all his camels. All religions and peoples are given to drink from this, and it all happens in haste. The drawing of water twice—once for the servant and once for the camels—points to the two Testaments <note>The Old and New Testaments of the Bible</note>, from both of which water is drawn. This drawing first occurred externally in the literal teaching <term>literal teaching: "Buchstabenlehre," the study of the outward "letter" of the law rather than the spirit</term>, at which the servant wondered and watched to see what would finally come of it, as will follow further: for beneath the external, the internal is always perceived, and the internal is indicated at the same time.

As it is in the Church and its teaching, so it is also in nature. That which waters the nature of the soul from God’s fountain of love is the spiritual virgin. This is the "Mary" within us, from whom Christ is born in us. If this does not happen within us, then no Christ outside of us can help us, even if there were a thousand of them outside of us. However, a person must ask for this drink from the water of life, just as Abraham’s servant asked the virgin for a drink. Then the virgin surrenders herself into the nature of the soul and the external man—for to him who asks, it shall be given—and then the servant, representing the soul, thanks God for having led him so happily and rightly. Just as the virgin watered not only Abraham’s servant but also the camels, and just as she watered not only the nature of the soul but also

->And Jehovah led me on the way: to the house of my lord’s brother.<-

The drawing and watering from the well of the Word of God has continued throughout the entire duration of the external Church. But the servant or man—representing the teaching office <note>original: "Lehramt"; the formal authority of the church</note>—remained silent regarding the virgin, who represents the true doctrine, and said nothing of it. He only marveled at the Word of God to discern whether Jehovah had granted success to his journey or not. But at the end, when all the camels have finished drinking—meaning when the watering from the external doctrine comes to an end—then the golden ring <note>original: "Spange"; likely referring to the nose-ring or forehead jewel mentioned in Genesis 24</note> weighing half a shekel <note>original: "Seckel"; a historical unit of weight and currency</note> and the two bracelets of ten shekels of gold are fastened upon the virgin, and she is asked for her name, which she reveals. 

The internal and external together constitute a whole, for the internal and external man make up the whole person; and so it is also with the internal and external doctrine. Now, if the virgin is taken to represent the internal, then the half-shekel is the external; and when the half-shekel is fastened to the virgin, it signifies how the external is overturned and turned inward, or united with the internal. When the Spirit of Life from God enters the external man, he is transformed into a spiritual will, mind, and desires, and becomes spiritually minded. Then he wears the bridal adornment of Christ, and the name of the internal is revealed and recognized in the external, just as the servant asked the virgin to reveal her name. 

In the same way, the doctrine of the Word of God is such an adornment when the internal sense of the Spirit is guided into the external doctrine of the letter. There, the names and images become manifest, and it is recognized what is meant by them, what such things are called, and what they intend to say. The two bracelets of ten shekels of gold point to the two Testaments <note>The Old and New Testaments</note>, which are the two hands in the Church. The number ten, however, points to the internal. In man, the soulful nature, which is eternal, and the external nature, which is temporal, constitute the number two; and when both are permeated with the internal divine life, those are also the ten shekels of gold. When the man asks for the name of the virgin, this must be understood both internally and externally, for things also have their names in the external world. The New Testament

The Testament gave the name of an external Christ, and so it was with all things. And they also thanked God that He had blessed the church and led it on the right path, but it was still only a night’s lodging; in the end, however, at the true wedding, there will be no more night. In such a way, the external is often mixed in and indicated at the same time as the internal, which also cannot be otherwise, because until now the external kingdom of nature and the internal kingdom of Christ have lain together and within one another, just like the internal and external man; and because the Spirit, amidst external things, has always looked toward the internal and toward the end.

Rebecca's name signifies fatness or being well-sustained <note>The author likely connects the Hebrew name 'Rivqah' to a root implying 'to tie' or 'stall-fed,' symbolizing spiritual abundance</note>, and Laban means white. The color white is an image of the interior, toward which Abraham's servant <note>The servant is interpreted here as the Word of God</note> travels. However, the Christian Church also stands as an image of the interior because of the name of Jesus; therefore, she is also Laban. There, Laban saw the man—the external Christ—and the external teachings of the New Testament. People ran out to this well, and because they saw the bridal jewelry of Christ attached to this Church and the teachings of the New Testament, they urged the man, or Jesus, to enter the house of the Church. They made the house of the Church clean from the external teachings and ceremonies of the Old Testament, and received Jesus and the teachings of the New Testament with joy. This is what it means when Laban says: "Come in, you blessed of Jehovah <note>original: "Jehova"</note>, why do you stand outside? For I have cleared the house, and there is room for the camels"—for the external doctrines and matters of faith. Thus, Jesus was led into the house of the Christian Church with his apostles, as the men who were with him; and the people of the Church themselves were also the men who were with this teaching and with this ministry, as if with Christ. The teachings of the New Testament are also the water with which they washed their feet, for with these teachings, all other teachings of the Jews and Heathens, in which they had walked until then, were washed away. Just as this is the case with the Christian Church in regard to all such things, so it is also with every other church, as each has willingly and joyfully received and accepted its Word of God as the servant of Abraham. This is the external kingdom of nature, where the Church has thus formed itself externally. The internal spiritual kingdom of Christ has the very same form, and has simultaneously proceeded in a hidden manner within the interior under the external form. Just as Abraham's servant, as the Word of God, came externally to Laban, so also the Spirit of God came internally to the believers. Laban, within a person, is their external nature, with which the natural soul is connected. When the Virgin, as the inner man in the spirit, comes into the outer man, and

working within it, Rebecca then comes to Laban and tells him of Abraham’s message. > 33. A. And he set food before him: but he said, I will not eat: until I have first told my business.

Eating signifies the communication or the entering of one thing into another. Abraham’s servant did not want to enter into the outer doctrine of the letter <note>The "letter" refers to a literal, superficial understanding of scripture as opposed to its spiritual essence.</note> with the inner or true sense of the Spirit; or rather, the Inner did not want to open itself in the Outer until the outer speaking and teaching from the Word of God had first come to an end. Laban presented an outer meal, or outer teachings and matters of faith, but the Spirit, according to the inner sense of the Word of God, did not want these. He desired no outer circumcision, but rather the circumcision of the heart <note>original: "Beschneidung des Herzens"; a common Pietist theme emphasizing internal transformation over external ritual.</note> from evil lusts and desires; no outer baptism, but a washing and cleansing of the heart from sins. He wanted first to recite his words in the outer church, from which narration one should then see at the end what he wanted, and what his courtship, his sense, will, and desire might be. To this Laban must then consent, and only then do both eat with one another. Just so, the inner Spirit does not immediately surrender itself to the outer man until it has first made known to him its will, driving, and working, and the outer man consents to it. Only then follows the inward tasting and sensing of how good and friendly God is. Laban says to Abraham’s messenger that he should speak on.
