<meta>This page continues the allegorical interpretation of the life of Abraham, specifically focusing on the mission of his servant to find a wife for Isaac as a type for the development of the Church and its doctrines.</meta>

stament <note>original: "stament"; likely the conclusion of "Testament" from the previous page</note> is designated, for this Woman comes out of the Christian Church, and yet also out of the Old Testament. God greatly blessed Abraham and made him great in all three dispensations <term>dispensations: "Haushaltungen," referring to the different eras of God's dealings with humanity</term> of the Church, just as this wealth of Abraham in sheep, cattle, and the like, stands in three parts. But Sarah, as the Christian Church, bore Isaac to him after she had become old; and to this one, who was begotten out of the true doctrine of the Old Testament, he gave everything that he has. He shall have no wife—no doctrines—from the daughters of the Canaanites, who also stand here in three parts. It shall be no doctrine from these three. Now, when the swearing-in of the servant is recounted here again, though it was already recounted above; it is done with a view toward the two Testaments, for these were sent into the already existing outward church and doctrine of the heathens, in order to bring forth another wife or another doctrine, which however also first happened outwardly, as the Virgin, as the true inner doctrine, did not yet follow the servant. Meanwhile, the Angel of Jehovah went along hiddenly on this path, and finally let the way of the Word of God succeed, so that the Virgin was found. Abraham says: then you shall be free from my curse, as the word "oath" here actually means. Above stands another word which expresses the oath, namely *Shebua* <note>Hebrew: שְׁבוּעָה</note>, but here stands *ala* <note>Hebrew: אָלָה</note>, which actually means "the curse." The previous outward condition of nature, and also the outward doctrine, was the curse into which the Church had to go, as Moses says: "You stand today all before Jehovah your Elohim, that you should enter into the covenant of Jehovah your Elohim, and into the curse (*ala*), which Jehovah your Elohim makes with you today," Deuteronomy 29:10-12.

From this curse the servant shall be free here, if he takes the Virgin from the kindred and house of Abraham. Now, however, it says once more: "If you come to my kindred, and they will not give her to you, then you shall be free from my curse." This is the beginning of a subsequent dispensation in the outward doctrine, where the following one, with its Word of God, should nevertheless be free from the outward doctrine of the preceding Church. Further, the servant now recounts his journey, and how he encountered the maiden.

The external Church came to the water-well of external doctrine, and was also wedded to the external Isaac. The external Church was adorned with external jewelry, and they worshipped Jehovah under external images. In such a manner, Abraham’s servant, acting as the Word of God, first came to the Church, and the Church came to the Word of God. In such discourses, it is all one and the same; one may understand by a person either the Word of God or the Church, whichever fits best, for God’s Word and the Church are called by the same names; one must only take care whether both must be understood internally or externally. The preceding discourses here primarily represent the External, and now at the end it is asked: Are you now those who wish to show kindness and truth to my master? If so, tell me; but if not, declare it to me, so that I may turn to the right or to the left. Man cannot show any kindness or truth to Abraham or to God; rather, conversely, the blessing and the good come from Abraham or from God. When that enters into man, then kindness and truth also proceed from man once more, and he accepts it with thanksgiving and gratitude when God—

sends something good. God works the good within man, and thus God and man are addressed under the same name; from this, one may understand why God, His Word, and the Church (or humanity) are addressed under the same name in such discourses. Therefore, when Abraham's servant appears with the true inner doctrine, those who possess goodness and truth within themselves will receive and accept him. In the external Church, Abraham's servant was not accepted according to the inner sense of the Word of God, even though that sense already lay within the Word. For this reason, the Spirit turned with the New Testament toward the exterior on the left, and with the Old Testament toward the right. <note>The author uses "left" and "right" to describe the directional flow of spiritual influence or the division of the covenants in history.</note> But now, at the end, neither Laban (representing the New) nor Bethuel (representing the Old Testament) stands against Abraham's servant, neither in evil nor in good. The two Testaments give their consent to the doctrine of the Revelation of Christ and say: "This comes from Jehovah; we can say nothing against you, neither bad nor good." <note>original: "Das kommt von Jehova, wir können nichts wider dich reden, weder Böses noch Gutes." In the process of the <term>rebirth</term> <note>original: "Wiedergeburt"; the spiritual transformation of an individual.</note> of man, the very same form exists. Abraham's servant, acting as the Spirit of God and the conscience within man, speaks and exhorts for a long time until finally the soul's will and nature surrender to God's will and say: "This comes from the Lord, against which nothing can be said; here is Rebecca, as my will immersed in you—take it." Laban stands as the image of the outer nature, and Bethuel as the image of the natural soul, for these two parts in man correspond to the two Testaments. When an exhortation comes from the outside, as happens through all teaching, the outer man in his external, temporal nature must consent; therefore, Laban stands at the forefront. The Revelation of Christ also comes out of the Christian Church, which is Laban, and in which the New Testament also stands at the forefront. If one reflects on all of this, one will see that these interpretations are not groundless whims. Thus, when the outer will yields to the inner, the inner also immerses itself into the outer; and this is the bowing of Abraham's servant, when he bows to the earth before Jehovah. The higher bows into the lower, and then causes the lower to also bow in deep humility before God.

->The silver and golden vessels and the garments that Rebekah receives are the treasures of wisdom, which the doctrine of the Revelation of Christ possesses and receives; and within a person, it is the treasure of divine wisdom in the inner divine life. For in Christ Jesus lie hidden all the treasures of wisdom and knowledge <note>A reference to Colossians 2:3: "In whom are hid all the treasures of wisdom and knowledge."</note>, and when Christ becomes truly manifest in us, then everything becomes manifest with Him. The gifts for her brother and her mother represent the outward Church with its doctrines and spiritual gifts. Therefore, Abraham's servant stayed overnight here with Laban, and they ate and drank among outward matters of faith only in "piecemeal" <note>original: "Stückwerk," referring to 1 Corinthians 13:9, "For we know in part [piecemeal]..." implying incomplete spiritual understanding in this life.</note>.<-

In the morning they rose up early, and he said, "Let me go to my lord." At the beginning of the Church of the Old Testament, the Spirit departed from the pagan church and set its face toward the end, toward the Kingdom of Christ. But it was nevertheless held back by Laban—representing the outer nature and outer doctrine—and this time of the great blessing of Abraham could not yet be attained. The number ten <note>The "Zehn-Zahl" or number ten often symbolizes the completeness of the law or the external structure of the church in mystical theology.</note>, as the totality of the outer church, had to first run its course. Likewise, in the Church of the New Testament, the Spirit passed over from the dispensation of the Old Testament into the Christian Church and said, "Do not hinder me, for Jehovah has made my way prosper," that is, the name of Jesus has now come with the preaching of the Gospel. But here too, the Spirit was still delayed, and they wished first to ask the maiden from her own mouth; that is, the true inner doctrine must first open its mouth and speak before the complete departure from the outer church can occur. Just as there was a moving forward of the Spirit in the outer church, and yet the Spirit was always hindered therein; even so, in the believing person, there is a constant going out from corruption, and yet one is also constantly held back. The Spirit desires, as it were, to go forth continually, but the soul, which is held back by the outer spirit of the world <term>Weltgeist: the temporal or mundane influence that distracts the soul from the divine</term>, prays and entreats forever that He might remain with her longer, just as the disciples said to Christ: "Lord, stay with us, for it is toward evening, and the day is far spent." This lasts until the delays of this time reach their goal, when the inner and eternal will gladly and willingly surrender itself into the outer and temporal, just as Rebecca says, "I will go." To this end, the adversary in the outer nature must be put away, so that the outer nature can give itself over with the eternal and inner nature into a single, persistent will; without this, it is not possible. Therefore, God will send the angel of His holy power, who must arrange all preparations and paths, so that his heavenly wife may be brought to Isaac.

The journey of Abraham's servant with Rebecca represents the journey of the Spirit with the Word of God through the entire Church, until the very end. The camels are the outward teachings in which the Spirit of God and the Church have traveled. Isaac’s coming forth from the South Country <note>original: "Mittagsland," literally 'midday land,' a traditional term for the South or the Negev</note>, from the Well of the Living and Seeing One <note>referring to Beer-lahai-roi</note>, represents the emergence of the doctrine of the Revelation of Christ out of the Old Testament. This occurs in the evening, or at the end of the outward Church. At this point, Rebecca falls from the camel; that is, the Spirit departs from the literal teaching <note>the "dead letter" of the law</note>, and with it, the Church of the faithful also falls from these camels upon which she has ridden until now. She covers herself with the true doctrine and is ashamed that she has ridden upon such animalistic things. Isaac leads this Rebecca into the tent of his mother Sarah, and is comforted again after Sarah's death; for with this Rebecca, Sarah is found again, and life comes forth once more out of death.

According to this figure of the Church, the same process occurs within man. When the natural soul, together with the outward nature, beholds the inner man in the Spirit within itself, it falls to the ground in the highest humility before God's holiness. It is ashamed that it has ridden for so long upon the animalistic senses and will. It covers this shame again with the essential robe of Christ's righteousness; and so Jesus receives the soul into His power and leads it into the tent of the inner divine life, and is comforted again over the loss of the same—all of which will only attain its true fulfillment in the future. This is the matter of Isaac's marriage in its true sense; and now follows the conclusion of the history of Abraham with his second marriage, death, and burial.

## Abraham’s Second Marriage.

When Sarah is compared against the other wife, Sarah represents the Inner, and Kethura represents the Outer. To Isaac, as the only heir, are given all the goods of Abraham in the Kingdom of Christ; however, all external religions are the concubines of Abraham, who receive only gifts in the form of external teachings. They were sent into the east country, that is, into the external beginning, for the external part of the Church is the "morning" <note>The author plays on the German word 'Morgenland' (East/Orient), literally 'Morning-land'.</note>. In total, there are 16 children across 4 generations, which represents the entire Church in all its numbers, according to the 4 main religions, 3 households, 2 testaments, and 6 ages. Kethura takes her name from "incense" <note>original: "Räuchern"; the Hebrew name Keturah is related to 'qetoret' meaning incense</note>, which refers to external matters of faith. She has 6 children, who according to their number point to the 6 ages of the Church. Jokshan has 2 sons, Sheba...

--- Page 244 ---
<meta>This page continues the allegorical interpretation of Abraham’s lineage, specifically focusing on the symbolic meaning of his second wife, Keturah, and her descendants in relation to the history of the church and human nature.</meta>

-> ✠ ) 0 ( ✠ <-

Sheba and Dedan <note>original: "Seban und Dedan"</note>, and so the Church stands in two Testaments. Dedan has three peoples, just as the Church stands in three dispensations <term>Haushaltungen: periods of divine administration or "households" of time</term>. Midian has five children, and all of these together make four times four, or sixteen children, who are all called the children of Keturah. If Sarah is placed at the beginning of the world with the blessing, then Keturah represents the period of corruption up to the present. The children of Keturah indicate in nature how all children of Adam descend from Abraham—that is, from Jesus Christ—even according to their outward birth; and how, according to our outward nature, we are all children of Keturah, who in this present time have not been able to attain the true inheritance of Isaac. 

People possessed their outward natural gifts, and even the believers had their gifts of the Spirit only in fragments <note>original: "Stückwerk"; a reference to 1 Corinthians 13:9</note> and by grace. One did not possess the blessed goods of Abraham by natural right, because nature was corrupted and gave only corrupted gifts. In the future, however, we shall possess them with Isaac by natural right, because nature will stand in the blessing and will itself produce nothing but good. Hitherto we were outside the house of Abraham, outside of Paradise with the Tree of Life, but in the future we shall be within it again. 

The fact that Abraham casts out his children by Keturah from his true inheritance indicates how believers should cast out and crucify their evil lusts, inclinations, and desires. Furthermore, it indicates how man, in times past, was ordered into the outward law of nature, in which man was meant to live in good and sound reason; yet he was not to distance his own nature too far from Christ—namely, from Christ within us—but was to be a member of Christ's household. 

We see the figure of this in the "Great World" <term>Macrocosm</term> regarding the Church, where the Law had to remain with the Jews alongside Christ during the course of the Christian Church. And when all goods were promised to the "outward Christ" in the letter of the New Testament—as to the outward Isaac—and God sent the Turkish Church <note>The author likely refers to Islam or the Ottoman Empire as a "church" within a providential framework</note> out of this outward house of Abraham and into the Orient <note>original: "Morgenland"</note> into the teachings of the Old Testament; they were nevertheless not to distance themselves too far from Jesus Christ, but had to retain the New Testament and remain included in the dispensation of the New Testament. God sent them into the life of reason, into a more rational and more natural religion, in which there was less superstition than in the Christian Church.

The Jews, as the children of the covenant, were cast out by Christ from the letter of the New Testament along with the Old Testament, to indicate that even the true children of the spiritual covenant were still cast out from the rightful inheritance of Abraham in their external and earthly humanity during the previous age. All of these were merely external figures <note>The author uses "figures" to mean symbolic types or shadows of a deeper spiritual reality</note>, showing how everything external was to be cast out through Jesus Christ in his revelation. The external Christ was only the figure in a literal image, and not the essence itself. Only now does Christ truly come into the flesh, as God puts away the figure; He also takes back the goods of the land of Canaan from the external Isaac of the Christian Church, just as He did to the Jews with the land of Canaan, and as He also allowed such land to be taken away from the Christians in the Orient by the Turks. All these images must now cease and come to an end when the Essence <note>original: "Wesen" — referring to the spiritual reality or the actual presence of God</note> arrives.

## Abraham's Death and Burial.

7. **Narrator.** And these are the days of the years of Abraham's life which he lived—

The years of Abraham's life are divided into three parts, just as was done with Sarah. These three parts represent the three dispensations <note>German: Haushaltungen; refers to the historical stages of God's governance of the church</note> of the church, in all three of which Abraham was present, both inwardly and outwardly. His death is one with the death of Sarah. In the beginning, the inner being entered into death, and now at the end, the outward Abraham dies along with all the figures of the Word of God—namely, as outward figures—and these figures are completely buried and covered over. It says that Isaac and Ishmael buried him, where Isaac stands first and Ishmael follows. This is the outward Isaac, namely the outward Christian Church, and Ishmael is the Turkish <note>In this 18th-century context, "Turkish" refers broadly to the Islamic world</note> Church. Both have, from the beginning, buried Abraham as the true inner Jesus Christ and inner doctrine; now at the end, however, these two will also bury the outward Christ, for the Turks must help tear down the Tower of Babel within the Christian Church. These two churches, the Christians and the Turks, constitute the dispensation of the New Testament church, and they contain the double cave within <note>A reference to the Cave of Machpelah where Abraham was buried</note>, in which all true wisdom has been buried until now, but from which it will now also rise again. They are the two twins, Jacob and Esau, who lay together in the single body of the New Testament dispensation and pressed and pushed against one another, just as the inner and outer man lie together in a single human being. The outward letter <note>The literal or surface-level meaning of the scripture</note> of Abraham must therefore die first, otherwise the true blessing does not come. Therefore it is said: after the death of Abraham, God blessed Isaac his son, and Isaac dwelt by the Well of the Living One who sees me <note>original: "Brunnen des Lebendigen und Sehenden"; Hebrew: Beer-lahai-roi</note>. There, the whole of nature on earth will be a pure well of life, in which one not only lives in the divine life, but also rightly perceives and sees God and all things.

16. A. These are the children of Ishmael, and these are their names: in their villages and their castles—
n. 12 princes according to their nations.
17. N. And these are the years of the life of Ishmael: 100 years and 30 years and 7 years—
a. and he gave up the ghost and died: and was gathered unto his people.
18. A. And they dwelt from Havilah unto Shur, which is before Egypt: as one goes toward Assyria—
n. He settled <note>original: "fiel," literally "fell," here meaning to settle or dwell</note> in the presence of all his brothers.

The history of Abraham is closed and concluded with the generation of Ishmael, the ground and cause of which must be considered. When both are held against one another, Abraham signifies the Inner, and Ishmael the Outer; therefore his years of life are likewise divided into three parts, just as the years of the lives of Abraham and Sarah, whereby all three households of the Church are seen. All of this is externally Ishmael with the Kingdom of Nature, and internally Abraham with the Kingdom of Christ. Abraham was among all nations, and among them all he externally begat this son Ishmael; therefore he is the son of the Egyptian handmaid, for everything external is "Egypt." Yet he is nonetheless a son of Abraham, a son of Jesus Christ, and thus all nations are included, taken up, and accepted into the covenant of Christ, and no nation is rejected because of its external faith. Jesus Christ is called the Beginning and the End, the First and the Last, and that is also Abraham. In the beginning of the world, the whole of nature on earth stood in blessing, and that was Abraham. When this time of blessing perished, then this Abraham died, and the subsequent time of corruption followed, which is Ishmael. Therefore, the generation of Ishmael follows here after the death of Abraham. Of this

<summary>This page lists the descendants and death of Ishmael, followed by a spiritual commentary identifying Abraham with the internal Kingdom of Christ and Ishmael with the external Kingdom of Nature.</summary>
<keywords>Ishmael, Abraham, genealogy, Kingdom of Nature, Kingdom of Christ, spiritual interpretation, covenant, Egypt</keywords>

--- Page 248 ---
<meta>Continuity established with the previous page's discussion of spiritual lineages; the catchword "bey" (at/with) connects to the final sentence of this page.</meta>

The external figure of the Christian and Turkish <note>The author uses "Turkish Church" to refer to Islam, common in 17th-18th century German mystical writing</note> churches stands before the eyes of the whole world. For the external Christ was the external Abraham, who was followed by Ishmael as the Turkish Church. This is the last of the four main religions of the world and stands under the Old Testament.

The Old Testament, however, also stands as an image of everything external; thus, the entire external church is "Ishmael," although the Turkish Church is primarily understood by that term. The number 12 is the image of the entire Church; therefore, the Old Testament has 12 tribes of Israel, and the New Testament has 12 apostles. These are also the 12 princes of Ishmael—namely, when viewed according to the external doctrine of the letter <note>The "letter" refers to literal, surface-level scriptural interpretation as opposed to spiritual meaning</note>. Every Word of God among all peoples is "Abraham" according to the internal sense of the spirit, and it is "Ishmael" according to the external doctrine of the letter. Now, Jesus Christ himself wrote every Word of God; therefore, it is all his own. Whoever has confessed even a single Word of God, whichever it may be, has already accepted the covenant of Christ through it, even if he did not yet know Christ. By this, all nations are gathered together into one under Jesus Christ. This occurs through his current revelation, wherein it is made manifest to all nations that he is the first forefather of all men, Adam; that this first Adam is the personal Jesus Christ or God himself; that there is no higher God than this one; and that he is the God whom every nation has honored and worshipped under its own Word of God. Behold, this God, this Jesus Christ, is Abraham, in whom all nations shall be blessed, and all the children of men are his son Ishmael, whom he begot naturally. With this figure of Ishmael, the external tree of Adam is transplanted into the internal tree of Jesus Christ; then Ishmael stands in the lineage of Christ. For this reason, the years of Ishmael's life are noted and counted, which is a feature unique only to the lineage of Christ. Among all other fathers who represent the kingdom of nature—such as Cain, Ham, Japheth, Esau, Moab, Ammon, and the like—one will find no recorded years of life, except here with Ishmael, the son of Abraham. By this, it is indicated that the external kingdom of nature and the external church are included in God's covenant of grace and shall be translated into the internal covenant at the end, just as the Ishmael of the Turkish Church has been included in the household of the New Testament Church and [becomes] an assistant

<summary>This page presents a universalist mystical theology where Islam (the "Turkish Church") and all world religions are unified under Christ, identifying the historical Adam as the personal Jesus Christ. The author argues that Ishmael represents the external form of faith which will eventually be integrated into the internal spiritual covenant.</summary>

--- Page 249 ---
<meta>Continuity established with the previous page's discussion on the spiritual lineage of the patriarchs; the catchword "the" connects to the preceding section regarding the state of the church.</meta>

at the Church under the name of Jesus. Ishmael, representing the external church and doctrine, has until now dwelt in every religion from Havilah unto Shur <note>Genesis 25:18; Havilah represents the beginning of growth, while Shur represents the "wall" or enmity</note>—that is, from the act of birthing, procreating, and multiplying, unto the state of enmity, where one religion was hostile toward another. And he dwelt in the presence of all his brethren; that is, one religion dwelt in the sight of the other. But now, the whole Ishmael shall also fall before all his brethren—meaning that all external religions and doctrines shall fall away. Until now, one has indeed fallen before another, but that was only the external figure of what is to happen universally at the end. Ishmael must fall and Isaac must arise, or rather, Ishmael is transformed into Isaac. He must only strip off the external garment of corruption and put on the inward robe of the righteousness of Jesus Christ; then he is the Isaac within the great blessing of Abraham. The holy, eternal God Himself must work, accomplish, and bring all this into effect, for with our own power nothing is achieved. Abraham must appear out of the Beginning in the End, and place the End back into the Beginning, so that the corrupted middle-ground may be taken away. This He intends to do, which I testify with trust and certain confidence; and it shall be testified for the service and benefit of all those who love the appearing of our Lord and Savior Jesus Christ.

This, my reader, is thus the history of Abraham from its beginning to its end. What I have written and noted of it will be enough to recognize the true nature of the Patriarchs of the Scripture: that these are not histories of individual persons, but rather images of the creation and formation of the world, of nations, of the church, and of all things. They represent the nature, kind, and character of everything, and of God’s government over it all. They are images of the external kingdom and rule of Nature, and of the internal kingdom and rule of Christ—of the external and internal man. With this explanation, I have only unlocked the doors and removed the bolts so that one may look into this House of the Wisdom of God; I have not, however, noted everything that is within this house, as that would not be possible in such brevity. What is still lacking will follow in its own time, and the Spirit of God will also

<summary>The author concludes the history of Abraham by explaining that Ishmael represents external, conflicting religions that must eventually give way to the internal righteousness of Isaac. He asserts that the biblical patriarchs are not merely historical figures but archetypes of the natural and spiritual worlds.</summary>

--- Page 250 ---
<meta>This page concludes the introductory explanation of Abraham's spiritual significance, transitioning into the upcoming section on the twelve tribes of Israel.</meta>

->※ ) 0 ( ※<-

He will guide His own people so that they will be able to see further through this opening of the doors. Let this serve only as instruction regarding who Abraham is, and what his great blessing is, in which all nations shall now be blessed. For because there is no nation on earth that does not have the history of Abraham in its own Word of God, and yet Abraham is Christ <note>The author interprets Abraham not as a historical person, but as a spiritual archetype or manifestation of Christ</note>, in whom all nations shall be gathered into one under one head; therefore, my duty and my office required me to provide this explanation. This is so that every nation may be convinced and obligated by its own Word of God to bow under Jesus Christ and to worship Him as the only true God, setting aside all external literal doctrines <note>original: "Buchstabenlehren" — refers to a rigid, surface-level understanding of scripture</note> with which all nations have been ensnared. For this purpose, this explanation is sufficient, and everyone shall be bound to respect and accept this revelation, which comes from God, or else face the loss of Abraham's inheritance and all his goods. However, so that one may see with certainty that God's Word is not to be understood according to the letter, the blessing of Jacob over the twelve tribes, together with their explanation—wherein lies the master key to understanding the images of God's Word—shall follow next as a second part to the section on Abraham. From these twelve patriarchs, one will see that they were not such external persons <note>The author argues that biblical figures are internal spiritual states rather than historical individuals</note>. But if this is the case, then Moses, the prophets, Christ and his apostles, and all the fathers of the Scripture were also not such external persons in such times, because they are derived from the twelve tribes; and one will find oneself forced by necessity to seek another sense of the Word of God, if one wishes to hold it as divine at all. Only then will one read this explanation of the history of Abraham with better conviction. May the holy God remember the seed of Abraham and lead him, like Lot, out of the coming destruction of Sodom for the sake of His promise. May this book serve that end, and may His high and mighty arm let it come into the hands of all those whom He wishes to serve by it.

Written, the 5th of September,
1769.

### Elias. <note>The author uses the pseudonym "Elias" (Elijah), signaling a prophetic persona intended to restore spiritual truths before a coming judgment.</note>
